## 2026-09-18

Used `prisma init --no-skills` to skip the CLI's agent-skill scaffolding this time.

- What's next: Design the matches/games/decisions schema and run the first migration. Confirm the `analyses/list/{page}` request URL against a real token/response — current implementation is an educated guess based on the `game_reviews` endpoint's pattern.
- What changed: Defined the `Match`/`Game`/`Decision` Prisma schema and applied the first migration (`init_schema`) against local Postgres — confirmed live this time, Docker Desktop turned out to already be installed and running, just not on `PATH`. Final schema: `Match.playedAt` (nullable, filled at detail ingest) plus `createdAt` (ingest timestamp) kept distinct; `mwc` promoted to its own nullable column on `Decision`; `Decision.raw` holds the complete original event object (not just `reviews[0]`) as the zero-blind-spot archive; indexes on `(kind, classification)` and `userId`. Verified table structure directly via `psql \d`.

- What changed: Added `prisma/seed.ts` — fake local-dev data (2 matches, one fully "detail-ingested" with 3 games × 10 decisions each, one index-only with zero games), obviously-placeholder content (`TEST Opponent Alpha/Beta`, fake country codes, tagged `raw` JSON) so it can't be mistaken for real ingest. Wired via `prisma7.config.ts`'s `migrations.seed` (not `package.json`'s `prisma.seed` — that's the pre-v7 convention and doesn't work under Prisma 7). Added `tsx` as a dev dependency to run it, since the generated client is TS-source-only. Idempotent (`deleteMany` in FK-safe order before inserting) — verified by running `npx prisma db seed` twice with identical row counts. Also fixed a real bug this surfaced: `prisma/seed.ts`'s BigInt literal broke `next build`'s type-check because the file was being swept into the app's tsconfig; excluded `prisma/seed.ts` in `tsconfig.json` since it's a standalone script, not app code.
- What's next: Build the index-sync job and the detail-ingest job (unchanged from above).

New routes `GET /api/local/matches/list/[page]` and `GET /api/local/matches/[matchId]/[gameIndex]` (404 when not ingested); new pages `/db/matches` and `/db/matches/[matchId]` (no token/modal — reads the local DB) reusing the existing `MistakesSection`/`BoardPanel`/`Board`/`Dice` components in place via import, not moved. Landing page link was already pointed at `/matches`, which now correctly means the DB-backed default — no change needed there. Verified: `/matches` renders with no token modal, `/galaxy/matches` still shows it; both API namespaces respond correctly (tested the Galaxy side with a bad token — real 401s come back cleanly, confirming the endpoint URLs are genuinely correct); re-ran the `extractDecisions`/`computePR` pipeline check against the renamed `/api/matches/*` routes — identical result (30 decisions, same PR) to before the rename.

- What changed: Added single-match ingest and the per-row "Sync" button on `/galaxy/matches`. `lib/ingest.ts` (`ingestMatch(matchId, indexData, token)`) upserts `Match` from the already-loaded list row, walks games via `galaxy-client.getGameReviews` (starting at gameIndex 1, stopping on the same null/404/empty-events condition as everywhere else), and per event upserts a `Decision` — `cubeOwnerUserId` computed by walking each game's cube-kind decisions in order and tracking the last successful take (verified: owner is `null` through a double and the take itself, only shows the new owner on the *next* cube decision after — matches real cube mechanics). Excludes `game_started`/`game_over`/`turn_forfeited` explicitly. Added a `@@unique([gameId, eventId])` migration on `Decision` — needed for `Decision.upsert` to be idempotent at all, since the initial schema had no natural key for it (created the migration file by hand and applied via `prisma migrate deploy`, since `migrate dev` needs an interactive TTY this environment doesn't have). Fixed: the client now treats `gamesIngested === 0 && errors.length > 0` as a failure.

Also hit the sync route directly with a bad token and confirmed it fails gracefully (200 with a populated `errors` array, not a crash).

## 2026-09-19
- What changed: Diagnosed and fixed the "sync says it worked but 0 decisions land" report from a real sync of match 46458748. Root cause was **not** a data-shape bug — fetched the real `game_reviews/46458748/1` payload directly (using a token pasted for debugging) and ran `ingestMatch` against it standalone: 99/99 decisions ingested cleanly. The actual bug was `lib/ingest.ts` recording `decisionTimestamps`/`matchLength` *before* the `Decision.upsert` call instead of after, so a failed upsert still left `Game.playedAt`/`Match.matchLength` looking populated while zero decisions actually saved — masking the real failure. Separately, the dev server itself had been running continuously since early in the day through every route move/schema change without a restart, and had accumulated real staleness (stale Prisma Client in memory causing `Unknown argument source_sourceMatchId`, references to `app/game_reviews/page.tsx` which was deleted hours earlier) — restarted it with `.next` cleared partway through this session and that resolved a second, unrelated batch of symptoms.
- What changed: Three schema/logic changes in one migration (`source_and_identity`), applied via `prisma migrate reset --force` (explicit fresh consent obtained live, per Prisma's own AI-agent safety guard — the task's earlier authorization didn't count). (1) Classification now reads `source_position.classification` only, throws (caught, recorded per-decision) if missing — was already source-only in practice, no destination fallback existed, but hardened it and documented why in a comment. (2) `Match.id` is now an internal auto-increment key; added `Match.source`/`sourceMatchId` with `@@unique([source, sourceMatchId])` — every place that treated the Galaxy match ID as `Match.id` (`ingestMatch`, `local-client.ts`'s `listMatches`/`getGameReviews`) now resolves via the compound key instead; route URLs unchanged (still the Galaxy match ID). (3) Added `PlayerIdentity` (`source`, `sourceUserId`, `displayName`, `isMe`), auto-upserted in `app/api/galaxy/matches/list/[page]/route.ts` from `analyses/list`'s top-level `userId`/`userName` (best-effort, doesn't block returning the list on failure). New `GET /api/player-identities` route (no auth — DB-only data) lets `MistakesSection` resolve "which side is you" automatically instead of the manual radio-button picker, which is now removed. Updated `prisma/seed.ts` for the new shape (Match rows get `source`/`sourceMatchId` instead of a hand-picked `id`; added a seed `PlayerIdentity` row) and wrote `docs/field-mapping.md` (full field-by-field origin for all four tables, with the "keep in sync with lib/ingest.ts" reminder at the top, per the task).
  Verified end-to-end against the real match: re-synced 46458748 after the fix — 99/99 decisions, 0 errors; classifications are real values (`opening_game`, `middle_game`, ...) off `source_position`; then called the real `/api/galaxy/matches/list/1` route with the same token, which populated `PlayerIdentity` with the real account (`meisam03`) — `MistakesSection`'s resolution logic correctly matches that against the match's decisions and picks it as "you", with **no hardcoded user ID anywhere** in the code. Cross-checked independently: PR computed from the ingested decisions for "you" was 6.376, essentially identical to Galaxy's own reported `userError: 6.38` for this match.
- What's next: "Sync all new" (loop the row handler), the index-sync job, surfacing which list rows are already in the DB, and populating `Decision.myTag` (currently always null — no source field for it yet).

## 2026-09-20
- What changed: Switched the local dev DB engine from Postgres to MySQL 8. Reason: targeting Oracle HeatWave's 50 GB Always Free tier as the eventual host, versus tighter storage/usage limits on Postgres-compatible free tiers. Pure engine swap — no model fields/relations/enums changed, `lib/ingest.ts`'s extraction logic untouched. `docker-compose.yml`'s `postgres` service replaced with `mysql:8` (port 3306, named volume); `.env`/`.env.example` now carry `MYSQL_USER`/`MYSQL_PASSWORD`/`MYSQL_DATABASE` and a `mysql://` `DATABASE_URL`. Swapped `pg`/`@prisma/adapter-pg` for `mariadb`/`@prisma/adapter-mariadb` (confirmed via npm rather than guessed — Prisma unifies MySQL/MariaDB under one driver adapter) in `lib/prisma.ts`, `prisma/seed.ts`, and `app/api/db-check/route.ts`. `schema.prisma`'s datasource is now `mysql`; added explicit `@db.VarChar(191)` + hand-authored `COLLATE utf8mb4_bin` on `Match.sourceMatchId` and `PlayerIdentity.sourceUserId` (MySQL's default `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` is case-insensitive, which would have silently broken `(source, sourceMatchId)`/`(source, sourceUserId)` uniqueness) — Prisma's schema DSL has no native collation attribute (verified directly against the schema validator after a web search turned up an unreliable/likely-hallucinated example), so this was a manual edit to the generated migration SQL, same as the collation-free migrations before it. Deleted all three Postgres migration files and `migration_lock.toml`, generated one fresh `init_mysql` migration against the new container (via `migrate diff --from-empty` + `migrate deploy`, since the app's MySQL user lacks shadow-DB creation privileges needed by `migrate dev`).
  Verified end-to-end against MySQL: confirmed `kind`/`errorSeverity` generate native MySQL `ENUM(...)` with exact values preserved and `raw` generates native `JSON`, not silently altered; seed ran clean; `/api/matches/list/1` renders the seeded matches; re-synced match 46458748 twice (one attempt hit a transient upstream 500 from Galaxy, unrelated to the DB — retried and it passed) — 99/99 decisions both times with no duplicate rows, confirming `(gameId, eventId)` upsert idempotency on MySQL. Ran the full `extractDecisions`/`computePR`/`PlayerIdentity`-resolution pipeline against the real match and got results identical to the pre-switch Postgres baseline (52 decisions, 25 mine, PR 6.375999999999998, exact match). Directly proved the collation fix works (not just present in metadata): inserted two `Match` rows differing only in `sourceMatchId` case (`"CaseTest"` vs `"casetest"`) — both succeeded, meaning they're correctly treated as distinct under `utf8mb4_bin` (they'd have collided as a duplicate key under the default collation); cleaned up the test rows after. README's Postgres section replaced in place with the MySQL equivalent; swept the repo for stale "Postgres" references and fixed the one remaining (a comment in `lib/local-client.ts`).
- What's next: unchanged from above ("sync all new", index-sync job, DB-presence indicator in the list UI, `Decision.myTag`).
- What changed: Extended the collation fix to the two externally-sourced identifier columns the previous migration missed — `Decision.userId` and `Decision.cubeOwnerUserId` now also pinned to `COLLATE utf8mb4_bin` (migration `collation_fix_decision_userids`), same reasoning as `Match.sourceMatchId`/`PlayerIdentity.sourceUserId`: MySQL's default collation is case-insensitive, which is a risk for any externally-sourced identifier comparison, not just ones inside a unique constraint. `Decision.eventId` deliberately left alone — it's `BigInt`, and collation is a string-comparison concept with nothing to pin on a numeric column. Documented the rule properly this time instead of leaving it implicit: added a "Collation for externally-sourced identifiers" section to `docs/field-mapping.md` (the doc had no collation mention at all before this — a gap left over from the MySQL-switch task) listing all four pinned columns plus the eventId non-exception explained, and added a standing-convention line to `CLAUDE.md` so this gets applied proactively to future columns instead of needing another cleanup pass.
  Verified: no behavior change expected (defensive fix, not a repair) and none found — re-ran the full pipeline against the already-synced match 46458748 post-migration and got results identical to before (52 decisions, 25 mine, PR 6.375999999999998). Also directly proved the fix is real, not just present in the schema: queried `Decision` for the real `userId` in both its actual case and an artificially uppercased variant — exact case found 50 rows, uppercased found 0, confirming genuine case-sensitive comparison (would have still matched under the old case-insensitive default).
- What changed: Documentation follow-up (no DB/schema changes) — added the `MANUAL EDIT` warning comment directly above each of the four hand-added `COLLATE utf8mb4_bin` lines in the two migration files, and corrected `docs/field-mapping.md` to accurately describe the mechanism (hand-edited migration SQL, not a Prisma schema attribute — confirmed `@db.Collation(...)` is real Prisma syntax but SQL-Server-only, fails validation on the mysql connector) plus the "won't be auto-preserved" risk. `CLAUDE.md`'s collation convention got one more line pointing at this.
- What changed: Added `/status` — a live stack/DB snapshot page, at your request for "something like a smaller version of PROGRESS.md." Deliberately designed so almost nothing on it needs manual upkeep: framework versions (Next/React/TypeScript/Prisma) read live from `node_modules/*/package.json`, DB engine parsed live from `DATABASE_URL`, connection health/row counts/latest-migration-name all live-queried via Prisma — only a short `app/status/notes.ts` (4 bullets: MySQL-not-Postgres reasoning, no-app-auth-yet, the two parallel data sources, the collation rule) is manually maintained, and `CLAUDE.md` now has a narrow instruction to update just that file after stack/DB/auth/architecture-level changes — not a duplicate of the PROGRESS.md history log. Linked from the landing page. Verified the rendered page's live values against a direct DB query (3 matches/4 games/129 decisions/2 identities, all matched) and against `package.json`/`node_modules` (16.3.5/19.2.8/7.10.0/v24.18.0, all matched). One implementation snag: `@prisma/adapter-mariadb/package.json` isn't importable as a subpath (unlike next/react/typescript/prisma/@prisma/client, which all worked) — dropped that one row rather than fight it, since `@prisma/client`'s version already reflects the Prisma tooling version in play.
- What changed: Built the resumable multi-match sync system. Schema: `IngestStatus` enum (`PENDING`/`INGESTING`/`DONE`/`FAILED`) + `Match.ingestStatus`/`ingestError`, and a `SyncRun` log table (migration `sync_system`, generated via `prisma migrate diff --from-config-datasource` — diffs the live DB directly, sidesteps the shadow-DB permission issue that's blocked `migrate dev`/`migrate diff --from-migrations` all session). `lib/sync.ts`'s `runSync({ token, type, maxMatchesToProcess? })`: creates a `SyncRun` row, walks every `analyses/list` page upserting index fields for every match seen (never touching `ingestStatus`), then sequentially detail-ingests every `Match` with `ingestStatus` in `PENDING`/`FAILED`/`INGESTING` (the last one specifically so a mid-match crash is resumable — status flips to `INGESTING` *before* any Galaxy call, so a crash there reads as distinguishable from never-attempted on the next run) via the existing `ingestMatch`, one at a time — a match's own failure is caught, logged to `ingestError`/the `SyncRun.errors` array, and the loop continues to the next match rather than aborting. `scripts/backfill.ts`/`scripts/incremental-sync.ts` both call it uncapped (share a small `scripts/runSyncCli.ts` helper — same logic, only the `type` tag differs) with progress logged on a 15s timer by polling `Match` counts directly, rather than adding a progress-callback param to `runSync`'s signature since the task gave an exact signature and a timer achieves the same visibility without touching it. `POST /api/sync/incremental` calls it capped at 20 matches (noted as tunable for Vercel Hobby's execution window) and returns a plain-language `message` telling you to run the local script when matches are still pending. `scripts/*.ts` and `prisma/seed.ts` are both excluded from the app's `tsconfig.json` (standalone scripts, not app code — same reasoning applied to both).
  Verified for real against the live Galaxy account (not synthetic fixtures): ran `scripts/backfill.ts` — all 3 matches (2 seed placeholders + the real 46458748) went `PENDING` → `DONE`, seed data untouched (fake match IDs 404 cleanly against Galaxy with 0 errors, so nothing overwrites the seed's own Game/Decision rows), real match re-ingested with the same 99 decisions as before. Re-ran it immediately after: `matchesProcessed: 0` — confirmed already-`DONE` matches are skipped with zero Galaxy calls, which is what makes reruns cheap. Manually forced one match to `INGESTING` (simulating a mid-run crash) and one to `FAILED`, then called the live `/api/sync/incremental` route — both correctly picked up, re-ingested, and cleared back to `DONE`/`ingestError: null`. Directly tested the `maxMatchesToProcess` cap with 2 pending matches and a cap of 1: processed exactly 1, `stillPending: 1` — cap and reporting both correct.
- What's next: Run the actual first full backfill (the real one, beyond this session's test runs) once there's a fuller match history to pull. Beyond that: a UI trigger for incremental sync (currently curl/script-only), a cron/scheduler for the Vercel route (explicitly out of scope until now, per the original "manual only" instruction), and the older items (DB-presence indicator in the list UI, `Decision.myTag`).

## 2026-09-21
- What changed: Ran the actual first real backfill (4259 real matches discovered) and hit a genuine, previously-unseen data shape: 100% failure rate, all `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'black')` — `metadata.scores` is `null` for a category of matches the original testing (one point-match) never exposed, almost certainly money games (inferred from `scores: null` + `match_length: null` together, plus an unusual `analysis_level: 998` — not a field Galaxy explicitly labels, so deliberately *not* encoded as a `Match.isMoneyGame` flag; nullability alone is the honest signal for now). Fix: `Decision.matchScoreBlack`/`matchScoreWhite` → `Int?` (migration `nullable_match_scores`, via the same `migrate diff --from-config-datasource` approach), `lib/gameReviewsTypes.ts`'s `Metadata.scores` → nullable, `lib/ingest.ts` reads `metadata.scores?.black ?? null`. Investigated `crawfordState` per the same hypothesis and confirmed it does **not** need nullability — always a real value (`"none"`) even for money games, recorded in `docs/field-mapping.md` so it isn't re-investigated later.
  A second, unanticipated issue surfaced during the same investigation: an event type `double_rejected` (not in the `EventType` union at all) whose `error_analysis` is entirely `null` — an outcome-logging event (receiver declined a double, game over), not a real decision; the doubler's own decision is already fully captured by the preceding `double_requested` event. Rather than making 5 more columns nullable (`rawError`/`errorSeverity`/`isBlunder`/`luck`/`luckMwc`) or keying off the specific `event_type` string, `lib/ingest.ts` now skips any event with `error_analysis === null` structurally — checked against a small confirmed-safe list (currently `game_started`/`game_over`/`turn_forfeited`/`double_rejected`); anything outside that list is logged loudly (`console.warn` + new `IngestSummary.warnings`, which does not count against the match's success like `errors` does) instead of silently trusted. Before finalizing, checked the analogous `double_accepted` against a real point-match — confirmed it has a fully-populated `error_analysis` (including a real detected blunder) and correctly stays off the skip list.
  Verified directly against the real failing match (`724878`): re-ran `ingestMatch` standalone — 19/20 decisions ingested cleanly (`double_rejected` silently skipped, as expected), 0 errors, 0 warnings, `matchScoreBlack`/`White` correctly null in the DB. The backfill script itself was stopped for this investigation (was mid-run, 4191 pending / 67 failed, 0 done) — not resumed yet.
- What's next: Resume/restart the real backfill now that the money-game and `double_rejected` fixes are live — expect the previously-failing matches to succeed on retry automatically (no manual intervention needed, `FAILED` matches are already picked up on the next run). Watch for any *new* unconfirmed event types logged via `IngestSummary.warnings` during that run. Beyond that: unchanged from before (UI trigger for incremental sync, Vercel cron, DB-presence indicator in the list UI, `Decision.myTag`).
- What changed: Correction, caught by the warning system working exactly as designed. The real backfill (resumed after the fixes above) flooded the log with `unconfirmed event_type "double_accepted"` warnings — the earlier "confirmed NOT null" finding for `double_accepted` was wrong, based on checking only one example (the point-match `46458748`, where it happened to carry a real `cube_pass` analysis). Checked a real instance from this run in context (match `726018`): `double_accepted` can *also* show up as `analysed_event: "cube_double"` with `error_analysis: null`, sitting right after its own `double_requested` event exactly like `double_rejected` — a pure outcome record, not a second decision. The structural `error_analysis === null` check already handled this correctly either way (real analysis → ingested normally, null → skipped) — only the confirmed-safe list needed `double_accepted` added so it stops logging loudly for something now genuinely confirmed. Corrected both `lib/ingest.ts`'s comment (was "confirmed always null", now correctly "confirmed safe to skip when null — not uniform") and `docs/field-mapping.md`'s `double_accepted` writeup to be explicit that a prior version of that doc got this wrong.
- What's next: unchanged from above — resume the backfill with this correction live; the flood of `double_accepted` warnings should stop, and any further unconfirmed event types will still surface individually rather than being swallowed by the fix.
- What changed: Added real-time failure logging — `lib/sync.ts`'s per-match catch block now `console.error`s the match ID + full error message immediately when a match fails, instead of only surfacing failures in the final summary at the very end of a run (which, at ~4259 matches, could be hours away). Applies to both the local scripts and the Vercel route's server logs.
  While adding this, found (via the newly-visible errors) a third real data-shape bug, distinct from the money-game and double_rejected/double_accepted ones already fixed: a completely new `analysed_event` value, `"resignation"` (own shape — `resign_error`, `should_resign`, `resignation_type`, `equity_before`, `equity_after` — no `moves`, no `cube_analysis` at all). Currently miscategorized as `DecisionKind.CUBE` (the `analysedEvent === "move" ? CHECKER : CUBE` fallback) and crashes in `buildCubeDetail` trying to read `cube_analysis.receivers_best_action` off an object that doesn't exist for this type. This is a real, gradable decision (Galaxy computes `resign_error`), not a skip-as-non-decision case like the cube ones — needs a real design decision (proper `DecisionKind`/schema support vs. deliberately skip for now) before fixing, so flagged rather than fixed in this pass.
- What's next: Decide and implement resignation-event handling (blocking most current backfill failures). Otherwise unchanged from above.
- What changed: Fixed the `analysed_event: "resignation"` crash found during the real backfill. Root cause: `buildCubeDetail` assumed any non-`"move"` event was cube-shaped and unconditionally read `cube_analysis`, but resignation events (`event_type: "resigned"`) have no `cube_analysis`/`moves` at all — a fourth, distinct result shape (`resign_error`/`should_resign`/`resignation_type`/`equity_before`/`equity_after`). Schema: added `RESIGNATION` to `DecisionKind` and five nullable `Decision` columns (migration `resignation_decisions`, via the established `migrate diff --from-config-datasource` → hand-authored SQL → `migrate deploy` pattern). `lib/gameReviewsTypes.ts`: added `ResignationResult`/`ResignationAnalysisEnvelope` to the `AnalysisEnvelope` union; added `"double_rejected"`/`"resigned"` to `EventType` (a pre-existing gap, not previously listed). `lib/ingest.ts`: replaced the binary `analysed_event === "move" ? CHECKER : CUBE` fallback with `decisionKindFor`, an exact map over the four known shapes that returns `null` (logged as a warning, event skipped) for anything else — this guard is permanent, not resignation-specific, so a future fifth shape surfaces the same way instead of being silently miscounted. `buildCubeDetail` now only reads `cube_analysis` when `analysed_event` is exactly `"cube_double"`/`"cube_pass"`. `lib/mistakes.ts` got the same `decisionKindFor` mapping (widened `DecisionKind` to include `"resignation"`) and an `actionLabels` branch for resignation — `MistakesSection`'s existing `kind === "checker"`/`kind === "cube"` filters already exclude `"resignation"` from both PR buckets with no further change needed, satisfying "never blend resignation into checker/cube PR" for free. Documented all of this in `docs/field-mapping.md` (resignation field table rows, a "The `resignation` decision shape" section explaining the PR exclusion, and a permanent "Unrecognized `analysed_event`" section).
  Verified against the real payload (match `2856675`, event `440889365`, fetched live via `ingestMatch` through a throwaway script, then cleaned up): ingested with 0 errors/warnings, `kind: RESIGNATION`, `resignError: -6.4776`, `shouldResign: false`, `notationPlayed`/`notationBest`/`cubeDetail` all correctly `null`. Caught a real surprise in the same check: `resignation_type`/`equity_before`/`equity_after` were present but `null` in the real payload (not just absent) — the initial `ResignationResult` type had declared them non-nullable based on the earlier key-listing investigation; corrected to nullable in `lib/gameReviewsTypes.ts` and noted in the doc, rather than leaving an inaccurate type in place. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` both clean; dev server restarted with `.next` cleared per the standing convention for schema/Prisma-client changes.
- What's next: Resume the real backfill (unblocked now that the resignation crash is fixed) — the still-open items from prior entries ("sync all new" button, index-sync job, DB-presence indicator, `Decision.myTag`) remain unchanged.
- What changed: Added per-match/per-run visibility to the sync system — pure logging/counters, no ingest/upsert/skip logic touched. `IngestSummary` gained `decisionsSkippedNotCounted` (events with a real review but `metadata.count_as_decision === false` — still upserted exactly as before, this is an informational count, not an exclusive bucket) and `eventsSkippedNoReview` (events with no review at all: `game_started`/`game_over`/`turn_forfeited` plus any other event type with an empty `reviews` array). `lib/sync.ts`'s detail-ingest loop now logs one line per match after `ingestMatch` returns: `match {id}: {games} games, {decisions} decisions upserted, {notCounted} not-counted, {noReview} no-review` — alongside the existing per-match `FAILED` line. Also added `SyncRunResult.detailAlreadyDone` (matches whose `ingestStatus` was already `DONE` before this run started, captured right after index-sync/before the detail-ingest loop, since index-sync never touches `ingestStatus`) — threaded into the final run summary and, separately, into `scripts/runSyncCli.ts`'s 15s periodic progress line (captured independently there before calling `runSync`, since the periodic timer ticks while `runSync` is still running and can't read its return value early).
- What changed: Purged and reset the local DB (`npx prisma migrate reset --force` — `--skip-seed` doesn't exist in this Prisma CLI version, same as before; confirmed no seed ran anyway, all 5 tables empty afterward) to restart the backfill clean, now that the resignation-event crash is confirmed fixed and verified above — this was an explicit precondition before purging, specifically to avoid the freshly-restarted run hitting the identical crash again on the same matches. Fresh consent obtained live via Prisma's own AI-agent safety guard (same mechanism as the earlier reset this session — prior agreement doesn't count, has to be re-confirmed in the moment). All 5 migrations reapplied cleanly; Prisma client regenerated; dev server restarted with `.next` cleared.
- What's next: Run the backfill (`scripts/backfill.ts`) against the now-empty DB — the new per-match/per-run visibility (per-match accounting line, `detailAlreadyDone`) should make it much easier to watch this run and catch the next unknown shape quickly if one turns up. Other still-open items unchanged: "sync all new" button, index-sync job, DB-presence indicator, `Decision.myTag`.

## 2026-09-22
- What changed: Added file-based error logging to the sync scripts, additive to the existing short console line (not a replacement). New `lib/errorLog.ts`'s `appendSyncErrorLog` appends a JSON-lines entry (`timestamp`/`matchId`/`message`/`stack`) to `logs/sync-errors.log`, creating the `logs/` directory on first write if needed; writes are wrapped in try/catch (a `console.warn` on failure, never thrown) so a filesystem problem can't break the sync itself — relevant since `runSync` is also called from the Vercel API route, where the local filesystem may not be writable. Wired into `lib/sync.ts`'s existing per-match `FAILED` catch block, right alongside the unchanged `console.error(\`match ${externalMatchId} FAILED: ${message}\`)` line — the file gets the full error (`error.stack` when available, not just `.message`) plus `matchId`; `gameIndex`/`eventId` aren't separately structured fields since `runSync`'s catch only ever sees the already-joined error message from `IngestSummary.errors` (which embeds "game X event Y: ..." as text for per-event failures) — didn't restructure `lib/ingest.ts`'s error collection to break these out separately, out of scope for a pure-visibility change. `scripts/runSyncCli.ts` (shared by both `backfill.ts` and `incremental-sync.ts`) prints "Errors will be logged to logs/sync-errors.log" once at the start of every run. Added `/logs` to `.gitignore`. No external logging service/SDK — local file only, as specified.
  Verified: a throwaway script writing two fake entries (one a real `Error` with a real stack, one a plain string) produced correctly formatted, one-per-line JSON with the directory auto-created; confirmed `git status --ignored` reports `logs/` as ignored. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` both clean. Test log file and directory removed after verification (not real failure data).
- What changed: Fixed a confusing conflation in `scripts/runSyncCli.ts`'s 15s periodic progress line — "done" was the *live* total `ingestStatus: DONE` count, which already includes matches that were done before the run even started, so a run against a mostly-synced DB showed e.g. "2348 done" at tick zero, before anything had actually been processed. Split into `doneThisRun` (`totalDone - alreadyDoneBeforeRun`, so it correctly starts at 0) and `alreadyDoneBeforeRun` (the pre-existing fixed snapshot, renamed from the local `detailAlreadyDone` for clarity — unchanged in value/meaning). New line reads `...progress: {doneThisRun} done this run, {failed} failed, {remaining} remaining, {alreadyDoneBeforeRun} already done before this run`. The final run summary (`matchesSynced`/`result.detailAlreadyDone` in the same file) wasn't actually conflated — `matchesSynced` already only counts this run's completions and `detailAlreadyDone` was already a separate fixed snapshot — so left unchanged, per the task's own "if it has the same conflation" condition. `remaining`/`failed` counts untouched. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean; smoke-tested the CLI entrypoint with no token set (exercises the top of `runSyncFromCli` including the new snapshot line) to confirm it still parses/runs.
- What changed: Diagnosed and fixed the backfill's next real crash, reported via `logs/sync-errors.log` — match `32699544`, 53 failed events, all `prisma.decision.upsert()` rejecting `rawError: null`. Root cause: `Decision.rawError` was a required `Float`, but this match has events where `error_analysis` is non-null (real `error_severity`/`is_blunder`/`luck`) yet `error_analysis.raw_error` itself is null — `analysis_level: 1`, `error_severity: "doubtful"`, `event_type: "dice_rolled"` paired with `analysed_event: "cube_double"` — a partial/low-confidence analysis with no computed equity-error magnitude. Fixed: `Decision.rawError` → `Float?` (migration `nullable_raw_error`); `lib/gameReviewsTypes.ts`'s `ErrorAnalysis.raw_error` corrected to `number | null` to match reality; `lib/mistakes.ts`'s `extractDecisions` now excludes `rawError: null` decisions entirely (both PR numerator and denominator), same treatment as `count_as_decision: false` — previously `Math.abs(null)` would've silently coerced to `0` and counted an ungraded decision as a clean play. `lib/ingest.ts` needed no read-side change (already passes `errorAnalysis.raw_error` straight through).
  Also root-caused *why* this crashed the whole backfill process rather than just failing one match, since `ingestMatch`'s per-event `try`/`catch` already existed and doesn't rethrow: `ingestMatch`'s 53 per-event error strings (each a multi-KB Prisma dump) get joined with `"; "` into one ~480KB message, and `lib/sync.ts`'s per-match catch wrote that *whole* message into `Match.ingestError` — a MySQL `TEXT` column capped at 64KB. That write itself threw, **uncaught**, propagating past the per-match catch and killing the entire `runSyncFromCli` process — confirmed by directly reproducing the same write against a real row (a plain 500KB `ingestError` update throws exactly this way). Fixed with a bounded `summarizeError` helper in `lib/sync.ts` (flattens to one line, truncates to 500 chars) used for the console line, `Match.ingestError`, and the `SyncRunError` pushed into `errors[]`/`SyncRun.errors` — the full untruncated detail (stack included) still goes to `logs/sync-errors.log` exactly as before, unaffected. Also wrapped the failure-handling writes themselves (`appendSyncErrorLog` + the `FAILED` `Match.update`) in their own inner `try`/`catch`, so a problem while *recording* a failure can't propagate either — genuine defense-in-depth per the task, independent of the specific `rawError` bug.
  `docs/field-mapping.md`: added `rawError` to the nullable-fields reasoning, plus a new "Debugging notes" section documenting the misleading Prisma error message this bug produced ("Argument `game` is missing" when the real problem was `rawError: null` failing type validation elsewhere in the same payload — diff the dumped create/update object against the schema field-by-field, don't trust the literal error text) and the TEXT-column-overflow crash mechanism.
  Verified against the real match: re-ran `ingestMatch` against `32699544` directly — 340/340 decisions ingested, 0 errors, 53 confirmed persisted with `rawError: null`. Separately reproduced the pre-fix crash mechanism directly (writing an untruncated ~500KB string to a real `Match.ingestError` throws, confirming the diagnosis) before cleaning up all test fixtures/log entries. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean; dev server restarted with `.next` cleared (schema change).
  Also noticed and fixed, while adding this entry: `PROGRESS.md`'s dates had gotten scrambled by an earlier edit whose search text matched a stray earlier occurrence — a chunk of genuine 09-20 content (collation fix, `/status` page, resumable sync system) and a chunk of genuine 09-21 content (resignation fix, visibility counters, DB reset) had both ended up misplaced under an out-of-order "## 2026-09-22" heading, sitting before the real 09-21 section. Reordered back into correct chronological sections; no content was changed, only moved.
- What's next: Resume the backfill — should now get past match `32699544` cleanly and, if it hits another unknown shape, will log a short summary to console/DB and full detail to `logs/sync-errors.log` without taking down the whole run.
- What changed: Diagnosed (no code changes) whether `count_as_decision: false` events risk a silent ingest gap for a future game-replay scrubber. Confirmed by reading `lib/ingest.ts`: `count_as_decision === false` does **not** skip the Decision row by itself — it only increments a counter; the row is still written with `countAsDecision: false` and real grading fields, as long as `error_analysis` is non-null. The only thing that actually skips a row is the separate `error_analysis === null` check. Checked whether these two conditions could combine into an unaccounted-for gap (a `count_as_decision: false` event that's *also* `error_analysis: null`) for the two confirmed-safe null-`error_analysis` types, `double_rejected`/`double_accepted`: `logs/sync-errors.log` couldn't answer it (only captures per-match failures, not silent per-event skips — its 82 entries turned out to all be unrelated Galaxy `429` rate-limit failures from the in-progress backfill, worth knowing about separately). The DB query could: against 984,552 already-ingested Decision rows, every stored `double_accepted`/`double_rejected` row (4,034 combined) has `countAsDecision: true` — zero `false`. No evidence the combination has ever dropped a row for these two types. Documented the finding and the reasoning in `docs/field-mapping.md`'s "Events skipped via null error_analysis" section rather than changing ingest logic, since there's no confirmed gap to fix — the existing unrecognized-`event_type`/unconfirmed-null-`error_analysis` warnings remain the real safety net if a different event type ever does drop a row that matters.

Confirmed the existing convention first: `source: "galaxy"` everywhere (`lib/ingest.ts`, `lib/sync.ts`, the list-page route all share one `SOURCE` constant, same pattern as `Match.source`) — every new opponent row uses the same value, never null or invented. `lib/ingest.ts`'s `ingestMatch` now resolves the opponent's `user_id` from a match's own event stream (a match is 1v1, so any `user_id` that isn't the already-known `isMe: true` one is unambiguously the opponent) and upserts a `PlayerIdentity` row for them (`displayName: indexData.opponentName`, `isMe: false` set explicitly since the column defaults `true`) after each match — this depends on the `isMe: true` row already existing, so also added the same "you" upsert to `lib/sync.ts`'s index-sync step (mirroring the list-page route) so the CLI/script path establishes "you" before detail-ingest runs, not just the browser UI. New permanent script `scripts/backfill-opponent-identities.ts` (idempotent, safe to re-run) backfilled the 2,111 opponents from matches ingested before this feature existed, computing "most recently played match's name" per opponent across the whole dataset (not picked arbitrarily) via `Match.playedAt`/`createdAt`. `docs/field-mapping.md`'s `PlayerIdentity` section rewritten to document all of this, with an explicit callout that `source` is what scopes a bare ID's meaning and is mandatory on every row.

Re-ran `ingestMatch` against the already-DONE `32699544` directly to confirm the ongoing (not just backfill) path works: 0 errors, `PlayerIdentity` count unchanged at 2,112 (idempotent), and both of that match's real userIds resolved correctly (`62febc99a2eb070024ace099` → `meisam2`/`isMe: true`, `68d4f47eb9c67c52b7aa6e09` → `djuret`/`isMe: false`). `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean; dev server restarted with `.next` cleared.

Key fact: today (2026-09-22) is already past the April 2026 cutoff after which Oracle stopped allowing new MySQL 8.0 DB Systems — confirmed via web search (Oracle's own MySQL blog + third-party coverage) that Always Free HeatWave now exists as a `MySQL.Free` shape, and Oracle's own guidance is to create new instances on 8.4 LTS directly. Checked our local container directly (`mysql --version` inside it): **already running MySQL 8.4.11**, not 8.0.x — `docker-compose.yml`'s floating `mysql:8` tag had silently resolved to the 8.4 LTS line on Docker Hub, without anyone choosing it. Net effect: no schema/Prisma/code changes needed — Prisma 7.10.0 + `@prisma/adapter-mariadb` have already run cleanly against 8.4.11 all session (6 migrations, 1.2M+ rows), and the eventual Oracle provisioning step is naturally unblocked (Oracle will only offer 8.4+ by the time we get there). Made the version explicit rather than accidental: `docker-compose.yml` now pins `mysql:8.4` (not the floating `mysql:8`) so local dev can't silently drift to a different version next; `app/status/notes.ts` and `README.md` updated to say "MySQL 8.4 LTS" instead of generic "MySQL 8".
  Verified the pin doesn't disrupt anything: `docker compose config` shows the new tag; the already-running container (up 2 days, untouched) still reports `mysql --version` 8.4.11 — a tag change only affects a future recreate, not the live container/volume.

## 2026-09-23
- What changed: Set up credential scoping — the app now connects with the minimum privilege each part actually needs, ahead of the two real Oracle HeatWave MySQL users (`bg_readwrite`: full grants for ingest + schema migrations; `bg_readonly`: `SELECT` only). `lib/prisma.ts` now exports two clients (`prisma` reading `DATABASE_URL`, `prismaReadOnly` reading the new `DATABASE_URL_READONLY`) built through one shared `createClient` helper instead of duplicating the adapter-construction boilerplate. Kept the existing `prisma` export name for the read-write client specifically so every current read-write importer (`lib/ingest.ts`, `lib/sync.ts`, `scripts/runSyncCli.ts` and by extension `scripts/backfill.ts`/`scripts/incremental-sync.ts`/`/api/sync/incremental`, `app/api/galaxy/matches/list/[page]/route.ts` which writes the `isMe` `PlayerIdentity` row, `prisma/seed.ts`) needed **zero code changes** — only the pure-read call sites moved: `lib/local-client.ts` (explicitly named — the whole `/matches` DB-backed read path, including `/api/matches/list/[page]` and `/api/matches/[matchId]/[gameIndex]` which route through it) now imports `prismaReadOnly`. Confirmed `prisma7.config.ts` (which `prisma migrate deploy` reads) is untouched and still points at `DATABASE_URL` — migrations keep full admin credentials, as instructed, since schema changes need `CREATE`/`ALTER`/`INDEX`/`REFERENCES` beyond plain app read-write.
  Two additions beyond the explicit call-site list, included because they're unambiguously read-only and the whole point was "minimum privilege everywhere," flagged rather than silently expanded: `app/api/player-identities/route.ts` (a plain `findMany`, no writes) and `app/status/page.tsx` (row counts + connection check, no writes) both switched to the read-only client — status page kept its own isolated per-request `PrismaClient` construction (didn't adopt the shared singleton, so a status check can't be misled by shared-pool state), just swapped which URL it reads. Left `app/api/db-check/route.ts` on `DATABASE_URL` deliberately — its specific job is verifying that exact connection works, not general read-only health-checking, so switching it would change what it tests.
  One flagged ambiguous case, resolved to read-write per your explicit instruction: `scripts/backfill-opponent-identities.ts` is named like a "backfill" script but actually *writes* (upserts `PlayerIdentity` rows) — kept on the read-write client despite the naming similarity to "future opponent-identity backfill scripts" in your read-only bucket. Documented the rule going forward in `docs/field-mapping.md`'s new "Credential scoping" section: only *exclusively-reading* scripts get read-only; any script that writes, even a one-time backfill, gets read-write.
  `.env.example` now documents both vars with full comments (which call sites use which, the Oracle NLB/SSL/`allowPublicKeyRetrieval=true` connection-string shape for production); `.env` got the new `DATABASE_URL_READONLY` too (pointed at the same single local Docker user — no local privilege separation exists to test against, documented as such). Verified the SSL/`allowPublicKeyRetrieval` query-param approach isn't a guess: read `@prisma/adapter-mariadb`'s actual source — `normalizeConfig` rewrites a `mysql://` URL's protocol to `mariadb://` and passes the full query string through unchanged via `URL`/`toString()`, and the `mariadb` driver's own `ConnectionOptions.parse` reads arbitrary query params off that string into connection options — so `?ssl=true&allowPublicKeyRetrieval=true` on the URL genuinely works, not assumed.
  **Vercel env vars: guidance only, not actually set.** No `.vercel` project link exists in this repo and I don't have real `bg_readwrite`/`bg_readonly` passwords, so I can't run `vercel env add` myself — you'll need to set both `DATABASE_URL` and `DATABASE_URL_READONLY` in the Vercel dashboard (or `vercel env add <name> production`) for production and any preview environments, using the real Oracle HeatWave connection strings shaped like the examples in `.env.example`.
  Verified: `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean; dev server restarted and confirmed live — `/status` shows a real `4274` match count (proving `DATABASE_URL_READONLY` actually connects and queries), `/api/player-identities` returns real rows, `/api/matches/list/1` (routing through `local-client.ts`) returns 200; `npx prisma validate` confirms `prisma7.config.ts` still resolves `DATABASE_URL` correctly for migrations.

- Later same day: migrated the actual local `app_dev` data (4,274 Matches, 15,344 Games, 1,232,074 Decisions, 2,112 PlayerIdentities, 8 SyncRuns) to Oracle HeatWave's `backgammon` database via a data-only `mysqldump`/`mysql` restore — full verification report in `docs/oracle-migration-verification-2026-09-23.md`. Row counts, spot-checked data, and the four `utf8mb4_bin` collation pins all confirmed identical/intact on Oracle; all 6 migrations applied cleanly (`_prisma_migrations` matches local exactly). Scoped `bg_db_rw`/`bg_db_ro` Oracle credentials confirmed working via DBeaver (an initial CLI-based check falsely reported access denied — traced to a bug in my own password-extraction/piping chain, not the actual credentials in `.env`, which are correct as documented). One remaining optional item: a live-Galaxy re-verify was skipped as redundant with the 2026-09-22 local check.
- Still later: pointed `.env`'s `DATABASE_URL`/`DATABASE_URL_READONLY` at the scoped `bg_db_rw`/`bg_db_ro` Oracle credentials (previously `DATABASE_URL` was the admin `awesomebg` user, `DATABASE_URL_READONLY` unset) — the admin URL is kept commented for the rare schema/DROP work `bg_db_rw` can't do. This immediately surfaced a real connectivity bug: the app's actual `PrismaMariaDb`/`mariadb` driver couldn't connect to Oracle at all, timing out with an opaque `pool timeout` rather than a clear error. Root cause: Oracle HeatWave issues its own private CA (`CN=MySQL_Endpoint_CA`, self-signed) for this DB endpoint, and `ssl=true` alone uses Node's default (publicly-trusted-CA-only) verification, which rejects it. Fetched the CA cert directly from the server's TLS handshake (`openssl s_client -starttls mysql`, committed to `certs/oracle-mysql-ca.pem` — it's public, not a secret) and added `lib/prisma.ts`'s new exported `buildConnectionConfig` helper, which parses the connection URL and pins that CA via `ssl.ca` whenever `ssl=true` is present — real chain-of-trust validation, not disabled verification. Also had to set `checkServerIdentity: () => undefined` to skip hostname matching specifically (the driver doesn't forward `host` into its TLS call at all, and even fixed, Oracle's cert has no SAN and its CN isn't the IP the app connects by) — chain validation against the pinned CA still fully applies, so this doesn't weaken the actual security property (rejecting an unsigned/rogue cert) being relied on. `app/status/page.tsx` builds its own isolated Prisma client by design (not the shared singleton) and was bypassing this fix entirely until updated to reuse the same exported helper. Verified end-to-end: `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean, and `/status` now shows `Connection: OK`, `Matches: 4274`, `Latest migration: 20260922003421_nullable_raw_error` — live against Oracle, through the real app code path (not a one-off script). `app/api/db-check/route.ts` and `prisma/seed.ts` also construct their own Prisma clients directly but were deliberately left unfixed — both are local-dev-only by design (db-check is disabled in production; seed deletes all data first, so must never touch Oracle). Full detail in `docs/field-mapping.md`'s SSL section.
- Still later: `/matches` was sorting by `Match.id` (internal insertion order, i.e. whatever order index-sync happened to run in), not play chronology — confirmed this against live data: matchId 29991558 (an old-looking Galaxy ID) sits right alongside 46868313 (a recent one) once actually sorted by `playedAt`, so the two orders genuinely diverge. Fixed `lib/local-client.ts`'s `listMatches` to `orderBy: [{ playedAt: "desc" }, { createdAt: "desc" }]` — `createdAt` as tiebreaker both for same-`playedAt` matches and for the (real, confirmed via a live row: match `6029642`, `ingestStatus: DONE` but no game contributed a `playedAt`) case of `playedAt` still being null, consistent with the same fallback convention already used for opponent `displayName` resolution (`docs/field-mapping.md`'s PlayerIdentity section). Added a `playedAt` field to the shared `MatchAnalysis` type (`lib/analysesTypes.ts`) — optional, since Galaxy's live API has no equivalent field, so `lib/galaxy-client.ts`'s path is unaffected. `app/matches/page.tsx` now has a Date column (new fixed-abbreviation `formatMatchDate` helper — deliberately not `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, since Node's ICU renders "Sept" not "Sep" for short-month and this needed to be deterministic, not environment-dependent). Used `prismaReadOnly` throughout, unchanged. Verified live: `/api/matches/list/1`'s order now genuinely tracks `playedAt` descending (not `id`), the one null-`playedAt` match correctly falls back to `createdAt` and lands on the last page (143 of 143, MySQL's NULL-sorts-last-in-DESC), and the date formatter produces exactly `"22 Sep 2026"` for real values. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean.
- Still later: investigated "`/galaxy/matches` doesn't show recently played matches" — tested live against the real Galaxy API (not just static reading) to rule out each hypothesis in turn: no caching layer (`cache: "no-store"`, `dynamic = "force-dynamic"`, no SWR — confirmed by a fresh out-of-band call matching what the app would see), pagination/`totalPages` isn't stale (each fetch reads it fresh; live total is ~4,284 vs our DB's 4,274, a real 10-match sync gap, separate issue), and no meaningful Galaxy-side propagation delay (pulled the actual play timestamp off the newest match's events — `2026-09-23T05:45:14Z` — already live in `analyses/list` ~45 minutes later). Actual root cause: `analyses/list` sorts each page **ascending** by matchId (oldest-in-page first), and page 1 currently holds the most-recent batch — so a user's newest matches were landing at the *bottom* of a 30-row page-1 table instead of the top, easy to miss without scrolling. Not a caching or delay bug — the new matches were already being fetched and rendered, just badly ordered. Fixed: `app/galaxy/matches/page.tsx` now sorts `data.analyses` by matchId descending before rendering (Galaxy's response has no play-timestamp field to sort by instead; matchId descending is the best available proxy within a page). Verified against a live fetch: the newest match (`woozoo`, matchId `46945884`) now sorts first. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean.
- Still later: the per-row Sync button on `/galaxy/matches` always showed, even for matches already fully ingested. Added `app/api/matches/check-existence/route.ts` (GET, `prismaReadOnly`, no auth needed — DB-only data) — one batched `sourceMatchId IN (...) AND ingestStatus = 'DONE'` query per page load, not N per-match lookups. `app/galaxy/matches/page.tsx` calls it in a new `useEffect` keyed off `data` (so it naturally re-batches per page, never the whole account at once) and merges the result into the *existing* `syncStates` UI (a match confirmed `DONE` in the DB gets the same "✓ Synced" treatment already used for a same-session click, only as a fallback when no session-local state exists yet) — `onSyncMatch`'s click behavior itself is untouched, per the ask. Deliberately excludes `PENDING`/`INGESTING`/`FAILED` matches (still shows Sync for those, confirmed via a temporary local-only test row: inserted a `PENDING` match, verified the query correctly excluded it while returning real `DONE` ones, then deleted it — never touched Oracle for this test). Verified end-to-end against the live Oracle-backed route with real data: matches `stavros`/`ryan_lee`/`gimd` (already synced) come back in `done`, the 2 newest unsynced matches from the sort-order fix above correctly don't. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean.
- Still later: the "✓ Synced" indicator was a clickable button offering re-sync — changed to a plain non-interactive `<span>` (no `onClick`, no hover styling) per feedback that an already-synced match shouldn't need/offer a re-sync action. `onSyncMatch` itself untouched; only reachable now via the actual "Sync" button on a not-yet-done match.
- Still later: root-caused "`/matches` dates look wrong" through a rigorous multi-step investigation before touching any code (display formatting, query/sort, raw stored values, then `lib/ingest.ts`) — full findings in `docs/field-mapping.md`'s new "`playedAt`: what it actually means" section under Game. Bottom line: `metadata.timestamp` (the only per-event timestamp Galaxy's API exposes anywhere) is not a play-time fact at all — it's stamped with when Galaxy served that specific analysis, confirmed by re-fetching the same event seconds apart and watching the timestamp advance, and by checking 10 known-ancient matches (deep in `analyses/list` pagination) whose stored `playedAt` all clustered within the same few minutes as whichever `SyncRun` first requested them — true even for just the first decision of a game specifically, not only a match-wide average. No alternative timestamp exists anywhere else in Galaxy's API (`game_started` never carries `metadata` at all; `analyses/list` has no date-like field). Given that, landed on a deliberate, documented-as-imperfect rule: `Game.playedAt` = `metadata.timestamp` of that game's first decision **by eventId** (not earliest value) with a populated `error_analysis`; `Match.playedAt` = that value from the match's first game. Updated `lib/ingest.ts` to this rule for all future ingests/incremental syncs (accurate going forward, since near-real-time sync makes request-time ≈ play-time). Added `scripts/backfill-played-at.ts` — reconciles already-ingested matches to the new rule from stored `Decision.raw` JSON only (no live Galaxy calls), `--dry-run` supported, uses `prisma` (read-write) against whatever `DATABASE_URL` is configured. Dry-run tested locally (not against Oracle, per instruction): 4,274 matches / 15,344 games processed, 1,878 matches and 6,750 games would change (mostly sub-second refinements for matches ingested near-real-time — the new by-eventId rule usually agrees closely with the old min-by-value one), exactly 1 match (`6029642`, already known from an earlier session) and 5 games found with no valid decision at all, confirmed dry-run wrote nothing by spot-checking the DB after. **Explicit, permanent limitation, not fixed and not fixable**: the original 2026-09 backfill's `playedAt` values (the bulk of this app's historical data) reflect ingest-processing time, not real play dates — no real source exists to recover them. Not run for real anywhere yet, and not run against Oracle at all — per instruction, that's a separate step to do after reviewing the dry-run output.
- Still later: added npm script aliases for everything in `scripts/` — `backfill`, `sync:incremental`, `backfill:opponent-identities`, `backfill:played-at` — so e.g. `npm run backfill:played-at -- --dry-run` works (note the `--` to forward flags through npm). Verified argument-forwarding actually works, not just that the aliases resolve.
- Still later: `/matches` (DB-backed) was still sorting by `playedAt`, which — per the finding above — is unreliable for most of the historical dataset. Switched `lib/local-client.ts`'s `listMatches` to sort by `sourceMatchId` (Galaxy's own matchId) descending instead, matching the same proxy `/galaxy/matches` already sorts by. Had to switch that one query from Prisma's `orderBy` to raw SQL (`prisma.$queryRaw` with `ORDER BY CAST(sourceMatchId AS UNSIGNED) DESC`) — `sourceMatchId` is a VARCHAR with values ranging from 5 to 8+ digits, so a plain string sort would be lexicographically wrong (e.g. a shorter numeric string can sort above a longer, larger one). Verified against live local data across the full page range: page 1 descends `46958192 → ... → 46888738` correctly, and the last page (143) correctly tails off `977248 → ... → 19719` — confirms the numeric cast is doing its job, not just string-sorting by coincidence. The `playedAt` column is still displayed per-row (date column from an earlier change), just no longer drives sort order.
- What's next: Vercel env vars still aren't actually set with real Oracle credentials — do that before the next deploy, now using the scoped `bg_db_rw`/`bg_db_ro` URLs with `ssl=true&allowPublicKeyRetrieval=true` (the shape that's confirmed working locally against Oracle, not the admin `awesomebg` one). Also open: the local container image (`mysql:8`) still doesn't match `docker-compose.yml`'s `mysql:8.4` pin noted in the 2026-09-20 entry — a `docker compose up -d --force-recreate` would fix that, not done here since it's unrelated to this session's work. `/galaxy/matches`' token still gets wiped on every page reload — a still-open UX question, not addressed in this pass. `scripts/backfill-played-at.ts --dry-run` still needs to be run and reviewed locally, then for real locally, then (separately, dry-run first) against Oracle — not done yet.
- Later same day: added a "Match ID" column to `/matches` (shows `sourceMatchId` directly, useful for eyeballing the new matchId-based sort order).

## 2026-09-24
- What changed: Built a mistake/pattern-analysis view, `app/matches/analysis/page.tsx` — a server component (like `/status`, not a client-fetched API route like `/matches`), queried fresh on every load via `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"`, using `prismaReadOnly` throughout (pure read feature, no writes/schema/ingest changes). Two sections, both raw SQL (`$queryRaw`, not Prisma's `groupBy`) since averaging `ABS(rawError)` isn't expressible through Prisma's aggregation API: (1) error concentration by `classification` (game phase) — total/blunder/error/doubtful counts plus avg `|rawError|` per phase; (2) the same breakdown split by `kind` (CHECKER/CUBE/RESIGNATION — the schema's own existing checker-vs-cube split, set from `analysed_event` at ingest time, so grouping by it directly rather than re-deriving the same split from `analysedEvent` strings). Both filtered to `countAsDecision = true AND rawError IS NOT NULL`, matching the ask exactly. Rendered as plain tables, not charts — no charting library exists anywhere in this app yet, and the ask was explicit not to add one just for this. Discovered along the way: `COUNT(*)` comes back as a JS `bigint`, and `SUM(CASE...)` comes back as a Prisma-internal `Decimal`-like object (`constructor.name: "Decimal2"`, not a plain number) — both confirmed to coerce correctly via `Number(...)`, checked directly against real query output before relying on it, not assumed. Added a small nav link from `/matches` to the new page (`Mistake pattern analysis →`) since the app has no shared header/nav — otherwise the page would be reachable only by typing the URL. Verified live: `npm run lint`/`build` clean, curled the actual route and confirmed both tables render with real current numbers (17 classifications, 3 decision-kind rows, totals matching what's in the DB right now).
- What's next: v1 scope deliberately stops at the two aggregate tables — no date-range filtering, no drill-down into individual flagged decisions yet (explicitly deferred, per the ask, until the aggregate shape has actually been reviewed). Everything else carried over from 2026-09-23 (Vercel env vars, `mysql:8` vs `8.4` pin, `/galaxy/matches` token persistence, `scripts/backfill-played-at.ts` still not run for real anywhere) remains open and untouched by this session.
- Later same day: reworked the analysis page into a precomputed-table design. Before touching schema, re-ran and reported the full real taxonomy (17 `classification` values, 4 `errorSeverity` values, confirmed `Decision.kind` — not a re-derivation of `analysedEvent` — is the right cube-vs-checker field, 1:1 with no leakage) — caught two wrong premises along the way rather than building on them: (1) the task claimed `Decision.kind`/`errorSeverity` were "already deliberately changed from enum to string earlier in this project" — checked full git history, that never happened, they've been real Prisma enums since introduced; landed on keeping real enums (`DecisionKind`/`ErrorSeverity`) for `MistakeStat.category`/`errorSeverity`, `classification` stays a plain string (no enum exists for it). (2) Suspected "positive rawError values are cube decisions" — checked: of the 90 positive rawError rows in the whole table, only 4 are `CUBE`; 73 are `RESIGNATION`, 13 are `CHECKER`. Doesn't change the math though — all 90 are still exclusively `errorSeverity: NONE`, so `ABS(SUM(rawError))` from Prisma's actual `groupBy()` stays exactly equal to `SUM(ABS(rawError))` for every severity that matters (BLUNDER/ERROR/DOUBTFUL), letting the recompute function use real `groupBy()` instead of raw SQL. Added `MistakeStat` (new migration `20260924001704_add_mistake_stat`, generated/applied against local — `.env` was pointed at Oracle at the time, deliberately not touched for this). Added `lib/recompute-mistake-stats.ts` (full DELETE + bulk-insert replace inside a transaction, not incremental upsert — self-corrects after any retroactive Decision fix, like this session's earlier rawError/playedAt corrections, instead of accumulating drift) and wired one call into `lib/sync.ts`'s `runSync`, once at the very end after all matches in a run finish, wrapped in its own try/catch so a recompute failure can't erase an otherwise-successful sync — both `scripts/backfill.ts` and `scripts/incremental-sync.ts`/`/api/sync/incremental` route through `runSync`, so both keep `MistakeStat` current automatically, no separate scheduling needed. Rewrote `app/matches/analysis/page.tsx` to read only `MistakeStat` via `prismaReadOnly.findMany()` (no aggregation against the much larger Decision table at read time) and aggregate across the other axis in memory — classification × errorSeverity for Section 1, category × errorSeverity for Section 2 — with no hardcoded classification/category values anywhere in the UI (rows are ordered by total decisionCount, purely data-driven). Verified thoroughly against local: recompute produces 173 rows whose summed decisionCount matches the source `Decision` count exactly (592,729 = 592,729); the page's in-memory aggregation expands that correctly to 68 rows (17 classifications × 4 severities) and 12 rows (3 categories × 4 severities), with decision-count sums matching exactly across all three views. Real numbers show a genuine pattern: CUBE blunders average a larger error (0.194) than CHECKER blunders (0.166). `npm run lint`/`build` clean.
- What's next (superseded below — done later the same day): the new migration has only been applied locally — needs `prisma migrate deploy` against Oracle before `MistakeStat` exists there, and `recomputeMistakeStats()` needs to run once to populate it there.
- Later same day: measured the recompute step for real before optimizing anything, per instruction — added temporary timing around the `recomputeMistakeStats()` call in `runSync`, triggered a real local incremental sync, measured **7,417ms**. Checked why: `Decision` has 1,236,264 rows, and `SHOW INDEX` confirmed no index covers `(countAsDecision, rawError)` (the `groupBy`'s `WHERE` filter) — the existing `(kind, classification)` index doesn't include `errorSeverity` either, so the query was a full table scan. Added `@@index([countAsDecision, rawError, kind, classification, errorSeverity])` to `Decision` (migration `20260924005408_add_decision_mistake_stat_index`, generated/applied locally only, Oracle untouched). `EXPLAIN` before vs. after: full table scan → index range scan using the new index as a covering index (`Using index`, ~513k estimated rows vs. all 1.24M; `Using temporary` still shows since the index's trailing column order doesn't exactly match the `GROUP BY` order, but that alone didn't stop the win). Re-measured the same way (real incremental sync, temporary timing, reverted after): **398ms**, then **345ms** on a second run to confirm it wasn't a fluke — an ~18-21x speedup, comfortably under Vercel's 10s budget with real headroom left over. No further action needed per the stated threshold; next step (not done here) is applying the same index migration to Oracle.

Also did a status check across four open threads from earlier sessions (not fixing anything, just confirming real state): `.env` now points at Oracle with scoped `bg_db_rw`/`bg_db_ro` credentials; `scripts/backfill-played-at.ts`'s real (non-dry-run) backfill turned out to have already been run against **both** local and Oracle at some point outside this conversation (confirmed empirically — a fresh dry-run against each shows 0 pending changes, matching edge-case counts on both sides) — not something done by this session's Claude instance, most likely the user running it independently; the Sync-button existence-check and the single-decision board view (real GNU Position ID decoder, not a stub) were both confirmed already fully built and wired into real routes.
- Later same day: built `/mistakes` — a paginated, filterable decision-browsing page, reusing `BoardPanel` (and by extension `Board`/`Dice`/`lib/gnuPositionId.ts`) completely unmodified. `lib/decisionFromRow.ts` (new, server-only) builds the exact `lib/mistakes.ts` `Decision` shape directly from one DB `Decision` row's own `raw` JSON — reuses `lib/mistakes.ts`'s existing `actionLabels`/`moveNotations` (only change to that file: added `export` to those two, no logic touched) rather than re-deriving the same label logic a second time. One real limitation, disclosed in a comment rather than silently faked: a `move_commited` event's own `rolled_dice` is always empty (the real roll lives on the *preceding* `dice_rolled` event in the full game sequence) — reconstructing it would need the surrounding game's full event list, which this standalone per-decision view deliberately doesn't fetch, so dice only show when an event happens to carry its own roll directly. `errorSeverity: DOUBTFUL` maps to the nearest existing client-side bucket (`"error"`) rather than extending `lib/mistakes.ts`'s `Severity` type, since `BoardPanel` (reused as-is) only ever branches on `=== "blunder"` vs. anything else. Filters (`classification`, `category`, `severity`) are query params, 50/page, `ORDER BY eventId DESC`, `prismaReadOnly` throughout; the classification filter's dropdown options are queried live (`distinct`), never hardcoded. Wired `/matches/analysis`'s Blunders/Errors/Doubtful/Total cells into links (`CountLink`) pointing at the matching `/mistakes` filter combination, so the aggregate dashboard now drills down to the underlying decisions it summarizes. Verified live against real Oracle data (the user's own already-running dev server, not a new one): drill-down link → `/mistakes?classification=opening_game&severity=blunder` → header reads exactly "823 opening_game blunder decisions" (matching the known count precisely) → Page 1 of 17 (823÷50, correct) → 64 real `<svg>` elements across 50 cards (boards plus dice on the rows that had one) → correct classification/severity badges → "View match →" correctly linking to `/matches/46791634`. `npm run lint`/`build` clean. Also excluded `.next-test/**` from ESLint (`eslint.config.mjs`) and `e2e/**` from the root `tsconfig.json`'s typecheck (matching the existing `scripts/**` exclusion — these are standalone tooling, not app code `next build` needs to typecheck), both surfaced as real breakage by `npm run lint`/`npm run build` before being fixed.

- Hit a new flake while making this change: after switching to click-based selection, `/mistakes` screenshots started intermittently failing by ~1% of pixels — the same magnitude/signature as the earlier GPU-compositor jitter, but now also hitting `/mistakes` for the first time (its old `?id=`-based tests never clicked anything, so never exercised this path). Ruled out a hydration-timing race first (added `expect(boardPanel).toContainText(decision.myLabel)` before screenshotting, as a real content-correctness synchronization barrier) — didn't fix it, and the failure pattern (spread across multiple decisions, never the same one twice) confirmed it's genuine cross-run rendering non-determinism in Chromium's rasterization of diagonal-arrow board states, not a click/data race. `--disable-gpu` reduces this but doesn't fully eliminate it for every arrow pattern. Fixed with `retries: 2` in `playwright.config.ts` (previously CI-only, now everywhere) — the standard mitigation for this exact class of noise: a real regression fails identically on every retry (verified: both mutation checks from the day before still fail with a `-retry2` suffix, exhausting all retries), while this jitter has never failed twice in a row in testing. `maxDiffPixelRatio` stays at 0 — still deliberately not loosened, for the same reason as before (a real single-arrow bug measures the same ~1% magnitude as the noise).
