## 2026-09-27
- Investigated (no code written yet, per explicit instruction) whether the source position's GNU Position ID is reliably extractable for a "repeated positions" feature. Corrected a wrong premise in the ask: `reviews[0].result.result.moves[n].final.gnubgid` is NOT the source position — verified directly against real data that it's the *destination* of whichever specific candidate move it's attached to (the `move_played: true` candidate's `final.gnubgid` prefix matched `destination_position.formatted_value` exactly, byte for byte). The real source field is `reviews[0].source_position.formatted_value` — already the exact field this codebase has used as `sourcePositionId` all session (via `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`), consistent with `classification`'s existing documented "source only, never destination" rule. Confirmed 100% present across all 502,178 in-scope `CHECKER` decisions (0 missing); confirmed `CUBE` decisions have no comparable `moves[]`/candidate structure, so they're excluded from this feature entirely. Distinct-position count: 452,122 of 502,178 (~90% unique, ~10% real repetition). Blunder-specific: 333 distinct positions repeated more than once (37,131 distinct of 38,219 total, 97.2% unique — blunders repeat *less* than average), highest repeat count 91 (the standard opening position, `4HPwATDgc/ABMA` — expected given backgammon's highly constrained opening combinatorics, not a bug). Performance finding that shaped the build below: a plain `COUNT(DISTINCT <JSON-extracted expression>)` took 3.5s ungrouped but **341 seconds** once `GROUP BY errorSeverity` was added (4 groups) — no index backs the JSON path, so MySQL re-scans per group; a real dashboard feature needs precomputation, not a live grouped query.
- Built the "repeated positions" feature on top of that investigation. Added `RepeatedPosition` (mirrors `MistakeStat`'s design/recompute pattern exactly) via a new migration (`20260926153758_add_repeated_position`, generated and applied locally only — not yet deployed to Oracle, see below) — one correction to the ask's literal schema sketch: used the real `ErrorSeverity` enum for `errorSeverity` instead of a plain `String`, matching `MistakeStat`'s own field and the reasoning already documented for it (a real enum exists, so it stays typed). `sourcePositionId` is an externally-sourced string identifier (Galaxy's GNU Position ID) — pinned to `COLLATE utf8mb4_bin` by hand in the migration SQL per the standing CLAUDE.md rule, and added to `docs/field-mapping.md`'s pinned-column list, exactly like `Match.sourceMatchId`/`PlayerIdentity.sourceUserId`/`Decision.userId`/`Decision.cubeOwnerUserId` already are.
- `lib/recompute-repeated-positions.ts`: deliberately NOT a grouped SQL query (per the investigation's 341s finding) — one **unaggregated** extraction (no `GROUP BY`, no `DISTINCT`, just `SELECT` the three needed fields for every in-scope `CHECKER` row), grouped/counted in a plain JS `Map` afterward. Measured 2,155ms for the extraction (all 502,146 local rows) — chosen specifically because grouping ~500k short rows in JS is fast and this avoids MySQL's per-group JSON-extraction cost entirely; write (full delete + bulk insert, one transaction, same self-correcting design as `recomputeMistakeStats`) took 396ms. Wired into `lib/sync.ts`'s `runSync` alongside `recomputeMistakeStats`, same placement, same own-try/catch isolation.
- Verified against **local** data (not Oracle — the migration hasn't been deployed there yet, so `RepeatedPosition` doesn't exist on Oracle as of this entry): local's in-scope `Decision` count (502,146) is close to but not identical to Oracle's (502,178, per the investigation) — real but minor sync drift. Recompute produced **4,741 rows**. Spot-check: `4HPwATDgc/ABMA` (the standard opening position) at `errorSeverity: BLUNDER` shows **occurrenceCount: 91** — an exact match to the investigation's Oracle-sourced figure, confirming local and Oracle agree on this specific position despite the small overall drift elsewhere. Noted honestly: the same unaggregated extraction query took 30.2s locally vs. 2.2s on Oracle during the investigation — confirmed via `EXPLAIN` that both use the identical index (`Decision_kind_classification_idx`), so this is an infrastructure/hardware difference (local Docker Desktop vs. Oracle HeatWave), not a query-plan bug; Oracle's number is the more representative one once deployed.
- Built `/repeated-positions`, reusing `/mistakes`'s exact patterns: `mistakes.styles.ts`'s shell/filter/pagination structure mirrored into a new page-local `repeatedPositions.styles.ts` (per `.claude/skills/styling-conventions`, loaded before writing this page); classification+severity dropdowns sourced from `RepeatedPosition` itself (small table, same reasoning as `/mistakes`'s `MistakeStat`-sourced dropdowns); a paginated list of repeated positions (`ClassificationBadge`/`SeverityBadge`/occurrence count, reused unmodified) sorted by `occurrenceCount` desc; clicking one (`?positionId=<RepeatedPosition.id>`) drills into `DecisionListWithDetail` (reused unmodified, same list+single-board-detail component `/mistakes` uses) showing every individual `Decision` occurrence of that exact position. The per-position occurrence lookup narrows by the same indexed columns the recompute query uses (`kind`/`countAsDecision`/`rawError`/`errorSeverity`) before the unindexed JSON match, keeping it cheap once narrowed rather than a full-table scan. Live-verified end to end against a temporary local-DB-pointed dev server (port 3200, cleaned up afterward, no persistent `.env`/tsconfig changes left behind): unfiltered list showed "4,741 all repeated positions" exactly; filtering to `severity=blunder` surfaced the opening position at `91×`; drilling into it rendered `DecisionListWithDetail` with the real "91 occurrences of this position (opening_game, blunder)" text, a real board (`data-testid="board-panel"` present once, matching the single-board-at-a-time pattern), and correct `OG`/`B` badges.
- No shared component (`BoardPanel`, `Board`, `Dice`, `DecisionCard`, `DecisionListWithDetail`, `SeverityBadge`, `ClassificationBadge`) was modified — all reused as-is, so the visual regression suite wasn't strictly required by the "if touching a shared component" instruction, but ran it anyway as a final check: 3 consecutive full runs, all 12/12 passing overall (one pre-existing, already-documented GPU-jitter flake recovered via `retries: 2`, unrelated to this work). `npm run lint`/`build` clean, `npm test` 21/21 unaffected.
- What's next (superseded below): the migration + a real recompute still need to go to Oracle before `/repeated-positions` works there (not done — schema/write changes to Oracle need explicit confirmation first, per this session's standing pattern; asked, not yet answered as of this entry). `/galaxy/matches`'s token persistence and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report are still open.
- Later same day: fixed `/repeated-positions`'s load order — it was missing the same fix `/mistakes` already got (see 2026-09-24 entries): `PositionListSection` rendered unconditionally with no filter gate, so first visit eagerly queried all 4,741 rows instead of showing a prompt. Added the same `hasFilter` gate `/mistakes` uses (`positionId` deep links bypass it, same as before — a click-through is already a deliberate, scoped action, not an eager fetch). Verified: unfiltered load now 20-60ms with zero `RepeatedPosition` query fired (confirmed no row-count text anywhere in the response), filtered load ~50-80ms with real results.
- While re-verifying the detail drill-down (unchanged by this fix, but re-checked anyway) with real timing for the first time — the original build's verification only checked HTTP status and content correctness, never actual latency — found a real bug: `PositionDetailSection`'s per-position `Decision` lookup took **11.4 seconds**, not the sub-second figure implicitly assumed. `EXPLAIN` showed MySQL's optimizer choosing `Decision_kind_classification_idx` (kind-only, ~513k rows to then filter and JSON-extract one by one) over the far more selective composite index that also covers `countAsDecision`/`rawError`/`errorSeverity`, even though the composite index was listed as a usable candidate. Fixed with `FORCE INDEX (Decision_countAsDecision_rawError_kind_classification_errorS_idx)` on that one query — confirmed via direct benchmark: 11.4s → 1.2s for the identical query, 11.4s → ~1.7s end-to-end through the actual page. Not fully instant, but appropriately scoped behind its own `<Suspense>` boundary already, and a genuine ~9x fix rather than a workaround.
- No shared component touched by either fix, but re-ran `npm run test:visual` anyway: 12/12 clean, zero diffs. `npm run lint`/`build` clean, `npm test` 21/21 unaffected.
- What's next (superseded below): the migration + a real recompute still need to go to Oracle before `/repeated-positions` works there — still open, still needs explicit confirmation first. `/galaxy/matches`'s token persistence and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report are still open.
- Later same day: investigated (no code changes) how many plies `classification: opening_game` actually covers, ahead of a planned "opening (first move)" vs "opening (response)" split. Checked 26 real games — completely consistent, zero exceptions: `opening_game` is a fixed 2-ply window (each side's own first move, not just the very first move overall), transitioning straight to `middle_game` (never an intermediate classification) immediately after both sides have moved once — tied to ply count, not to the resulting position (pip count, contact, hits, doubles all irrelevant to when the transition happens).
- Built `lib/classificationLabels.ts` — a single source of truth mapping the 17 real `Decision.classification` values to human-readable labels (`opening_game` → "Opening", `one_man_back` → "One Man Back", etc.), structured as an array of `{ value, label, classification, extraFilter? }` entries (not a flat string dictionary) specifically so the upcoming opening/response split can add two new `value`s that both resolve to `classification: "opening_game"` plus their own `extraFilter`, without inventing a second lookup shape later — deliberately not implemented yet, per the ask, just the shape it needs. `opening_game`'s label changed from lib/badges.ts's old "Opening Game" to "Opening" — a deliberate, small wording change anticipating that split (bare "Opening" reads as the natural umbrella term for both halves).
- Consolidated `lib/badges.ts`'s `classificationBadges` to derive its `label` (tooltip text) from this new mapper instead of hand-maintaining a second copy — kept its own 2-4 char `code` mapping separately (a Badge-display-only concern the label mapper has no reason to know about). Wired the mapper into every other place classification showed as raw DB text or populated a dropdown: `/mistakes` and `/repeated-positions`'s classification `<FilterSelect>` (added an optional `labelFor` prop, defaulting to the old raw-lowercased behavior, so category/severity dropdowns — which have no separate label mapper — are unaffected) and both pages' "N `<classification>` `<severity>` decisions/positions" description lines; `/repeated-positions`'s detail-view "N occurrences of this position (`<classification>`, `<severity>`)" line; and — found via a full grep sweep beyond the 3 sites named in the ask — `/matches/analysis`'s classification breakdown table, which was rendering the raw value as a table cell (guarded to only apply when `paramName === "classification"`, since the same table component is reused for the unrelated `category` breakdown too). `DecisionCard`/`DecisionListWithDetail`/`MistakesSection` needed no direct changes — they already render classification only via `ClassificationBadge`, which now inherits the fix through `lib/badges.ts`.
- `lib/badges.test.ts` still passes unchanged (asserts structure — key coverage, code-collision-freedom, color uniformity — never the exact label text, so the "Opening Game" → "Opening" wording change didn't need a test update). `ClassificationBadge` is a shared component covered by the visual regression suite, so re-ran `npm run test:visual`: label/tooltip text isn't rendered as visible pixels in a static screenshot (only shown on hover), and confirmed empirically — 4 consecutive runs, always converging to 12/12 overall (a different test flaking each time, always recovering on retry — the same pre-existing GPU-jitter noise already documented, not a systematic diff, which would fail the same test every time instead). `npm run lint`/`build` clean, `npm test` 21/21.
- What's next (superseded below): the migration + a real recompute still need to go to Oracle before `/repeated-positions` works there — still open, still needs explicit confirmation first. The opening/response split itself (using `classificationLabels.ts`'s new `extraFilter` shape) is the natural next step, not started. `/galaxy/matches`'s token persistence and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report are still open.
- Later same day: implemented the "Opening (first move)" vs "Opening (response)" split. Added `lib/constants.ts`'s `STARTING_POSITION_ID = "4HPwATDgc/ABMA"` (the confirmed standard starting position's GNU Position ID). Before wiring anything in, verified the split against live data: every `opening_game` `Decision` row's `sourcePositionId` (`reviews[0].source_position.formatted_value`) either matches `STARTING_POSITION_ID` exactly (**15,395 rows**) or doesn't (**30,985 rows**), summing to the classification's real total exactly (**46,380**, no gap, no overlap, 0 missing/null); sampled the top 10 non-matching values to confirm they're real, varied positions, not a garbage third case.
- Replaced `CLASSIFICATION_OPTIONS`' single `opening_game` entry with two — `opening_first_move` (`extraFilter: { sourcePositionId: STARTING_POSITION_ID }`) and `opening_response` (`extraFilter: { sourcePositionId: { not: STARTING_POSITION_ID } } }`) — both still pointing at the real `classification: "opening_game"` underneath.
- Caught and fixed a regression this split would otherwise have introduced: `getClassificationLabel()`/`lib/badges.ts`'s `classificationBadges` used to be keyed by `CLASSIFICATION_OPTIONS`' dropdown `value`, which no longer includes `"opening_game"` itself (only the two split values) — so any lookup by an actual row's raw classification (always literally `"opening_game"`, never a dropdown-only split value) would have silently fallen back to showing the raw string instead of "Opening", breaking `ClassificationBadge` everywhere, `/matches/analysis`'s breakdown table, and `/repeated-positions`'s detail-view text. Fixed by splitting the label source into two keyspaces: `CLASSIFICATION_LABELS_BY_RAW_VALUE` (17 real raw values, what a badge/display of an actual row uses) feeding both `getClassificationLabel()`'s fallback and `lib/badges.ts`'s `classificationBadges` directly, separate from `CLASSIFICATION_OPTIONS` (18 dropdown-only values, what a filter selection uses).
- Wired `extraFilter` into both query sites. `/repeated-positions` (real `sourcePositionId` column): resolves the classification URL param via `getClassificationOption()`, spreads the new `sourcePositionIdPrismaWhere()` helper straight into the Prisma `where` object alongside the base classification match — no raw SQL needed. `/mistakes` (Decision — `sourcePositionId` only lives inside the `raw` JSON blob, no real column): when the resolved option has an `extraFilter`, switched to a two-step approach mirroring `PositionDetailSection`'s existing pattern — a `FORCE INDEX`-hinted raw SQL query (same composite index, same reasoning as the 2026-09-26 fix) narrows to matching ids first (with its own `COUNT(*)` for the total and `LIMIT`/`OFFSET` for pagination, both ordered by `eventId DESC` to match the existing non-split behavior exactly), then a normal Prisma `findMany({ where: { id: { in: ids } } })` fetches full rows; the non-split 16 classifications keep the original single-query path unchanged. Both pages' classification `<select>` now render via a new `classificationOptionsFor(rawValuesPresent)` helper that expands `opening_game` into its two dropdown options whenever any `opening_game` rows exist, leaving every other classification 1:1.
- Hit a real Turbopack build break while wiring this up: `lib/classificationLabels.ts` originally imported the `Prisma` namespace value (for `Prisma.sql`/`Prisma.raw`, needed to build the JSON-path SQL condition) — but that file is also imported by `lib/badges.ts`, which `ClassificationBadge` (a client component) pulls in, and Prisma's runtime needs `node:module` (a Node built-in unavailable in a browser bundle). Confirmed by bisecting via `git stash` (baseline built fine) before making any fix. Resolved by moving the Prisma-dependent SQL helper (`sourcePositionIdSqlCondition`) into a new, deliberately separate, server-only module, `lib/classificationSql.ts`, imported only by `/mistakes/page.tsx` (the one query site that actually needs it) — keeping `lib/classificationLabels.ts` itself free of any Prisma import so it stays safe for `lib/badges.ts`/`ClassificationBadge` to import.
- End-to-end verification against a live dev server: `/repeated-positions?classification=opening_first_move&severity=blunder` returns exactly one row — the standard starting position `4HPwATDgc/ABMA` at **91×**, matching the investigation's known figure exactly; `/repeated-positions?classification=opening_response&severity=blunder` returns different, varied response positions and confirmed `4HPwATDgc/ABMA` appears zero times in that result set. `/mistakes?classification=opening_first_move` returns **15,356** decisions (a countAsDecision+rawError-not-null subset of the 15,395 total, as expected) and `/mistakes?classification=opening_response` returns **17,004** (same relationship to 30,985) — both non-zero, both properly paginated (1,536 pages at 10/page for the first-move count, matching exactly), confirming the raw-SQL-narrowed path actually returns real rows, not an empty/broken set.
- `npm run lint`/`build`/`tsc --noEmit` all clean, `npm test` 21/21 (including `lib/badges.test.ts`, unaffected by the raw-value-keyed rework since it only asserts structure, not exact labels). Visual regression suite not re-run this time — no shared component's rendered output changed (badge label/code values for real classification values are unchanged from before this split; only dropdown option lists and query filtering logic changed).
- What's next (superseded below): the migration + a real recompute still need to go to Oracle before `/repeated-positions` works there — still open, still needs explicit confirmation first. `/galaxy/matches`'s token persistence and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report are still open.
- Later same day: replaced the STARTING_POSITION_ID/opening-split approach entirely with a general, data-source-agnostic ply-number filter, per explicit instruction that the position-matching approach was Galaxy-specific and fragile. Added `Decision.plyNumber Int?` (migration `20260927080000_add_ply_number`, generated via the established `migrate diff --from-config-datasource` → hand-authored SQL → `migrate resolve --applied` pattern since the local DB user lacks shadow-DB privileges for `migrate dev`; local only, not deployed to Oracle) — ordinal position (1-4, null beyond) among a game's own `CHECKER` decisions ordered by `eventId` ascending. No collation concern (plain `Int`, not a string identifier). Mirrored the same column onto `RepeatedPosition` as an independent grouping dimension alongside `classification`/`errorSeverity` (unique constraint widened to `[sourcePositionId, errorSeverity, plyNumber]`) — reasoned that a real position within the first 4 plies always arises at the same fixed ply across every occurrence (it takes exactly that many prior checker decisions to reach it), so this refines existing groups rather than fragmenting them; confirmed empirically after recompute — the standard opening position still shows exactly **91** blunder occurrences, now at `plyNumber: 1`, unchanged from before the split.
- Ingest-time computation in `lib/ingest.ts`: a new `checkerPlyByEventId()` helper pre-scans a game's events once (before the main per-event loop) for ones eligible to become a `CHECKER` Decision row (has a review, resolves to `CHECKER` kind, `error_analysis` non-null — exactly what actually gets written further down), sorts by `eventId` ascending (events are NOT assumed to already arrive in that order — same caution the existing `playedAt` logic already takes), and maps the first 4 to plyNumber 1-4. Looked up per-event and stored as part of the same `decisionData` object each Decision row is written from — a single computation per game, not a running counter vulnerable to out-of-order events. Since `backfill.ts`/`incremental-sync.ts`/`/api/sync/incremental` all route through this same `ingestMatch()`, this covers all future ingestion with no per-script wiring. Added a dedicated `lib/ingest.test.ts` case with a deliberately shuffled, gapped event array (eventIds 50/10/90/20/30(null error_analysis)/40/70/60) confirming plyNumber lands on 10→1, 20→2, 40→3, 50→4, with 60/70/90 null and the null-error_analysis event (30) correctly consuming no ply slot at all.
- `lib/recompute-repeated-positions.ts`: added `plyNumber` to the raw SQL extraction (a real column now, no JSON extraction needed for it) and to the grouping key.
- `scripts/backfill-ply-number.ts` (new, one-time backfill for matches ingested before this ingest-time logic existed): deliberately deviates from `backfill-played-at.ts`'s own "re-read `Decision.raw`" pattern — reasoned that a stored `CHECKER` Decision row's mere existence already means its `error_analysis` was non-null at ingest time (a null one never gets a row at all, see "Events skipped via null error_analysis"), so `kind`/`eventId` (real, already-indexed columns) are a complete and equivalent source; re-parsing `raw` would be redundant work for zero benefit here, unlike `playedAt`'s backfill where the rule genuinely isn't captured by any existing column. One full-table extraction of all `kind = 'CHECKER'` decisions (581,204 rows) grouped in a JS `Map` by `gameId`, then 4 batched `updateMany` calls (one per target plyNumber value) rather than a per-game or per-row round trip — same "one full pass beats many small queries" lesson `recompute-repeated-positions.ts` already established. `--dry-run` support, before/after count reporting.
- Ran the dry run first, matching exactly on the live re-run: **581,204** `CHECKER` decisions found (**15,311** games); after backfill, **ply 1: 15,311**, **ply 2: 15,200**, **ply 3: 15,078**, **ply 4: 15,019**, **520,596** null (beyond ply 4 or n/a) — **60,608** changed, **520,596** already correct (all were already null, as expected for a brand-new column). Ply 1's count (15,311) roughly matches the old STARTING_POSITION_ID-matching figure (15,395, expected — different scope: this is a plain per-game count with no `countAsDecision`/`rawError` filter, that one was JSON-position-matched within `opening_game` classification specifically). **Ply 2 (15,200) is now a genuine ~1:1 ratio with ply 1 (15,311)** — directly resolving the old "response" bucket's unexplained ~2:1 count (30,985 vs. 15,395) predicted by the task; documented as a concrete before/after data point in `docs/field-mapping.md`'s new "Ply number" section rather than fully root-causing the old discrepancy, since the position-matching approach it came from no longer exists to investigate further. Ran the live backfill after the dry run matched expectations — actually applied.
- Removed the old approach entirely, not left alongside the new one: deleted `lib/constants.ts` (`STARTING_POSITION_ID`, its only export) and `lib/classificationSql.ts` (`sourcePositionIdSqlCondition`, its only export — both existed solely to support the removed split) — confirmed via a full grep sweep that nothing else referenced either symbol afterward. `lib/classificationLabels.ts` collapsed back to a flat `CLASSIFICATION_LABELS_BY_RAW_VALUE` map + `getClassificationLabel()`, dropping the `ClassificationOption`/`extraFilter`/`classificationOptionsFor`/`getClassificationOption`/`sourcePositionIdPrismaWhere` machinery entirely, since nothing needs a dropdown value to diverge from a raw classification value anymore. `lib/badges.ts` needed no logic change (already keyed off `CLASSIFICATION_LABELS_BY_RAW_VALUE`, not the removed `CLASSIFICATION_OPTIONS`), just a stale comment fix.
- `/mistakes` and `/repeated-positions` both reverted their classification `<select>` to the plain pre-split `FilterSelect` (raw distinct values + `labelFor={getClassificationLabel}`) and where-clause (`{classification}` directly, no option-resolution step) — and both gained a new, genuinely independent "Ply" dropdown (`1`/`2`/`3`/`4`/`Any`, hardcoded like `CATEGORY_PARAM_MAP`/`SEVERITY_PARAM_MAP` already are, since it's an application-defined fixed range, not an open-ended Galaxy taxonomy) filtering on `plyNumber` directly, spread into each page's `where` object alongside — not instead of — the classification/category/severity conditions already there, confirming the two dimensions compose via plain `AND` with no special-casing needed on either page.
- End-to-end verification against a live dev server: `/repeated-positions?ply=1&severity=blunder` → exactly one row, the standard opening position at **91×** (unchanged from the old split's own verification); `/repeated-positions?ply=2&severity=blunder` → 28 different positions, opening position absent. `/mistakes?ply=1` → 15,272 decisions, `/mistakes?ply=2` → 14,949 (a `countAsDecision`/`rawError`-filtered subset of the Decision-table-wide 15,311/15,200 — same near-1:1 ratio holds). Combined filter check: `/mistakes?ply=1&classification=opening_game&severity=blunder` → **91** decisions, exactly matching the long-established opening-position blunder count, confirming ply and classification compose correctly together.
- Proved future-sync correctness with a real test, not just code inspection, per explicit instruction: triggered a real `scripts/incremental-sync.ts` run against live Galaxy (the standalone backfill script was run earlier, only against the then-existing 4,287 matches — never re-run afterward) — discovered and ingested **11** genuinely new matches (37 games, 1,618 `CHECKER` decisions) that didn't exist locally before this run. Queried their decisions directly: every single game's plyNumber sequence is exactly `1, 2, 3, 4, null, null, ...` (or shorter, correctly truncated, for games with fewer than 4 checker decisions) — **0 problems** across all 37 games, **157** decisions correctly non-null. This is real, live-ingested data the backfill script never touched, so it demonstrates the ingest-time code path itself, not the backfill.
- `npm run lint`/`build`/`tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm test` 22/22 (21 prior + 1 new plyNumber-ordering case). `npm run test:visual`: 11 passed, 1 flaky-then-recovered (the same pre-existing GPU-rasterization jitter documented since 2026-09-26, absorbed by `retries: 2` — confirmed not a real diff) — `ClassificationBadge`'s rendered output is unchanged (same labels/codes for real classification values), and the new "Ply" dropdown only touches each page's filter form, outside the `boardPanel` locator the suite screenshots.
- Documented the reasoning in `docs/field-mapping.md`'s new "Ply number" section: why ordinal ply is portable to any future data source (including the `.xgp`/`.xg` import idea on the roadmap) where a Galaxy-specific position-ID constant has no equivalent, plus the ply-1/ply-2 ratio data point above as concrete evidence the switch resolved a real, previously-unexplained discrepancy.
- What's next (superseded below): the `plyNumber` migration + a real recompute still need to go to Oracle before this ships there — not done, needs explicit confirmation first, same standing pattern as the still-pending `RepeatedPosition` migration from 2026-09-26. `/galaxy/matches`'s token persistence and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report are still open.

## 2026-09-28
- Merged `/mistakes`'/`/repeated-positions`' separate Classification and Ply dropdowns into one "Phase" dropdown, per explicit instruction. Added `PhaseOption`/`FIXED_PHASE_OPTIONS`/`phaseOptionsFor()`/`resolvePhaseWhere()`/`getPhaseLabel()` to `lib/classificationLabels.ts` (kept there rather than a new file — no Prisma dependency needed this time, since both `classification` and `plyNumber` are plain columns on both `Decision` and `RepeatedPosition`, unlike the old JSON-extracted `sourcePositionId` case that forced `lib/classificationSql.ts` to exist as a separate server-only module). 5 fixed options in order — Opening (both plies) → `{classification: "opening_game"}`, 1st roll → `{plyNumber: 1}`, 1st roll – response → `{plyNumber: 2}`, 2nd roll → `{plyNumber: 3}`, 2nd roll – response → `{plyNumber: 4}` — followed by every other classification actually present, sourced dynamically from the same live `MistakeStat`/`RepeatedPosition` distinct-values query both pages already ran (not hardcoded), labeled via the existing `getClassificationLabel()`. `resolvePhaseWhere()` treats anything outside the 5 fixed values as a raw classification match directly, with no validation against a known list — exactly how the plain `classification` param already behaved before this merge.
- Before writing the 4 ply labels, checked the mover-alternation assumption they imply (ply 1/3 = one player's turns, ply 2/4 = the other's responses) against every game with ply data: **14,928 of 15,351 games (97.2%) alternate strictly across all 4 plies; 423 (2.8%) don't.** Inspected the violation patterns directly — the color sequence breaks are concentrated at the ply2→ply3 boundary (189 games) and ply3→ply4 boundary (232 games), with only 2 at ply1→ply2, consistent with a forced-pass/dance turn somewhere consuming what would otherwise be the other player's ply slot and shifting everything after it by one player; not investigated further since a dance's own event shape wasn't part of this task. Documented in `lib/classificationLabels.ts`'s own comment: the "response" wording is a reliable convenience label (~97%), not a hard guarantee — `plyNumber` itself (an objective ordinal count) is correct regardless either way.
- Single URL param (`phase`) replaces the separate `classification`/`ply` params entirely on both pages — `ply` and its param handling removed. Old-style `?classification=<value>&severity=<severity>` links from `/matches/analysis` (unchanged, not touched) keep working via an explicit alias: both pages read `sp.phase`, falling back to `sp.classification` only when `phase` itself is absent — chosen over updating `/matches/analysis`'s link generation since that would only fix internally-generated links, not any already-bookmarked/shared old-style URL. Verified: `?classification=opening_game&severity=blunder` on `/repeated-positions` still resolves to the "Opening (both plies)" bucket (29 positions, standard opening still at 91×); `?classification=middle_game&severity=blunder` on `/mistakes` still resolves to 24,307 decisions labeled "Middle Game blunder" — identical to pre-merge behavior.
- No schema/data changes, as instructed — purely a UI/query-building consolidation on top of the already-existing `Decision.plyNumber`/`RepeatedPosition.plyNumber` columns from 2026-09-27.
- Verified overlap is preserved, not deduped: `/repeated-positions?phase=ply_1&severity=blunder` → still exactly the standard opening position at **91×** (unchanged from every prior verification of this figure); `?phase=ply_2&severity=blunder` → 28 different positions, standard opening absent, confirming "1st roll" and "1st roll – response" remain mutually exclusive by construction (different plyNumber values) while both legitimately overlap with "Middle Game"/other classifications at the data level, exactly as expected.
- `npm run lint`/`build`/`tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm test` 22/22 (unaffected — no ply/classification-label test asserted the old two-dropdown shape). `npm run test:visual`: 12/12 clean, no flake this run — neither `ClassificationBadge` nor any other shared component's rendering logic changed (only each page's own filter form markup and where-clause construction), so this was a confidence check rather than a required one per the shared-component-touched rule, but run anyway.

- Investigated the slow one rather than just reporting the number: `EXPLAIN` on the underlying query (`countAsDecision = 1 AND rawError IS NOT NULL AND plyNumber = 2`) showed a full table scan over ~1.02M rows — no index on `Decision` covers `plyNumber` at all (the existing composite index covers `countAsDecision, rawError, kind, classification, errorSeverity`, none of which help narrow a ply-only filter). This is a real, measurable finding, not investigated further and **not fixed** — adding an index would be an application/schema change, and this task was explicitly scoped to data migration only ("nothing here changes application code"). Flagged as a candidate follow-up: a `plyNumber` index (or a composite one including it) if ply-only filtering on `/mistakes` turns out to be a common access pattern.

- Later same day: fixed the flagged `plyNumber` index gap. Measured before touching anything — local `/mistakes` query shapes (`?phase=ply_1`, `?phase=ply_2`, plus `&severity=blunder`/`&category=checker` combinations), matching `DecisionListSection`'s real `where`/`orderBy`/pagination exactly (count + findMany, page 1, pageSize 10): all 5 a full table scan, **10.7s-43.4s** locally. Same 5 shapes on Oracle (via `bg_db_ro` only, per instruction — local and Oracle have differed enough in this project before that neither is trusted alone): **1.26s-2.58s** — much faster than local (consistent with the already-documented local-Docker-I/O-vs-Oracle-HeatWave gap), but still a real full scan, still worth fixing.
- Compared a wide vs. narrow composite index with `EXPLAIN` before choosing, exactly as instructed, rather than guessing: `(plyNumber, countAsDecision, kind, errorSeverity, eventId)` (wide) vs. `(plyNumber, countAsDecision, eventId)` (narrow), both added temporarily via raw SQL on local, `FORCE INDEX`-tested against each of the 5 query shapes. Wide wins only for the single fully-specified case (severity *and* category both given — an exact index lookup to ~500 rows); everywhere else, `kind`/`errorSeverity` sitting between `countAsDecision` and `eventId` breaks the index's ability to satisfy `ORDER BY eventId DESC` directly once either is left unfiltered, forcing an in-memory sort — measured **500-950ms** on wide for the 3 of 5 realistic combinations missing `errorSeverity`. Narrow stayed **1-12ms** on every combination, since `eventId` sits immediately after the two conditions `/mistakes` *always* applies (`plyNumber`, `countAsDecision`), preserving index order regardless of what else is or isn't filtered — residual `kind`/`errorSeverity`/`rawError` checks stay cheap because `plyNumber` alone already narrows ~1M rows to ~15-30k. Chose narrow. Full reasoning recorded in `prisma/schema.prisma`'s own comment on the index and `docs/field-mapping.md`'s "Ply number" section.
- Added `@@index([plyNumber, countAsDecision, eventId])` (migration `20260928120000_add_decision_ply_number_index`, generated via the established `migrate diff --from-config-datasource` pattern). Applied locally first: `EXPLAIN` confirmed the plan changed from a full table scan to a reverse index range scan on the new index for all 5 shapes, no `FORCE INDEX` needed — the optimizer picked it correctly on its own every time (also verified it correctly falls back to the *wide* candidate specifically for the one case where wide was faster, when both temporary indexes existed side by side — the optimizer picked right in every case tested, so no hint was needed anywhere; nothing to report on that front this time, unlike the drill-down query's earlier `FORCE INDEX` story). Local timings dropped to **447-527ms** (20-97x faster) — `COUNT(*)`'s own inherent need to scan every matching row, now bounded to ~15-28k instead of ~1M, is the remaining cost; the paginated `findMany` itself dropped to single-digit milliseconds.
- Before deploying anywhere, confirmed no collateral damage locally: `recomputeMistakeStats` (548ms) and `recomputeRepeatedPositions` (27.9s — consistent with this project's already-documented local-I/O-specific slowness for this exact query, not a new regression) both still use their own existing indexes, `EXPLAIN`-confirmed; `PositionDetailSection`'s `FORCE INDEX` query is naturally unaffected (a hint pins the choice regardless of what else exists). `npm run lint`/`build`/`tsc --noEmit`/`npm test` (22/22) all clean.

Oracle timings for the same 5 query shapes dropped to **118-149ms** (10-21x faster), same reverse-index-scan plan in every case, `EXPLAIN`-confirmed. - Later same day: prepared the app for a Vercel deployment as a read-only viewer, plus a CI pipeline. No data changed, Oracle/Vercel settings untouched (per explicit instruction) — every Oracle-related claim below was verified against a local MySQL copy or by static/build-time inspection, never a live Oracle write.
- **Audit finding 1 (real bug, fixed)**: `lib/prisma.ts` built both `prisma` and `prismaReadOnly` eagerly at module-top-level (`export const prisma = ... ?? createClient(...)`), which threw the instant `DATABASE_URL` was unset — including for files that only ever use `prismaReadOnly` (`lib/local-client.ts`, `app/api/player-identities`, every read-only page), since ES module evaluation runs the whole file regardless of which export the importer uses. Production/preview only ever sets `DATABASE_URL_READONLY`, so this would have crashed the entire app. Fixed with a lazy `Proxy` (`createLazyClient`): importing the module, or even importing `prisma` without calling a method on it, no longer throws; actually calling a method on an unconfigured client still throws immediately and loudly, same message as before. Verified both halves directly with a throwaway script (`DATABASE_URL` unset): import succeeded, `prisma.match.count()` threw `"DATABASE_URL is not set — see .env.example."` — exactly as designed, not silently swallowed.
- **Audit finding 2 (already correct, no fix needed)**: the generated Prisma client (`lib/generated/prisma`, gitignored) already regenerates via the existing `postinstall: "prisma generate"` script. Confirmed with a genuine from-scratch test, not assumed: copied the working tree (including this session's edits) into a scratch directory with `node_modules`, `lib/generated`, and every `.env*` file removed, then ran `npm install && npm run build` — succeeded cleanly, `postinstall` regenerated the client, and — critically — the build needed zero DATABASE_URL/DATABASE_URL_READONLY/GALAXY_TOKEN anywhere (no `.env` file, no shell env vars), confirming both this item and the CI requirement (item 4) at once.
- **Audit finding 3 (real risk, defensively fixed)**: `certs/oracle-mysql-ca.pem` is read off disk at runtime via `fs.readFileSync(path.join(process.cwd(), "certs", "oracle-mysql-ca.pem"))`. Checked, not assumed, whether Vercel's file tracing would bundle it: inspected `.next/server/app/**/*.nft.json` after a real build and found the cert **already correctly traced** into every route that imports `lib/prisma.ts` (status/mistakes/repeated-positions/matches-analysis pages and every DB-touching API route), and correctly absent from client-shell pages that don't need it (`/matches`, `/matches/[matchId]`) — `@vercel/nft` resolves `path.join(process.cwd(), ...)` statically on its own. Added `outputFileTracingIncludes: { "/*": ["./certs/oracle-mysql-ca.pem"] }` to `next.config.ts` anyway as an explicit, non-heuristic guarantee — the failure mode (nothing works against Oracle) is total and silent if a future refactor ever makes the path less statically analyzable. Verified the explicit declaration works too: re-checked the trace and confirmed the cert now appears even in `/matches`'s own trace (previously absent), proving the config key is real and applied broadly as intended.
- **Audit finding 4 (already correct, no fix needed)**: `next.config.ts`'s `NEXT_DIST_DIR` override already falls back safely (`process.env.NEXT_DIST_DIR || ".next"`) and is only ever set by `playwright.config.ts`'s dedicated test server — normal dev, `next build`, and Vercel all get the untouched default. No change made.
- **Audit finding 5 (real risk, fixed — judgment call, reported here as instructed)**: the mariadb driver's own default pool size (`connectionLimit`, unset anywhere in this codebase) is **10 connections per pool** (confirmed by reading `node_modules/mariadb/lib/config/pool-options.js` directly, not assumed) — with two pools per process (`prisma`, `prismaReadOnly`) times however many concurrent serverless instances Vercel spins up, this could multiply well past Oracle HeatWave's Always Free connection ceiling. Set `connectionLimit: 3` in `buildConnectionConfig`. Judgment call, disclosed: applied it unconditionally (both the Oracle/SSL branch and plain local-dev connections), not scoped only to the `ssl=true` branch — chose this for one code path instead of an environment-conditional one, verified safe by grepping the codebase for any concurrent-query pattern that would suffer (only two small `Promise.all`s exist: `/status`'s own 5-way one and `local-client.ts`'s 2-way one; the mariadb driver queues excess concurrent queries against a full pool rather than failing them, so this is a latency tradeoff, not a correctness one). No specific Oracle Always Free connection cap number is documented anywhere in this repo or confirmed against the Oracle console in this task (out of scope — no Oracle settings touched) — flagged as unverified precisely, not asserted as a fact.
- **Galaxy gate**: added `lib/galaxyGate.ts`'s `isGalaxyEnabled()` (`Boolean(DATABASE_URL) && READ_ONLY_MODE !== "true"`, fails closed) as the single source of truth, read in exactly two places: `proxy.ts` (project root — this Next.js version deprecated `middleware.ts` in favor of `proxy.ts`, confirmed via `node_modules/next/dist/docs`, same mechanism/renamed) with a `matcher` covering `/galaxy/matches`, `/galaxy/matches/:path*`, `/api/galaxy/:path*`, `/api/sync/:path*`, `/api/matches/check-existence` — returns a plain 404 for all of them when the gate is closed — and `app/page.tsx`, which now conditionally hides the "Fetch live from Galaxy" home-page link on the same check. Grepped the whole `app/` tree first to confirm this was the only such link (it was) and that nothing else touches the write `prisma` client or a Galaxy token outside `lib/`/`scripts/` (local-only) and this exact gated set of routes — confirmed via `grep` for every import of the write `prisma` client, `lib/galaxy-client.ts`, `lib/ingest.ts`, and `lib/sync.ts` across `app/`, `lib/`, and `scripts/`.
- Production simulation, both required configurations, run against a real local MySQL copy (never Oracle) via `next build && next start` in a scratch checkout: **configuration 1** (`DATABASE_URL_READONLY` + `READ_ONLY_MODE=true` only, `DATABASE_URL` unset) — every gated path returned 404 (`/galaxy/matches`, `/galaxy/matches/:id`, `/api/matches/check-existence`, all 4 `/api/galaxy/**`+`/api/sync/**` POST routes), the home page had no Galaxy link, and all 5 read-only pages plus `/status` returned 200 with real data (`/status` showed live match/connection-OK output, proving the lazy read-only client genuinely connects and queries with only the read-only URL set). **Configuration 2** (both URLs set, `READ_ONLY_MODE` unset) — Galaxy pages returned 200, the home page showed the Galaxy link, `check-existence` returned real JSON, and all read-only pages still worked. One real nuance surfaced and correctly diagnosed rather than glossed over: `/` is statically prerendered (`next build`'s own output marks it `○ Static`), so `isGalaxyEnabled()`'s home-page link visibility is fixed at *build* time, not evaluated per request — the first config-2 test (reusing configuration 1's build output, only restarting with new runtime env vars) incorrectly showed no link; rebuilding with configuration 2's env vars present at build time fixed it. This matches how Vercel actually works (each environment gets its own build with its own env vars), so it's not a real-world bug, but it means flipping `READ_ONLY_MODE` on Vercel without a fresh deploy won't change the visible link until the next build — the actual security boundary (`proxy.ts`) is unaffected by this, since it's a genuinely dynamic route re-evaluated every request, confirmed by the same test correctly reflecting the new runtime env immediately without a rebuild.
- `npm run lint` / `tsc --noEmit` / `npm test` (22/22) / `npm run test:visual` (**17 tests, 18 baselines, unchanged** — `git status` on the snapshots directory shows no diff; 1 flaky this run, the already-diagnosed pre-existing GPU-contention jitter, unrelated) all clean on the real repo. The Playwright suite's own `/galaxy/matches` fixtures, `.env.test` server, and `bg_test` all continued to work unchanged, confirmed by this same clean run — `.env.test` sets `DATABASE_URL` with no `READ_ONLY_MODE`, so `isGalaxyEnabled()` is `true` there and the gate never engages for the test server.
- Added `vercel.json` (`regions: ["syd1"]`) — confirmed valid on the Hobby plan by fetching Vercel's own current docs before writing it, not from memory: Hobby supports exactly one function region (Pro supports up to 5), and `syd1` is a real, currently-listed region code. Added nothing else to the file (no `functionFailoverRegions`, which is Enterprise-only anyway).
- Added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (triggers: PRs and pushes to **`master`**, this repo's actual default branch — the task said "main", substituted the real branch name, flagged here rather than silently guessed). Runs `npm ci`, lint, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm test`, `npm run build`, deliberately with **no DB env vars set at all** (not even a dummy value) — turning the build step itself into an ongoing regression test for finding 1 above. Does not run `npm run test:visual`, per instruction.
- Added `docs/deploy.md`: required env vars (only `DATABASE_URL_READONLY` + `READ_ONLY_MODE=true`, never `DATABASE_URL`, on Vercel), the CA-cert/region reasoning above, how to lift the Galaxy gate (a pure env-var change on Vercel — set `DATABASE_URL` again and remove `READ_ONLY_MODE`, no code edit needed, since both `proxy.ts` and `app/page.tsx` already read `isGalaxyEnabled()` as their only source of truth), that syncing stays local-only for now, and the manual steps only the user can do.
- **Flagged for the user's decision, not resolved here** (out of scope — "don't touch Oracle... settings"): Oracle HeatWave's port-3306 firewall is currently scoped to the user's home IP only, and Vercel Hobby functions have no fixed, allowlist-able outbound IP range — so as deployed today, Vercel's functions cannot reach Oracle at all until either the ingress rule is opened to `0.0.0.0/0` (relying on TLS + the scoped read-only credential for security) or Vercel Pro's Secure Compute is used for a static IP. Documented both options with their trade-offs in `docs/deploy.md`, decision left to the user.
- **Also flagged**: Vercel does not put a login screen in front of a deployed app by default — `docs/deploy.md`'s "Auth verification" section instructs opening the production URL in a private window with no Vercel session after every deploy; if it loads, the site is genuinely public (read-only, since the Galaxy gate blocks writes regardless, but still exposes synced match data) and needs Vercel's Deployment Protection or an app-level gate, neither of which was set up here (a Vercel-settings change, explicitly out of scope).
- Changed files: `lib/prisma.ts`, `next.config.ts`, `app/page.tsx` (modified); `lib/galaxyGate.ts`, `proxy.ts`, `vercel.json`, `docs/deploy.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new). Nothing committed, per instruction.
- What's next (superseded below): the two flagged decisions above (Oracle ingress, production auth) block an actual working deployment and need the user's input before `vercel link`/first deploy. Otherwise unchanged: `/galaxy/matches`'s token persistence and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report remain open.

## 2026-09-29
- Renamed the Galaxy-gate flag `READ_ONLY_MODE` → `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE` and flipped its polarity: previously the safe state required explicitly setting `READ_ONLY_MODE=true`; now the safe state (writes disabled) is the *default* with zero env vars set, and enabling writes requires explicitly setting `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE=true`. `isGalaxyEnabled()` (kept the same name, per the task's "or its replacement" allowance) still requires both conditions independently — `DATABASE_URL` present is still load-bearing on its own, the flag alone was never sufficient before and still isn't: `Boolean(process.env.DATABASE_URL) && process.env.ENABLE_WRITE_MODE === "true"`.
- Every read site renamed: `lib/galaxyGate.ts` (the actual check, plus its explaining comment), `docs/deploy.md` (env var table, the `isGalaxyEnabled()` code block and its "fails closed" explanation, the "Lifting the gate" steps — which now flip from "remove a var" to "add a var", since enabling is opt-in rather than disabling being opt-in — and the "manual steps" section), `app/status/notes.ts`'s one line. Grepped the whole repo (`.ts`/`.tsx`/`.md`/`.yml`/`.json`/`.example`) for `READ_ONLY_MODE` afterward — zero remaining hits outside this file's own historical entries above (deliberately left those alone, per the append-only-log convention — PROGRESS.md records what was true *at the time*, not retroactively edited). `vercel.json`/`.github/workflows/ci.yml` never referenced either name (confirmed, not assumed) — no changes needed there.
- Picked and documented one convention, as instructed: local dev (`.env`, `.env.test`) sets `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE=true` explicitly, since it's genuinely a write-enabled environment; Vercel sets nothing at all (not an explicit `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE=false`) — absence already means disabled, and one fewer var to keep in sync. Documented this explicitly in `docs/deploy.md` and `.env.example` (which gets a new `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE=true` entry with its own explanatory comment — it never had `READ_ONLY_MODE` documented there in the first place, so this is a net-new addition, not a rename, in that one file).
- Caught before it became a real regression: the real local `.env` and `.env.test` (both gitignored, edited directly, never committed) needed `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE=true` added too, or local dev and the Playwright suite's own `/galaxy/matches` tests would have silently broken under the new default-off polarity (previously `READ_ONLY_MODE` unset was already the "gate open" state locally, so nothing needed to be set; now something does). Added it to both with a short comment explaining why.

Also spot-checked `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE=false` explicitly (not just fully-unset) against the same build without rebuilding, since the gate itself is a genuinely dynamic route re-evaluated every request — also 404, confirming "not `\"true\"`" is handled correctly for an explicit wrong value, not just absence.

- Later same day: added automated LLM PR review via `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1`, with severity-based merge blocking. Two new workflow files only — `ci.yml` untouched, confirmed via `git status` showing no modification to it.
- Looked up the current documented setup at code.claude.com/docs/en/github-actions (fetched live) before writing anything, per instruction, and followed it where it differed from the task's own suggested shape: the docs' own worked review-workflow example uses `contents: read, pull-requests: read, issues: read, id-token: write` (no `pull-requests: write`) — narrower than the task's suggested "pull-requests: write" and narrower than one of the action repo's own example workflows (`examples/pr-review-comprehensive.yml`, which does grant `pull-requests: write`). Used the docs page's narrower version deliberately (PR comments post through the Claude GitHub App's own separately-scoped token when `github_token` is omitted, not this workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN` — so the workflow-level permission doesn't need write access for that to work), flagging the discrepancy rather than silently picking one.
- Pinned `anthropics/claude-code-action` to a real, verified commit SHA (`8ce9314fa9a404564fa7e954cd84f25bcba2b829`) rather than the floating `@v1` tag — cross-checked two independent ways (`git ls-remote --tags` and the GitHub API's tag-object resolution), both agreeing this is exactly the commit the `v1.0.236` release (published 2026-09-28, the latest release at verification time) points to.
- `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml`: triggers on `pull_request` only, `types: [opened, ready_for_review]` — no `synchronize`, no `reopened`, matching the explicit instruction that re-review-after-a-fix is the separate on-demand `claude.yml` workflow, not an automatic rerun on every push. A direct commit to `master` structurally cannot fire this: `pull_request`/`pull_request_target` are the only events that can, and pushes emit neither — confirmed as a property of the event model itself, not just asserted. Added a job-level `if: github.event.pull_request.draft == false` guard beyond the trigger types alone, since `opened` fires even for a PR created directly as a draft (only `ready_for_review` structurally guarantees non-draft). `concurrency: group: claude-review-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}, cancel-in-progress: true`. No `pull_request_target`, no elevated checkout — `actions/checkout@v6` (matching the docs' own pinned major version) with default (same-repo PR head) ref and `fetch-depth: 1`; on a public repo, GitHub withholds secrets from fork-PR runs entirely, so a fork PR's review step fails closed (no API key available) rather than running with any elevated privilege.
- Structured output: used the action's own first-class `--json-schema`/`structured_output` mechanism (verified real via the action repo's `docs/usage.md` and `examples/test-failure-analysis.yml`, not invented) rather than instructing Claude to hand-write a JSON file — the schema requires `findings: [{reason, severity (low/medium/high enum), confidence (low/medium/high enum), possible_solution}]` per finding, with `possible_solution` explicitly scoped in both the schema description and the prompt to "describe the fix in words — never a code diff or patch." The same prompt also grants `mcp__github_inline_comment__create_inline_comment` (real tool name, confirmed via the action repo's own example workflows) plus scoped `Bash(gh pr comment:*),Bash(gh pr diff:*),Bash(gh pr view:*)` so Claude posts real human-readable inline comments per finding and one top-level summary comment, in addition to returning the same findings as validated structured output.
- Severity-to-outcome mapping decided and written directly into the prompt (not left as "the AI decides"), covering every failure class from the task's list, each with a stated typical severity and the reasoning: secrets/credentials in the diff → always high; Galaxy-payload assumption violations (metadata.scores nullability, metadata.timestamp, count_as_decision, source-vs-destination-position, non-exhaustive analysed_event handling) → high if it could crash ingest/sync or silently miscount a graded decision, medium if defensive-only; unindexed Decision queries → medium by default, high specifically for the plyNumber/countAsDecision/classification/errorSeverity/kind access pattern this project has already measured as a full-table-scan at its current scale; credential-scoping violations (write client where read-only would do, anything weakening isGalaxyEnabled()'s fail-closed `&&`) → always high; missing collation pin on a new externally-sourced string ID column → always high (silent, hard-to-detect-later data integrity bug); inline Tailwind literals → low (this project's own convention explicitly allows gradual adoption); unexplained visual-baseline PNG changes → medium by default, high only if nothing in the same PR's diff could plausibly explain a rendering change at all; server-only imports leaking into a client bundle → always high.
- Blocking logic (item 3): a real `jq`-based parsing step after the review step, not a vague AI decision. Exact rule, implemented and shown verbatim in both workflow files: **fail the job iff at least one finding has `severity == "high"` AND `confidence != "low"`.** Everything else (any low/medium severity regardless of confidence, or any severity at low confidence) already got posted as a PR comment by Claude and just passes here. A missing or malformed `structured_output` also fails the job (with a distinct error message) rather than silently passing unreviewed code — deliberate fail-safe design, not an oversight.
- `.github/workflows/claude.yml`: on-demand re-review via `@claude` mention, for after a fix is pushed. Comment-based triggers only (`issue_comment`, `pull_request_review_comment`, `pull_request_review`), matching the real official `claude.yml` example's shape but narrowed: dropped the bare `issues:` trigger entirely and added an explicit `github.event.issue.pull_request != null` guard on the `issue_comment` branch, since this workflow's scope (per the task) is re-reviewing a *PR*, not general issue assistance — same reasoning, none of these fire from a plain push either. Deliberately reused `claude-review.yml`'s narrower, read-only permission set (`contents: read, pull-requests: read, issues: read, id-token: write`) instead of the docs' own broader general-purpose `@claude` example (`contents: write, pull-requests: write, issues: write`) — flagged as a real, disclosed deviation from the docs' generic example, justified because this workflow's job is strictly re-review (same prompt/schema/blocking logic as claude-review.yml, literally duplicated rather than factored into a reusable workflow, since there's no existing reusable-workflow infrastructure in this repo to extend for one caller), never "let Claude push a commit."
- Documented the manual branch-protection step in `docs/deploy.md` (new section, "Making the Claude PR review actually block merges"): a failing job doesn't block a merge on its own — GitHub only enforces that once the `review` job from `claude-review.yml` is added as a required status check under Settings → Branches → the `master` protection rule, which can't be done from a workflow file. Also noted explicitly that `claude.yml` should *not* be added as a required check, since it only runs on-demand (an @claude mention), not on every PR — requiring it would block every PR nobody happens to mention it on.
- Verification: `actionlint` (installed via `brew install actionlint`, real GitHub-Actions-semantics validator — checks expression syntax, permission keys, trigger types, and runs shellcheck against embedded `run:` blocks) — **zero findings, exit 0**, against all three workflow files including the untouched `ci.yml`. Also validated YAML syntax directly with `js-yaml` (already a project dependency) and ran `shellcheck` standalone against the extracted blocking-logic script — clean.
- Simulated the parsing/blocking logic against the real extracted script (not a re-implementation) with 7 fake `structured_output` payloads, no real PR needed: **(1) one high/high-confidence finding → exit 1 (fail), as required.** **(2) medium+low findings only, no high at all → exit 0 (pass), as required.** **(3) one high-severity/low-confidence finding → exit 0 (pass, comments-only), as required** — the exact three the task asked for. Plus 4 extra edge cases for rigor: mixed findings (one blocking high alongside a low) → exit 1 (any qualifying high blocks regardless of what else is present); empty findings array → exit 0; missing `structured_output` entirely → exit 1 (fail-safe, not a silent pass); malformed JSON missing the `findings` key → exit 1. All 7 behaved exactly as designed.

## 2026-09-30
- Added a full game replay: `/matches/[matchId]/replay/[gameIndex]`, stepping through every Decision row for a game in order — a true, unfiltered reconstruction (forced moves and `countAsDecision: false` cube-checks included), unlike `/mistakes`' aggressive filtering. Reuses `BoardPanel` (unmodified), `MoveDelta`/`DiceRoll` (unmodified) for notation/roll display, and the list+detail interaction pattern `DecisionListWithDetail.tsx` already proved (client-side selection, board+list side-by-side) — applied to a complete sequence instead of a ranked mistake set.
- New server component (`page.tsx`, `prismaReadOnly`, same read-only pattern as `/mistakes`/`/repeated-positions`) resolves `Match` by `(source, sourceMatchId)`, then `Game` by `(matchId, gameIndex)`, fetches every `Decision` row for that `gameId` ordered by `eventId` ascending (no `countAsDecision`/`rawError` filter at all), and computes the full sorted game-index list for the match to derive prev/next game boundaries. A new client component (`GameReplay.tsx`) owns the interactive state: selected index, my/best move tab, a position counter ("Move N of M"), Next/Previous buttons, and a `window`-level keydown listener for ArrowLeft/ArrowRight.
- Real gap found and fixed in `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`: the existing `decisionFromRow()` helper (already used by `/repeated-positions`) silently returns `null` for any row with `rawError === null` — correct for its own mistake-focused callers, but would have silently dropped exactly the "ungraded/partial-analysis" rows a true replay needs to show. Added `decisionFromRowForReplay()` alongside it (same file, reusing the same `actionLabels`/`moveNotations`/`severityFor`/`KIND_MAP` internals, `KIND_MAP`/`severityFor` promoted from private to exported for this reuse) — identical to `decisionFromRow` except it never skips on a null `rawError` (defaults `absError` to 0 instead), so every ingested row for the game renders. Confirmed via `lib/ingest.ts` directly (not assumed) that every stored `Decision` row already has a non-null `error_analysis` at ingest time — only truly outcome-logging events with nothing to grade are skipped before a row is ever created — so "every row for a gameId" is already the correct, complete reconstruction; nothing needed re-fetching from Galaxy.
- Game-boundary navigation: pressing Next/Previous *at* the last/first decision of a game — or the equivalent arrow key — crosses directly into the adjacent game (landing on its first decision going forward, or its **last** decision going backward, via a `?position=last` query param the server component reads to pick the initial selected index) rather than dead-stopping, so keyboard-driven fast browsing stays continuous across a whole match. A separate, clearly-labeled "Next game →"/"← Previous game" link also appears at the boundary alongside the Prev/Next buttons, so crossing into a different game is still visually explicit rather than a silent counter reset. Both buttons disable only at the true start/end of the whole match (no adjacent game at all).
- Reused `MoveDelta` verbatim for the replay list's per-row notation, including its existing severity→color logic — checked first (not assumed) whether extending it to give clean/ungraded (`severity: null`) decisions a distinct neutral color would be a safe, isolated improvement: it would not, since `/mistakes`' own query only filters `rawError: { not: null }` (not `errorSeverity !== NONE`), so a zero-error clean decision already reaches `MoveDelta` there today and already renders amber under the current logic — changing that ternary would be a real, live behavior change to `/mistakes`, not a no-op. Left `MoveDelta`/`MoveDelta.styles.ts` untouched; the replay page's own clean/ungraded rows render with the same established (if amber-leaning) convention the rest of the app already uses, not a new inconsistency.
- Entry points, both wired: `/matches`' row table gets a new "Replay" column (`Link` to `.../replay/1`, `stopPropagation` on click so it doesn't also trigger the row's own whole-row navigation — same pattern `MoveDelta`'s own tab-click spans already use for the identical problem); `/matches/[matchId]` gets a new "Replay: Game 1 · Game 2 · ..." row once its client-side game-by-game fetch has loaded at least one game, added directly in the page's own header block rather than inside the shared `MistakesSection` component (kept page-local, didn't touch a component used by 3 other pages for a page-specific need).
- New page-local `gameReplay.styles.ts`, following the styling convention exactly — mirrors `DecisionListWithDetail.styles.ts`'s list/board-split key names where the shape is identical, plus new keys for the nav row, position counter, and game-boundary links. Grepped all touched/new files for raw `className="..."` — zero matches.
- Visual regression decision: **deferred, not added as a 4th BoardPanel screenshot context.** Reasoning: `BoardPanel` itself is completely unmodified by this task — its rendering fidelity is already exhaustively covered by the 3 existing real call sites (`/matches/[matchId]`, `/galaxy/matches/[matchId]`, `/mistakes`), so a future regression there would already be caught before ever reaching a 4th context. What's genuinely new here (the replay page's own nav chrome — counter, buttons, list, boundary links) falls outside what the board-only, testid-clipped screenshots even capture, and matches this project's own already-established precedent (2026-09-28's inventory entry: page-local chrome on an otherwise-uncovered route gets a manual check, not a new `page.goto()` screenshot, since the blast radius of a missed regression is real but contained to one page and the lift is disproportionate) — satisfied here via the thorough manual/Playwright-driven verification below instead.
- Verified against real local data (match `32699544`: 4 games, game 1 has 85 decisions including 35 `countAsDecision: false` and 17 `rawError: null` — deliberately picked via a throwaway DB-query script to cover every edge case at once, not a convenient easy case): loaded game 1 at "Move 1 of 85" with exactly 85 list rows (nothing skipped); Next-button clicks, ArrowRight/ArrowLeft, and direct row clicks all moved the counter/selection correctly and in order; the last decision (a `passed` cube call) showed the "Next game →" link and rendered correctly with no board arrows (a cube decision, not a checker move); a `did not double roll` `countAsDecision: false` cube-check decision (screenshotted directly) rendered sensibly — dice, static "did not double roll"/"roll" labels, no crash; pressing Next at the boundary crossed live into `/matches/32699544/replay/2` at "Move 1 of 91"; pressing ArrowLeft there crossed back into `/matches/32699544/replay/1?position=last` and correctly landed at "Move 85 of 85" (the previous game's actual last decision, not its first). Both entry points verified by actually clicking through a real browser session: `/matches`' first row's "Replay" link landed on `/matches/47224119/replay/1`; `/matches/32699544`'s "Game 3" link landed directly on `/matches/32699544/replay/3` at "Move 1 of 81" (the specific game, not always game 1).

Read the actual diff image rather than assuming: the committed baseline (last updated 2026-09-26, 4 days before this session) shows the Next.js dev-mode floating devtools button ("N", bottom-left) inside the clipped `board-panel` screenshot region; a fresh capture today shows the same region with no button at all — a genuinely different root cause (devtools-overlay-visibility timing, not diagonal-arrow anti-aliasing) from anything previously documented in this project. Confirmed unrelated to this session's work: `e2e/` was never touched, and none of the 5 changed/new files sit anywhere near `/matches/[matchId]`'s `BoardPanel`/`MistakesSection` render path. Flagging as a new, real, pre-existing test-infrastructure finding worth a future look (e.g. suppressing the devtools indicator for the dedicated test server), not something this task caused or should fix.
- Changed files: `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`, `app/matches/page.tsx`, `app/matches/matches.styles.ts`, `app/matches/[matchId]/page.tsx`, `app/matches/[matchId]/matchDetail.styles.ts` (modified); `app/matches/[matchId]/replay/[gameIndex]/page.tsx`, `GameReplay.tsx`, `gameReplay.styles.ts` (new). Otherwise unchanged: the branch-protection step, Oracle port-3306 ingress, production auth protection, `/galaxy/matches`' token persistence, and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report all remain open.
- Later same day: filtered `countAsDecision: false` cube-checks out of the replay's stepped sequence — these are Galaxy's background per-roll "not close enough to double" evaluations, not real moments in the game, and were cluttering the walk-through. Kept everything else exactly as before, including every `countAsDecision: true` cube decision regardless of outcome (doubled, passed, took, or a graded "correctly/incorrectly declined to double") and every checker decision, forced or not.
- The one subtlety that made this not a one-line change: `countAsDecision: false` cube-check rows are also the *source* of dice-roll data for checker decisions (each one carries a `dice_rolled` event's roll, per `buildRollLookup`'s own existing comment in `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`) — filtering them out of the Prisma query itself would have silently blanked every checker decision's dice display along with hiding the background checks. Fixed by fetching every row for the game unfiltered (as before, for `buildRollLookup`), then filtering to `countAsDecision: true` only when building the *displayed* sequence — one added `.filter((row) => row.countAsDecision)` before the existing `.map(decisionFromRowForReplay)`, nothing else touched.
- Confirmed, per the explicit ask, that this doesn't reintroduce the bug the replay was built to avoid: the filter is strictly `row.countAsDecision`, nothing touches `rawError`/`errorSeverity` — `decisionFromRowForReplay`'s own null-tolerance (added in the earlier entry this same day) is completely unchanged, so a real, graded-but-ungraded-magnitude decision still renders if it's `countAsDecision: true`. Matches `MistakeStat`/`RepeatedPosition`'s own scoping exactly, per the ask.
- Verified against real data, not assumed: found `match 977248 game 4` by direct DB query (75 total decisions, 32 `countAsDecision: false`, 12 real `countAsDecision: true` cube decisions — deliberately searched for a game exercising both kinds rather than reusing an easy case). After the fix: **"Move 1 of 43"** (75 − 32 = 43, exact arithmetic match) and exactly 43 list rows. A direct DB re-query of the same game (not a fragile browser-side text match — see below) confirmed **zero** mismatches both ways: every kept row genuinely has `countAsDecision: true`, every dropped row genuinely has `countAsDecision: false`, and all 12 real cube decisions survive the filter untouched. A screenshot confirmed dice/roll data is still fully populated for checker decisions (the roll-lookup fix held).
- Caught and corrected my own flawed first verification attempt rather than trusting it: an initial browser-side check (`getByText("did not double roll", { exact: true })`) reported zero matches and I nearly took that as confirmation — but a screenshot then showed rows still labeled "did not double roll" in the list. Traced this to a false negative in the test itself, not the feature: `MoveDelta` renders "did not double" and its best-action label ("roll") as two separate sibling `<span>`s, so no single element's own text is ever the literal exact string being searched for — a real, graded "correctly declined to double" decision (`countAsDecision: true`, same label text as the background check, since `actionLabels()` doesn't know about `countAsDecision` at all) produces identical-looking text to the now-filtered-out background checks. Replaced the flawed browser-text assertion with the direct DB cross-check above once the discrepancy surfaced, rather than papering over it.
