## 2026-10-01
- Investigated (no code) whether a fixed-perspective board view is feasible for the replay, per explicit request, before building anything. Two findings reported and confirmed against real data/code, not assumed:
  1. "My color for this game" is reliably derivable, but not directly off `Decision.color` — checked 40 real games: `Decision.color` is blank for most `CUBE` rows (including at least one genuine `countAsDecision: true` one), while `Decision.userId` is always populated and its color (via any row where color IS populated) never alternates within a game, in any of the 40.
  2. The checker-position half of a flip is a clean, decoder-independent output transform (decodeGnuPositionId itself needs no changes); the point-number labels and move-arrow anchoring are not purely external, since `Board.tsx`'s printed labels are hardcoded to the physical loop index rather than derived from the decoded position at all — confirmed by reading `Board.tsx` directly.
- Built exactly the plan reported and approved:
  - `lib/gnuPositionId.ts`: new `flipPerspective(decoded)` — swaps mine/opponent, index-reverses both 24-arrays, swaps bar/off pairs. Pure, decoder-independent. Added 4 new tests to `lib/gnuPositionId.test.ts` (reversal-relationship check against real match data already in the file, a synthetic bar/off-swap check, an involution check — flipping twice returns the original, proven across all 3 existing fixtures — and a 15-checkers-per-side invariant re-check post-flip). All pass.
  - `lib/backgammonNotation.ts`: new `mirrorSubMoves(subMoves)` — mirrors numeric `from`/`to` point refs (`25 - n`), leaves `"bar"`/`"off"` untouched (those resolve correctly on their own once the underlying bar/off counts are swapped). No dedicated test file added for this one — simple enough, and verified end-to-end via the real rendered output below, matching this project's own pattern of not creating a new test file for every small lib addition.
  - `Board.tsx`: new optional `flipped?: boolean` prop (default false — every existing call site omits it, so their rendering is byte-identical to before). Used for exactly two things: the printed point-number label (`flipped ? 25 - point : point`) and the roll-dice color (`flipped ? "opponent" : "mine"` — the one small design decision flagged during investigation, resolved this way since a flipped board is by definition showing the opponent's own roll).
  - `BoardPanel.tsx`: same optional `flipped?: boolean` prop — applies `flipPerspective`/`mirrorSubMoves` once, in one place, before handing off to `Board`, rather than duplicating that logic at each future caller.
  - Replay `page.tsx`: added a `PlayerIdentity.findFirst({ isMe: true })` lookup; built a `userId -> color` map from the full unfiltered row set (same logic verified in the investigation) and used it to both resolve `myColor` and overwrite every displayed decision's own `color` field (fixing the blank-color issue found in step 1 — required for the toggle to work at all, not optional); passes `myColor: string | null` down.
  - `GameReplay.tsx`: new `fixedPerspective` state (default off) and a checkbox, shown only when `myColor !== null` (hidden rather than offered broken when no resolvable identity exists for this game); computes `flipped = fixedPerspective && selected.color !== myColor` per decision and passes it to `BoardPanel`.
  - `gameReplay.styles.ts`: one new entry for the checkbox/label row.
- Verified against real data (match `977248` game `4`, the same one used for the cube-filter verification, chosen again since it already has a confirmed mix of my/opponent decisions): toggle checkbox appears (myColor resolved); toggled on, my own decisions (1, 4, 7 — checker moves and cube actions alike) render byte-identical to the toggle-off state, confirming `flipped` correctly stays false when `selected.color === myColor`; the opponent's interspersed cube-checks and a real opponent checker move ("8/7\* 7/5 6/5", with a hit marker) both render flipped — point-number labels swapped top/bottom exactly as expected (`25 - point`), my checkers stayed in the same physical position and the same dark color throughout, the move arrows landed exactly where the (now-relabeled) notation numbers point, and the roll dice switched to the lighter "opponent" styling. Screenshots confirmed all of this directly, not just reasoned about from the code.
- Confirmed no regression to the 4 existing `BoardPanel` call sites: ran `npm run test:visual` 5 times after these changes. Every run showed the exact same 2 pre-existing tests (`matches-normal-midgame`, `decision-card`) already root-caused to the Next.js dev-tools-overlay-visibility issue documented in the prior entry — never a new or different test, across 5 consecutive runs. `e2e/` itself untouched, 18 baselines unchanged.
- `npm run lint`/`tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build`/`npm test` (26/26, +4 from the new flipPerspective tests) all clean.
- Changed files: `lib/gnuPositionId.ts`, `lib/gnuPositionId.test.ts`, `lib/backgammonNotation.ts`, `app/components/match-analysis/Board.tsx`, `app/components/match-analysis/BoardPanel.tsx`, `app/matches/[matchId]/replay/[gameIndex]/page.tsx`, `GameReplay.tsx`, `gameReplay.styles.ts`. Committed as `40da6b8 add replay board`.

- Fixed a bug in the fixed-perspective toggle (above) found right after shipping: move arrows rendered incorrectly for opponent decisions when the toggle was on. Root-caused before fixing, per request:
  - `Board.tsx`'s `moveAnchor()` — the function that converts a sub-move's `from`/`to` point refs into pixel anchors for the arrow — hardcoded `decoded.mine`/`decoded.mineBar`/`decoded.mineOff` (and the bottom-half bar/off-tray geometry constants that go with "mine") regardless of whether the board was being rendered flipped. That assumption ("the mover is always on the `mine` side") holds pre-flip (GNU position IDs are always decoded from the on-roll player's own perspective) but is exactly what `flipPerspective` inverts for an opponent decision — post-flip, the mover's checkers live in `decoded.opponent`, not `decoded.mine`. `decoded`/`subMoves` arriving in `Board.tsx` were already correctly transformed upstream (`BoardPanel.tsx`'s `flipPerspective`/`mirrorSubMoves`); only the arrow-anchoring code itself had the stale assumption.
  - Confirmed with real data before fixing anything: decision `2524` (match `977248` game `4`, `8/7* 7/5 6/5`) — point case: arrow read `decoded.mine[16] = 0` instead of the correct `decoded.opponent[16] = 3` for its origin's stack height (column/point itself was unaffected — only the vertical anchor within it). Decision `2540` (`Bar/23 13/11(3)`, same game) — bar case: anchored at the bottom-half ("mine") bar position regardless of flip, while the actual checker is drawn in the top half (`decoded.opponentBar`) once flipped — a wrong board *half*, not just a wrong offset. Decision `672276` (match `33425869` game `1`, `7/3(3) 4/off`) — same root cause for the off-tray case, found via a DB search for a genuine opponent bear-off decision (none existed in game 4 of 977248 itself).
  - Fix: `moveAnchor()` now takes the same `flipped` boolean `Board` already receives (not a new flag), and reads `decoded.opponent`/`decoded.opponentBar`/`decoded.opponentOff` plus the opponent's own bar/off-tray geometry (top-half `baseY`/`dir` for the bar, matching the existing bar-checkers render block; new `OPP_OFF_TOP_Y`/`OPP_OFF_BOTTOM_Y` constants for the off-tray, mirroring the existing `MINE_OFF_*` pair and replacing the inline literals the opponent `OffColumn` call used before) whenever `flipped` is true. Point-case geometry (`stackBase`) itself didn't need changes — both sides already share the same column/row at a given point; only which array supplies the stack count needed to switch.
  - Verified against the exact three decisions above via real browser screenshots (not just re-derived math): all three now anchor at the correct stack height / correct board half when flipped, and the unflipped (default) rendering of the same decisions is visually unchanged.
  - `npm test` (26/26, unaffected — the bug lived in `Board.tsx`'s rendering, not the pure transform functions), `tsc`/`lint`/`build` clean.
  - `npm run test:visual` x5: every failure across all 5 runs was spot-checked by viewing its diff image directly, and every one showed the already-documented Next.js dev-tools-overlay button ("N", bottom-left) — never an actual content/arrow difference. Worth refining the earlier note: the overlay flake isn't confined to the 2 specific tests first documented (`matches-normal-midgame`, `decision-card`) — this run it also hit `bar-checkers` (both `/matches` and `/mistakes` contexts) and `both-arrows`, a different, non-overlapping set each run. It's timing-dependent on which screenshot region the dev-tools button happens to be visible over at capture time, not tied to any specific test — still the same pre-existing, environment-only cause, unrelated to any code change this session.
  - Changed files: `app/components/match-analysis/Board.tsx` only. Not yet committed.

- Fixed the devtools-overlay screenshot flake (above) properly, rather than continuing to treat it as unavoidable environment noise. It was never actually a flake in the sense of random timing — it was a mismatch between stale committed baselines and live rendering, root-caused by directly comparing the "diff" visualization against the real "actual" screenshot (which I hadn't done before — I'd been reading Playwright's red/dimmed diff-highlighting as if it were a literal screenshot of an on-page element, which it isn't): the *baselines themselves* had the real Next.js dev-tools indicator (a dark circle with "N", bottom-left) baked in from whenever each one happened to be captured with the indicator visible; live re-renders without it then "failed" against that stale baseline, for whichever tests' baselines happened to have captured it — which is exactly why it looked like it was hitting a different 1-2 tests each run.
  - Fix: `next.config.ts` now conditionally sets `devIndicators: false` (confirmed as the current v16 API via `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/01-next-config-js/devIndicators.md` — the old `appIsrStatus`/`buildActivity`/`buildActivityPosition` options were removed in v16) behind a new `DISABLE_DEV_INDICATOR` env var, read only when set. `playwright.config.ts`'s `webServer.env` sets `DISABLE_DEV_INDICATOR: "1"` for the dedicated :3100 test server only — the normal :3000 dev server never sets it, so the indicator stays on there deliberately (it's genuinely useful for real local dev).
  - Updated the 5 baselines that had the old indicator baked in (`matches-bar-checkers`, `matches-normal-midgame`, `galaxy-matches-bar-checkers`, `galaxy-matches-normal-midgame`, `mistakes-bar-checkers`) via `npx playwright test --update-snapshots`, per this project's own documented baseline-update workflow (`e2e/README.md`). Verified each one by eye against its old version before trusting it — confirmed only the corner badge is gone, nothing else shifted.
  - Verified: 10 consecutive full-suite runs, 17/17 passing every time, zero retries. Also stress-tested against a cold server (`.next-test` wiped) with `--repeat-each=5` (85 total test runs across all 17 tests) — 85/85 passed, including the exact `bar-checkers` scenario that had 100%-reproducibly shown the stale-baseline mismatch before the baseline update.
  - Changed files: `next.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, and the 5 baseline PNGs above. Not yet committed.
- What's next: the branch-protection step, Oracle port-3306 ingress, production auth protection, `/galaxy/matches`' token persistence, and the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report remain open.

- Component-architecture audit (read-only report, no code), then a 7-batch extraction plan (Batch 0 shared duplicated logic → 1 board geometry → 2 badge typing → 3 /mistakes + /repeated-positions dedupe → 4 MistakesSection split → 5 page pairs → 6 remainder). Executed **Batch 0** only:
  - `lib/playerIdentity.ts`: `PlayerIdentity` type (moved out of `MistakesSection.tsx`) + `resolveMyIdentity(identities, presentUserIds)` — the "isMe identity actually present in this data" rule, now shared by `MistakesSection` and the replay `page.tsx` instead of two hand-synced copies. Returns the identity (not just the id) since `MistakesSection` also needs `displayName`; each caller keeps its own null policy (first-player fallback vs. hidden toggle). Replay page still queries `findFirst({ isMe: true })` — behavior unchanged; note it only considers the *first* isMe row, whereas `MistakesSection` considers all of them. Only matters if more than one isMe identity ever exists; left as-is deliberately, flagged for a decision.
  - `lib/listSelection.ts`: `resolveSelected(list, id, getId)` (select-or-fall-back-to-first) + pure `listSelectionReducer`; `app/hooks/useListSelection.ts` (new folder) is a thin `useReducer` wrapper so the logic is testable under Vitest's `node` env without adding React Testing Library/jsdom. Replaces the three copies of `selectRow` in `MistakesSection`, `DecisionListWithDetail`, `GameReplay`. `GameReplay` uses the hook but not `resolveSelected` — its selection is positional (index, seeded from `?position=last`), with no first-item fallback.
  - Tests: `lib/playerIdentity.test.ts` (7), `lib/listSelection.test.ts` (11). `npm test` 44/44 (was 26).
  - Verified: lint/tsc/build clean; `npm run test:visual` 17/17, zero diffs (covers `MistakesSection`, `DecisionListWithDetail`, `MoveDelta` tab states). Replay route isn't in the visual suite, so checked server-rendered output against the real DB: match 977248 game 4 still renders "Move 1 of 43", `?position=last` → "Move 43 of 43", fixed-perspective toggle present (myColor resolved via the shared function). Replay keyboard/click navigation itself not exercised by any automated check.
  - Not committed.
- Batch 0 committed as `695a313`.
- Aligned the replay page's "who is me" with `MistakesSection`: now `findMany({ isMe: true })` → `resolveMyIdentity` over all of them, not `findFirst`. Behavior-neutral today — the real DB has exactly one isMe identity (`galaxy:62febc99…`), so both queries return the same row; pinned by a new `playerIdentity.test.ts` case comparing against the old first-only rule, and re-checked on match 977248 game 4 (still "Move 1 of 43" / "Move 43 of 43", toggle shown).
- **Batch 1** — board geometry extraction:

  - `Board.tsx` now renders only: `OffColumn` + its tray-panel rect became `OffTray({ side, count })` (was 11 props), `Stack` takes `{ base, count, side }`; label color comes from the palette instead of comparing hex strings. `Dice.tsx` reads face/pip from `CHECKER_PALETTE` (its own die-outline colors stay local).
  - Tests: `lib/boardGeometry.test.ts` (22) — layout invariants (p and 25-p share a column/opposite rows, each side's bar/off-tray stays in its own half, off-slot fill + next-slot agreement for every count), and flipped-perspective regressions modeled on the three decisions the original arrow bug was confirmed on (2524 point, 2540 bar, 672276 off) plus an end-to-end flip + mirror check over real positions. Mutation-checked: reverting moveAnchor to always read the mine side fails exactly the 5 flipped tests.

- **Batch 2** — badge typing:
  - `BadgeConfig` moved from `app/components/ui/Badge.tsx` into `lib/badges.ts` (fixes lib → app import).
  - Typing, two different answers on purpose: **severity** is a closed set (Prisma `ErrorSeverity`), so `SeverityKey = Lowercase<ErrorSeverity>`, `severityBadges satisfies Record<SeverityKey, BadgeConfig>`, and `SeverityBadge` takes `SeverityKey` — a new enum value fails to compile until mapped, and a bogus literal is rejected at the call site (both confirmed with a throwaway compile probe). `severityKey(errorSeverity)` replaces the inline `.toLowerCase() as …` in `/repeated-positions`. **Classification** is an open set (Galaxy data, no enum, every caller has a DB string), so `ClassificationBadge` takes `string` honestly — a literal-keyed prop could only be satisfied by a cast, which is what the old no-op `keyof typeof` was. All 6 casts at call sites removed.
  - Lookups + fallbacks moved into `lib/badges.ts` (`badgeForSeverity`, `badgeForClassification`), both falling back to `{ code: value, label: value }`. Writing the tests surfaced a pre-existing bug: the old `map[type] ?? fallback` resolved inherited names like `"toString"` to a function instead of falling back — now an own-property (`Object.hasOwn`) lookup. Same latent pattern still exists in `getClassificationLabel` (`lib/classificationLabels.ts`) — not touched, flagged.
  - Tests: +6 in `lib/badges.test.ts` (`severityKey` for every enum value, known-key lookups, unknown-key fallback for both, inherited-property case). `npm test` 73/73.

Reachable from the URL, not just theoretical: `getPhaseLabel` falls through to it with the raw `?phase=` value, so `/mistakes?phase=toString` put the function's source text into the result-count line. New `lib/classificationLabels.test.ts` (6) — inherited-name cases fail without the fix. `test:visual` not run for this change alone: no shared component in the suite calls it (only page-level server code; the `/mistakes` filter dropdown that uses it is outside every screenshot) — it ran as part of Batch 3 below anyway.

The new parsers use own-property lookups; verified it now behaves exactly like any other unknown value (`?category=bogus` → no filter, "594,564 … decisions.").

Classes moved verbatim; the now-unused filter/pagination keys removed from `mistakes.styles.ts` / `repeatedPositions.styles.ts`.

The DB side went to a new `lib/decisionQueries.ts` rather than `decisionFromRow.ts` itself, so that file stays Prisma-free and testable: `DECISION_LIST_SELECT` (the select both pages duplicated), `loadDecisionItems(rows)` (sibling-row fetch → roll lookup → items), and `findPositionOccurrences(position)` (the `FORCE INDEX` raw SQL + follow-up `findMany`, comment moved with it).

- Inherited-key sweep (plain-object lookups keyed by external/user input — the class fixed in `getClassificationLabel`, the badge lookups and `severityFromParam`/`categoryFromParam`): grepped every object-map declaration and every dynamic `obj[key]` lookup across `app/`, `lib/`, `scripts/`, `proxy.ts`, plus API route body/param handling. One more instance: `Dice.tsx`'s `PIPS[value] ?? []` — die values come from Galaxy's JSON unvalidated, and a non-numeric inherited name would pass the fallback and crash on `.map`. Fixed via an exported `pipsFor` own-property lookup + `app/components/match-analysis/Dice.test.ts` (3; mutation-checked — reverting to `PIPS[value] ?? []` fails exactly the inherited-key test). Everything else is safe by key provenance (code literals, Prisma enum columns, numeric ids, own-key iteration) — table in the session report. Lint/tsc/build clean, 103/103, visual 17/17.

Fix would be an allow-list of the 8 `MatchIndexData` fields.

- **Fixed the `indexData` mass-assignment** in `POST /api/galaxy/matches/[matchId]/sync`:
  - Allow-list = the 8 `MatchIndexData` fields, confirmed against both legitimate senders (`app/galaxy/matches/page.tsx`'s Sync button and `lib/sync.ts`'s batch path build exactly these): `opponentName`, `opponentCountry`, `opponentRating`, `opponentError`, `opponentScore`, `userError`, `userRating`, `userScore`.
  - `lib/ingest.ts`: `pickMatchIndexData` (field-by-field copy, so a missing field won't compile and nothing extra can ride along) is what `ingestMatch` now writes to the `Match` upsert — the guarantee sits at the DB write, covering any future caller. `parseMatchIndexData` validates the route's untrusted body (object, all 8 present as own properties, strings/finite numbers, extras dropped) → 400 "Missing or malformed indexData." instead of passing junk on.
  - Tests in `lib/ingest.test.ts` (+8): a payload also setting `id`/`source`/`sourceMatchId`/`ingestStatus`/`ingestError`/`matchLength`/`playedAt`/`createdAt` writes only the 8 (mutation-checked — the old spread fails it); the 8 still written with their values; parse cases (extras dropped, missing, wrong type, non-object, inherited props). Typed the harness's `matchUpsert` mock to read its args.
  - Gate: the route is only reachable behind `isGalaxyEnabled()` — `proxy.ts`'s matcher includes `/api/galaxy/:path*`. Verified locally: with `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE` emptied, the sync, list and incremental routes and `/galaxy/matches` all 404; with it on, they're reached. Per `docs/deploy.md`, Vercel sets neither `DATABASE_URL` nor `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE`, so production should be gated closed — not independently verified (no access to Vercel's env).

  - Pre-existing, noticed, not changed: a sync through this route never sets `ingestStatus` to DONE (only `lib/sync.ts` does), so a UI-synced match stays PENDING — the batch sync will re-ingest it, and after a reload `/galaxy/matches` shows "Sync" for it again.

- Added doubling-cube rendering to the board, across all 4 `BoardPanel` call sites (replay, `/matches/[matchId]`, `/galaxy/matches/[matchId]`, `/mistakes`). Investigated and planned before building (plan reviewed and approved), including two domain-correctness questions resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: cube owner = the *taker's* side (standard rule, matches `lib/ingest.ts`'s existing `cubeOwnerUserId`, not the literal "doubler's side" a first read of the ask suggested), and cube state shown for a decision = the state *entering* it, before that decision's own outcome applies (matches both the board's own source-position timing and `cubeOwnerUserId`'s own comment).
  - Real-data investigation found no field anywhere stores the resulting cube value (2/4/8…) — only `analysed_event`/`review.double`/`review.take` signal that a cube action happened at all, and `cubeOwnerUserId`/`cubeDetail` are the only structured DB columns, neither a value. Confirmed via a real double→take→redouble→pass sequence (match 46797221 game 3, gameId 7901, decisions 635034/635035/635052/635053).
  - New `lib/cubeState.ts`: `computeCubeStates(events)` walks one game's events in order and returns a `Map<eventId, CubeState>` (value/owner/`confident`, owner relative to each event's own actor — same "mine" framing `decoded` positions already use); `flipCubeState` mirrors `flipPerspective`/`mirrorSubMoves`. Since neither `Game`/`Match`/`SyncRun` has any per-game ingest-completeness marker (checked `prisma/schema.prisma` in full) and `game_over` events never become Decision rows at all, incomplete-data detection leans on the game's own legality instead: a `cube_double` can only legally be followed by its own `cube_pass` — anything else observed while one is pending (a checker/resignation decision, a second double, or a pass with nothing pending) permanently flips `confident` to false for the rest of the game. `Board.tsx` renders nothing for the cube when `confident` is false, rather than a possibly-wrong number — a wrong value with no indication it might be wrong was treated as worse than showing nothing, per explicit instruction.
  - Threaded through exactly like `buildRollLookup` already was: new `buildCubeStateLookup` in `lib/decisionFromRow.ts` (same `gameId:eventId`-keyed shape), both `decisionFromRow*` functions and `toDecisionListItems` take it as an optional extra param; `lib/mistakes.ts`'s `extractDecisions` (the `/matches`/`/galaxy/matches` path) computes it once per game directly, since those two already hold the full raw event array in memory. `Decision` gained a `cubeState: CubeState | null` field — no new prop-threading chain needed, `BoardPanel.tsx` just reads `selected?.cubeState` the same way it already reads `roll`/`myLabel`/etc., and applies `flipCubeState` alongside its existing `flipPerspective`/`mirrorSubMoves` calls. `/mistakes`'s existing per-game side query (`lib/decisionQueries.ts`, originally built only for the roll lookup) turned out to need no `select` widening at all — `buildCubeStateLookup` needs exactly the same 3 columns (`gameId`/`eventId`/`raw`) that query already selects.
  - `lib/boardGeometry.ts`: new left-edge gutter column (`CUBE_COL_W`) added by shifting the whole existing grid's X origin right, not by renumbering `BAR_COL`/`OFF_COL` or touching `COL_WIDTHS` — every existing geometry function and the 21 pre-existing `boardGeometry.test.ts` tests kept passing unchanged. New `cubeBadgeCenter(owner)`: the board's own dividing line when centered, inset into the owning side's half otherwise. `lib/checkerPalette.ts` gained neutral `CUBE_FILL`/`CUBE_CONTRAST` (not a per-side record — the physical cube doesn't change color when it changes hands, only position).
  - `Board.tsx`'s new `Cube` subcomponent: a rounded-square badge (deliberately not a circle, to read as distinct from checkers/off-tray badges).
  - Verified against the real game-7901 sequence via actual browser screenshots at every relevant replay step (double offered → still centered; take decision → still centered; the next decision → value 2, correctly on the *opposite* side from the doubler; redouble decision → value 2, taker's own side; final pass decision → still value 2, opposite side) — matching a direct DB-level computation of the same sequence beforehand. Cross-checked the fixed-perspective flip toggle via direct SVG DOM inspection (not just eyeballing a screenshot, which gave a false read once at full-page zoom) — confirmed the badge's own `y` coordinate moves between the two halves correctly. Also spot-verified on `/matches/[matchId]` (same redouble decision, same correct position/value) and `/mistakes` (a different real double, centered going into it — correct).
  - `lib/cubeState.test.ts` (11 tests, fixture-built from the real match-7901 field values): the full real sequence, a no-cube-action-all-game case, all 3 structural-violation rules, a routine "did not double" no-op case, and `flipCubeState` invariants. `lib/boardGeometry.test.ts` gained 5 new `cubeBadgeCenter`/gutter-shift tests (27 total, all passing). `npm test` 179/179, `tsc`/lint/build clean.
  - `npm run test:visual --update-snapshots`: 13 baselines changed (every board + `decision-card` screenshot, since the cube now renders everywhere) — each reviewed by eye before trusting (cube renders correctly in its own gutter, nothing else shifted), per `e2e/README.md`'s documented workflow. 10 consecutive re-runs after the update: 17/17 passing every time, zero flakes.
  - Changed files: `lib/cubeState.ts` (new), `lib/cubeState.test.ts` (new), `lib/boardGeometry.ts` (+`.test.ts`), `lib/checkerPalette.ts`, `lib/mistakes.ts` (+`.test.ts`), `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`, `lib/decisionQueries.ts`, `app/matches/[matchId]/replay/[gameIndex]/page.tsx`, `app/components/match-analysis/BoardPanel.tsx`, `app/components/match-analysis/Board.tsx`, 13 baseline PNGs. Not committed.

- `MoveDelta.tsx` (shared by `MistakesSection.tsx`, `DecisionListWithDetail.tsx`/`DecisionCard.tsx`, and `GameReplay.tsx`): when the played move has no error, collapse the usual my-label/best-label pair into a single green label instead of showing both — redundant when identical. Used the project's own existing signal, `Decision.isMistake` (`absError > 0`, from `lib/mistakes.ts`), rather than re-deriving equality from label text.
  - Real-data check before assuming text equality: `myLabel`/`bestLabel` are byte-identical at `isMistake: false` **only for CHECKER decisions**. For CUBE and RESIGNATION they're independently phrased even at zero error — confirmed against real rows, e.g. `myLabel: "did not double"` / `bestLabel: "roll"`, and `myLabel: "resigned"` / `bestLabel: "should not resign"` with `rawError: 0` (resigning cost nothing in that exact position despite not being the nominal best action; also confirmed `rawError`/`errorSeverity` match the resignation-specific `resignError`/`shouldResign` columns exactly, so `isMistake` is still the right trigger, just not a text-equality one). So the collapsed view shows `myLabel` — the one description that's always accurate regardless of kind — not `bestLabel`, styled green via a new `collapsedLabel` style (same green as `bestLabel`, minus its `ml-1.5`, since it's now the only child, not the second of a pair).
  - **Where it's reachable**: `MoveDelta` is rendered from exactly 3 places (`MistakesSection.tsx` — serving both `/matches/[matchId]` and `/galaxy/matches/[matchId]`; `DecisionListWithDetail.tsx` — `/mistakes`; `GameReplay.tsx` — replay), confirmed via a full grep, not assumed. The fix is identical code in all 3, but `MistakesSection.tsx`'s own tables (`lib/mistakes.ts`'s `partitionMistakes`) only ever list `isMistake: true` rows by construction — so the collapse branch is structurally unreachable there, not a gap in the fix. Confirmed `/mistakes`'s own default (unfiltered-severity) view can include zero-error decisions (the `errorSeverity` filter is optional in `app/mistakes/page.tsx`'s query), so it and the replay are where this is actually reachable.
  - Verified against real data in all 3 reachable contexts (replay, `/mistakes`, and a control check that `/matches/[matchId]`'s own mistake rows are unaffected): a plain checker case (`myLabel === bestLabel` literally), the CUBE "did not double"/"roll" case and the RESIGNATION "resigned"/"should not resign" case (both showing the correct `myLabel` text, not `bestLabel`), and a real mistake decision confirmed to still show both labels unchanged. `npm test` 179/179, `tsc`/lint/build clean.
  - `npm run test:visual` initially showed **zero diff** — correct given the suite's existing 4 representative decisions are all deliberately-chosen real mistakes (by the suite's own design), so none ever exercised the collapse path. Added a 5th representative decision, `clean-move` (`isMistake: false`, real data from the same match/games the suite already uses), plus a dedicated `move-delta — collapsed` shared-markup test asserting exactly one child `<span>` before screenshotting. Found mid-way that `clean-move` can't be tested on `/matches`/`/galaxy/matches` at all (times out — `MistakesSection` never shows it, per the point above), so split the board-test loop into `MISTAKE_DECISIONS` (4, those two contexts) vs. the full `DECISIONS` (5, `/mistakes` only) rather than forcing a uniform grid — 13 board tests, not 15. Also hit pre-existing, unrelated schema drift surfaced by re-running the seed script for the first time since `plyNumber` was added: `bg_test` was missing 3 migrations (`add_repeated_position`, `add_ply_number`, `add_decision_ply_number_index`); applied via `prisma migrate deploy` against `.env.test` (local test DB only, not touching `app_dev` or Oracle).
  - 2 new baselines (`mistakes-clean-move`, `move-delta-collapsed`), both reviewed by eye; the pre-existing 17 baselines **unchanged** (0 modified), confirming the fix is fully isolated to the new path. 10 consecutive re-runs: 19/19 passing every time.
  - Changed files: `app/components/match-analysis/MoveDelta.tsx`, `app/components/match-analysis/MoveDelta.styles.ts`, `e2e/board-visual.spec.ts`, `e2e/README.md`, `e2e/seed-test-db.ts` (comment only), `e2e/fixtures/seed-data.json` (+2 real rows), 2 new baseline PNGs. `bg_test`'s schema migration state fixed as a side effect (not file-tracked). Not committed.

- Fixed the chained-notation arrow bug investigated earlier today: `parseNotation` (`lib/backgammonNotation.ts`) silently dropped any token with 2+ slashes (e.g. `13/10*/6`, one checker's two sequential sub-moves written as a single slash-chain) — not merged into one long arrow as first guessed, just gone, since the old regex was anchored end-to-end around exactly one `from/to` pair. Confirmed via real decision 655340 (match 32807941, game 2, `9/5*/2`): the board showed **zero** arrows for that move before the fix.
  - Replaced the single-hop `TOKEN_RE` with a generic `CHAIN_RE` (`point(/point*?)+(\(count\))?`) + a `HOP_RE` that walks the chain's own capture group one hop at a time — handles arbitrary chain length rather than hardcoding 2 hops, since real data has 2-, 3-, and (rarely) 4-hop chains. Each hop becomes its own `ParsedSubMove` with its own independent `hit` flag (`bar/21*/18*` hits at both landing points). A trailing `(count)` is applied to every resulting hop via the existing `count` field (not duplicated into separate entries) — e.g. `5/4*/3(2)` → 2 `ParsedSubMove`s, each `count: 2` — matching how a plain repeated single move already represents repetition, so `Board.tsx`'s existing one-"×N"-label-per-subMove rendering generalizes with zero changes needed there or in `mirrorSubMoves`, confirmed by not touching either file.
  - **Correction to this morning's own investigation**: re-checked the "2 four-segment chains" claim before citing it in tests — it was actually one real decision (id 1106206, `bar/20*/15*/10*/5`) counted twice because it appears identically in both `notationPlayed` and `notationBest`, not two distinct examples. The `24/16*/12*/8*` example cited then is a 3-segment (4-point) chain, not 4-segment — corrected in the test suite below rather than carrying the error forward.
  - `lib/backgammonNotation.test.ts` (new, 20 tests): simple 1-hop moves unchanged (regression safety), the real 2-hop `9/5*/2` case, a real 3-hop case (`24/16*/12*/8*`), the real 4-hop bar-starting case (`bar/20*/15*/10*/5` — doubles as the "arbitrary length" and "bar geometry" cases in one), the trailing-count case (`5/4*/3(2)`), bar-chains with and without hits, a chained token mixed with a normal token in the same notation (both orders), and `mirrorSubMoves` applied to a parsed chain (confirming the flip still works hop-by-hop with no changes to that function). `npm test` 197/197, `tsc`/lint/build clean.
  - Re-screenshotted the previously-broken decision (655340) on the real replay page: two correctly anchored arrow segments now render (9→5 with a hit-circle at 5, then 5→2), where zero arrows rendered before.
  - Checked all 5 `board-visual.spec.ts` representative decisions' `notationPlayed`/`notationBest` directly against `bg_test`'s seed data — none use chained notation, so `npm run test:visual` was expected to show zero diff, and did: 19/19 passing, 5 consecutive re-runs, no baseline changes.
  - Changed files: `lib/backgammonNotation.ts`, `lib/backgammonNotation.test.ts` (new). Not committed.

- Implementing the raw-field audit's recommendations (`reports/2026-10-01-decision-raw-field-audit.md`), one step at a time with verification between each. **Step 1 done**: `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`'s `decisionFromRow`/`decisionFromRowForReplay` now read the already-existing `notationPlayed`/`notationBest` columns directly instead of re-parsing `raw` via `moveNotations()` on every call — zero schema change, pure read-path fix. `DecisionRow`'s type and both `select` sites (`lib/decisionQueries.ts`'s `DECISION_LIST_SELECT`, the replay page's own inline select) updated to fetch the two columns.
  - Verified byte-identical equivalence against 2,700 real rows (2000 CHECKER, 500 CUBE, 200 RESIGNATION) before switching — 0 mismatches.
  - New unit tests in `lib/decisionFromRow.test.ts` prove the column is actually read (not silently still re-parsed): a row where the column is deliberately set to a different value than raw-parsing would produce confirms the column wins, plus real-data cases for all 3 kinds (CHECKER with real notation, RESIGNATION and CUBE confirming the columns are correctly null, the CUBE one via a real null-`rawError` fixture through `decisionFromRowForReplay`).
  - `npm test` 202/202, `tsc`/lint/build clean. `npm run test:visual`: touches `/mistakes` and replay's data source, so ran it — 19/19 passing, zero diff, confirming the switch changed nothing observable.
  - Environment note: `.env` was left pointed at Oracle from earlier troubleshooting; switched to the local dev DB (`mysql://app:app@localhost:3306/app_dev`) via inline env overrides for this work, `.env` itself untouched. Also hit a *new* Oracle symptom worth flagging separately — `certificate signature failure` (via a connection-pool timeout), different from the earlier `Got an error reading communication packets` — not investigated further since it wasn't blocking this step, but worth knowing before any future Oracle-side work.
  - Changed files: `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`, `lib/decisionFromRow.test.ts`, `lib/decisionQueries.ts`, `app/matches/[matchId]/replay/[gameIndex]/page.tsx`. Not committed.

## 2026-10-02

- Raw-field audit implementation, **Step 2**: resolved the severity-computation inconsistency (cross-cutting finding #1). `extractDecisions` (live-fetch: `/matches`, `/galaxy/matches`) used to compute severity itself from a local `absError >= 0.08` threshold, ignoring Galaxy's own `error_analysis.error_severity` classification entirely; `decisionFromRow`/`decisionFromRowForReplay` (DB-row: replay, `/mistakes`) read the real `errorSeverity` column instead. Confirmed `error_analysis.error_severity` is present in raw JSON at the exact same path as `raw_error` (no technical reason the live-fetch path couldn't read it) — chose to make both paths read Galaxy's own classification, via one new shared `severityFromErrorSeverity` (in `lib/mistakes.ts`) that both now call (`extractDecisions` directly; `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`'s `severityFor` as a thin adapter from the Prisma enum's uppercase values). Not a case of "document a deliberate reason for independent computation" — there wasn't one; it was an unintentional drift the audit happened to catch.
  - Found a real, concrete disagreement to anchor the fix on: decision id 7315, `absError: 0.0799` — just under the old 0.08 cutoff (old logic: "error") — while Galaxy's own `error_severity` is `"blunder"`. Saved its real raw JSON as a new fixture, `lib/__fixtures__/galaxy-payloads/blunder-below-0.08-threshold.json` (no PII — only an opaque `user_id`, consistent with this project's existing fixture convention).
  - `extractDecisions` had zero prior test coverage — added `lib/mistakes.test.ts` tests for `severityFromErrorSeverity` directly and for `extractDecisions`'s severity sourcing, including a real case (`moneyGameMove`'s own event) that `isMistake` and `severity` aren't the same signal: `absError` nonzero (`isMistake: true`) but `error_severity: "none"` (`severity: null`) — confirming severity truly comes from Galaxy's classification, never re-derived.
  - Cross-path verification, both function-level and live: queried for a real decision belonging to "me" (MistakesSection has no opponent toggle — confirmed the hard way, an opponent-owned mismatch decision is structurally invisible there) where the old/new severity actually differ (id 43063, match 3436925 game 5, `absError 0.0799`). Directly confirmed all 3 relevant functions (`decisionFromRow`, `decisionFromRowForReplay`, `extractDecisions`) now return `"blunder"` for the same real decision. Then screenshotted it live on `/matches/[matchId]`, `/mistakes` (had to compute the exact page number directly from the DB — `/mistakes` pagination is `eventId desc` with a max page size of 50, and this decision was 2,399 rows in; that itself is an unrelated, pre-existing UX friction point worth knowing about, not something this step touched), and replay — all three show the move in red/blunder styling consistently. `/galaxy/matches` wasn't separately screenshotted — it shares `MistakesSection`/`extractDecisions` with `/matches` exactly (only the data-fetch differs), already verified function-level, so re-screenshotting would be redundant given the shared code path.
  - `npm test` 205/205, `tsc`/lint/build clean. `npm run test:visual`: 19/19, zero diff — the 4 existing representative decisions' old/new severity happen to already agree, so no baseline shift was expected or seen.
  - Environment note: the long-running `next dev` server (port 3000) had the *old* Oracle-pointed `.env` baked in from before the user's fix — `.env` changes aren't picked up by an already-running dev server. Restarted it mid-step once this surfaced (as empty query results, not an error) to pick up the corrected local DB.
  - Changed files: `lib/mistakes.ts`, `lib/mistakes.test.ts`, `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`, new fixture `lib/__fixtures__/galaxy-payloads/blunder-below-0.08-threshold.json`. Not committed.
- Raw-field audit implementation, **Step 3**: `sourcePositionId` added as a real `Decision` column (`VARCHAR(191)` nullable, `COLLATE utf8mb4_bin` — same precedent as `RepeatedPosition.sourcePositionId`), replacing the two unindexed `JSON_EXTRACT` call sites. Proposed as a design-review report first (`reports/2026-10-02-step3-sourcepositionid-column-design.md`), approved, then implemented.
  - Migration `20261001210108_add_decision_source_position_id`: hand-added `COLLATE utf8mb4_bin` (Prisma's DSL can't express it) plus new composite index `@@index([sourcePositionId, errorSeverity])` — same column order as `RepeatedPosition`'s own index (same logical key), chosen because `sourcePositionId` equality is by far the most selective predicate available (609,305 distinct values across 1,257,534 rows; worst-known `(sourcePositionId, errorSeverity)` pair is 10,923 rows). Generating the migration itself needed MySQL's shadow-database privilege, which neither `bg_db_rw` nor the local `app` user has locally — used local `root` (empty password, per `docker-compose.yml`) via an inline env override just for `prisma migrate dev --create-only`, `.env` untouched; applied via `migrate deploy` under the normal `bg_db_rw` credential, which does have the `ALTER`/`INDEX` grants that needs.
  - `findPositionOccurrences` (`lib/decisionQueries.ts`) rewritten as a single plain Prisma query — no more raw SQL, no `FORCE INDEX` hint, no intermediate id-list round trip. Confirmed via real `EXPLAIN` (not estimated) that the optimizer picks the new index unprompted. Real wall-clock timing against the actual worst-case literal (10,923-row position, `sourcePositionId: "4HPwATDgc/ABMA"`, `errorSeverity: NONE`): old unforced ~28.4s, old `FORCE INDEX`-forced ~21.2s, new column+index ~0.3s — roughly 70-90× faster than either old path, not just the forced one.
  - `recompute-repeated-positions.ts`'s dropped-`GROUP BY` workaround **kept deliberately** — its `WHERE` filters on `kind`/`countAsDecision`/`rawError`, none of which share a prefix with the new `(sourcePositionId, errorSeverity)` index, so SQL-level grouping still can't be satisfied by an index scan; only the per-row projection got cheaper (plain column read vs. `JSON_EXTRACT`/`JSON_UNQUOTE`), not the grouping mechanics. Converted its raw SQL to a plain `prisma.decision.findMany` regardless, since the column makes that straightforward.
  - `lib/decisionFromRow.ts` (`DecisionRow`, both `decisionFromRow*` functions) and `lib/ingest.ts` (new rows going forward) now read/write the real column instead of `review.source_position?.formatted_value` — same "stop re-parsing raw for something that's now a column" pattern as Step 1's notation fix. `extractDecisions` (`lib/mistakes.ts`, the live-fetch path) is unchanged on purpose — it has no DB row to read a column from, same reasoning as Step 1.
  - Backfill: `scripts/backfill-source-position-id.ts`. First version followed the `backfill-ply-number.ts` shape (pull rows into JS, group by target value, batched `updateMany`) — wrong shape for this field specifically, since `sourcePositionId` has 609,305 distinct output values (vs. `plyNumber`'s 5): a single-shot full-table pull reliably crashed Node with a heap-out-of-memory error, and a batched/paginated version was still far from done after several minutes of ~609k individual `updateMany` round trips. Replaced with a single server-side `UPDATE ... SET sourcePositionId = JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(...)) WHERE sourcePositionId IS NULL` — MySQL computes and writes each row in one pass, no per-value round trip or JSON materialized in Node at all. `--dry-run` (count-only) and idempotency (`WHERE sourcePositionId IS NULL`) both kept from the established pattern despite the implementation swap; the killed first attempt's partial progress (~180k rows) was picked up cleanly by the idempotent `WHERE` clause. Real run: remaining 1,076,899 null rows backfilled locally in ~68 seconds, 0 unresolvable, 0 null afterward — 100% real-data coverage confirmed matches the design report's prediction exactly.

- Raw-field audit implementation, **Step 4**: dice roll (`Decision.roll`) added as a real column, computed once at ingest via the same `findPrecedingRoll` backward scan the old read-time `buildRollLookup` (removed) used to re-run on every page load. Proposed as a design-review report first (`reports/2026-10-02-step4-dice-roll-column-design.md`, per the user's explicit framing that the propose-then-implement pattern from Step 3 was already validated — implemented directly after, no separate pause), then implemented.
  - **Cardinality confirmed low** (36 distinct non-empty rolls, like `plyNumber`, not like `sourcePositionId`'s 609k) — but extraction needs *sibling-row* context (the nearest preceding `dice_rolled`/`game_started` event in the same game), not a single row's own JSON, so it can't be a single server-side `UPDATE` like Step 3's. Backfill is per-game-chunked (bounded memory) with a global low-cardinality output bucket (~37 keys), mirroring `backfill-ply-number.ts`'s bucket-then-batch-write shape.
  - **Corrected my own mistaken belief, not just the ask's framing**: initially thought `decisionFromRow`'s `rollLookup.get(key) ?? event.rolled_dice ?? []` meant a live "fall back to this row's own roll" behavior needed replicating. Confirmed directly (`[] ?? x` evaluates to `[]` in JS, not `x`) that this fallback is dead code today — `rollLookup` always has a defined entry for every row, so the real current value is just `findPrecedingRoll`'s raw output. Rewrote the proposal report before implementing rather than silently course-correcting.
  - **Measured the true null rate** (2.52%, 31,639/1,257,534 rows — not 2.52%→1.29% as a "fix," the 2.52% *is* the faithfully-replicated correct value): three real categories, all confirmed directly — 15,635 genuine first-moves-of-a-game; 15,441 a `dice_rolled` event's own cube-check row (its own roll exists but the backward scan looks past it by design — a real, pre-existing display quirk, deliberately *not* fixed here since the ask was to relocate computation, not change semantics); 563 `RESIGNATION`/`CUBE` decisions with no roll associated at all (inspected 6 real examples directly). Also flagged, not fixed: `extractDecisions` (live path) gates roll to `kind === "checker"` while the DB-row path doesn't — a separate pre-existing divergence between the two paths, same category as Step 2's severity bug, out of this step's scope.
  - `lib/ingest.ts` gained `rollByEventId` (mirrors `checkerPlyByEventId`'s shape, operates on the full in-memory events array including `game_started`/`game_over` — never stored as their own row, but confirmed via `lib/__fixtures__/galaxy-payloads/game-started-game-over.json`'s own real data that `game_started` *can* carry a real roll, which ingest-time correctly captures and the backfill script structurally can't, since it only sees what's already stored — a known, documented, accepted limitation). Prisma's Json input type needed `Prisma.DbNull` (not plain `null`) to mean "SQL NULL" rather than a stored JSON null literal.
  - `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`: `buildRollLookup` removed entirely; `decisionFromRow`/`decisionFromRowForReplay`/`toDecisionListItems` all dropped their `rollLookup` parameter (now just read `row.roll` directly, `?? []`). `buildCubeStateLookup` (Step 5 territory) untouched.
  - Backfill: `scripts/backfill-decision-roll.ts` — per-game batches of 300, idempotent via a value comparison (old vs. computed) rather than a `WHERE roll IS NULL` pre-filter, since a legitimate final value can itself be `null` (unlike `sourcePositionId`'s "null = not yet backfilled"). Dry-run matched the full-scale investigation exactly (1,225,895 would change, 31,639 already correct). Real run: ~7m40s (36 `updateMany` calls, each touching ~33-35k rows via a large `id IN (...)` — I/O-bound, not CPU-bound), verified afterward with a direct count (31,639 null remaining, exact match).

Applied both pending migrations to `bg_test`, computed correct `sourcePositionId`/`roll` values for all 10 seed decisions directly from their own raw JSON/sibling context, wrote them into the JSON fixture, re-seeded. Fixed the now-stale `buildRollLookup` comments in `e2e/seed-test-db.ts` and `e2e/README.md` (the latter also had a pre-existing, unrelated staleness — "8 Decisions / 4 representative" — corrected to the real current 10/5 while already touching that paragraph).

- Raw-field audit implementation, **Step 5**: cube value (`Decision.cubeValue`) and confidence (`Decision.cubeConfident`) added as real columns, computed once at ingest via `lib/cubeState.ts`'s `computeCubeStates` (reused directly, not reimplemented); `cubeOwnerUserId` (already existed) widened from `CUBE`-kind-only to every kind. Report-then-implement again (`reports/2026-10-02-step5-cube-value-confident-design.md`), but paused mid-investigation for an explicit scope decision from the user before finishing the design — see below.
  - **Investigation found something much bigger than "relocate a computation"**: with a properly representative sample (first attempt was clustered on a narrow early `gameId` range — caught and fixed before trusting it), `confident: false` on ~21% of all real decisions today, not a rare edge case. Root cause confirmed directly: 52.7% of games with a real double offer have no stored `double_accepted`/`double_rejected` resolution row at all — those event types are already in `lib/ingest.ts`'s own `EVENT_TYPES_SAFE_FOR_NULL_ERROR_ANALYSIS` list (Galaxy sometimes sends them with a null `error_analysis`, silently dropped), and the old read-time `computeCubeStates` (via `buildCubeStateLookup`) only ever saw stored rows, so it could never see the resolution happened. Ingest-time computation sees `review.take`/`review.double` before that filtering decision, so it would fix roughly half of these retroactively — but only for new ingests, not already-stored data (backfill has no live Galaxy access, by design).
  - Surfaced this to the user directly (via `AskUserQuestion`, not a unilateral call) rather than either quietly building the bigger fix or quietly ignoring it: three options (full fix incl. re-ingesting historical data live; ingest-time only with DB-rows-only backfill; value-only, leave confident untouched). **Chosen: ingest-time only, DB-rows-only backfill** — new data gets the improved computation automatically; historical data keeps today's ~21% rate until/unless naturally re-ingested.
  - Design: `cubeValue Int?`/`cubeConfident Boolean?` (nullable for migration-sequencing only, same as `plyNumber`/`sourcePositionId`/`roll`). `lib/cubeState.ts`'s public `CubeState` shape and its ~10 exhaustive `lib/cubeState.test.ts` assertions were deliberately left untouched — rather than widening `CubeState` to also expose an absolute owner (which `cubeOwnerUserId` needs but `computeCubeStates` only returns relative), `lib/ingest.ts` keeps its own separate absolute-owner walk, unchanged logic, just un-gated from `kind === CUBE`. Verified directly (not assumed) that the two independent walks never disagree: 0 mismatches across all 1,257,534 real rows, converting `computeCubeStates`' relative owner back to absolute via each event's own `user_id`.
  - Backfill: `scripts/backfill-decision-cube-state.ts`, same per-game-chunked shape as `roll`'s script (needs full-game context) — measured real cardinality 1,066 distinct `(cubeOwnerUserId, cubeValue, cubeConfident)` tuples (between `roll`'s 36 and `sourcePositionId`'s 609k), global batching same as `roll`. Dry-run then real run, ~3m42s, verified afterward: 0 nulls remaining, `cubeConfident: false` rate 21.89% (matches the representative-sample measurement), 121,366 non-`CUBE` rows now carry a real `cubeOwnerUserId` (confirms the widened population rule took effect).
  - `lib/decisionFromRow.ts`: new `cubeStateFor`/`relativeCubeOwner` helpers convert the row's absolute `cubeOwnerUserId` to the relative "mine"/"opponent"/"center" framing `Decision.cubeState`/`BoardPanel` already use. `buildCubeStateLookup` removed entirely (mirrors Step 4's `buildRollLookup` removal). `lib/decisionQueries.ts`'s `loadDecisionItems` no longer needs its own extra per-game DB query at all — roll and cube state were the only two things it existed for, and both are plain columns now.
  - Caught the same `bg_test`-migration-not-applied-yet gap as Step 4 before it could cause a silent `test:visual` failure: applied the new migration, computed and backfilled `cubeOwnerUserId`/`cubeValue`/`cubeConfident` into `e2e/fixtures/seed-data.json` directly (all centered/undoubled for these specific 10 seed rows — no cube action among them), re-seeded. Fixed the now-doubly-stale Step-4-era comments in `e2e/seed-test-db.ts`/`e2e/README.md` that still described cube state as needing the sibling-row fetch.

Screenshotted the replay page live: cube renders correctly (value 2, positioned on one player's side, non-centered), no crash.

## Raw-field audit implementation: summary (Steps 1-6)

All 6 steps of `reports/2026-10-01-decision-raw-field-audit.md`'s recommendations are now implemented locally (not yet applied to Oracle). Tying back to the audit's own recommendation table:

- **`sourcePositionId`** ("both agree — top candidate") — ✅ done, Step 3.
- **Dice roll** ("both lean yes") — ✅ done, Step 4.
- **Cumulative cube value** (flagged disagreement, resolved as "cheap... `cubeOwnerUserId`'s walk already does ~90% of this work") — ✅ done, Step 5, plus a materially bigger finding along the way (the ~21% `confident: false` rate and its root cause), surfaced to and resolved by the user before implementing.
- **`myLabel`/`bestLabel`/`detail`** ("both say low priority — recommend leaving as read-time-derived text") — confirmed untouched: still computed at read time in both `lib/mistakes.ts`'s `actionLabels`/`buildDetail` (live path) and reused identically in `lib/decisionFromRow.ts` (DB-row path, via the same `actionLabels` import). No column added, as recommended.
- **`review.double`/`review.take` (raw booleans)** — the one item the original instruction's "four" may have had in mind that genuinely wasn't built as its own column: the audit called this "low overall priority — mostly superseded in importance by the cube-value item," not a clear top-priority recommendation like the first three. Step 5's `cubeValue`/`cubeConfident` capture the *cumulative* state entering a decision, not whether *this specific* decision was itself a double/take action — that per-decision boolean fact is still only available via the lossy `cubeDetail` display string or by re-parsing `raw`, exactly as before this work started. Flagged here rather than silently claimed done, since it wasn't.
- Cross-cutting findings #2 and #5 — ✅ documented explicitly, Step 6.
- Cross-cutting findings #1, #3, #4 — already resolved/addressed in Steps 1-2 (notation columns now read instead of re-parsed; severity computation unified) or were observations rather than action items.

What's next: Oracle rollout for Steps 3-5 (pending user go-ahead; Step 6 is docs-only, nothing to roll out), a decision on whether `review.double`/`review.take` warrants its own follow-up given the above, the branch-protection step, Oracle port-3306 ingress, production auth protection, `/galaxy/matches`' token persistence, the unresolved false-blunder/missing-move report, and the now-much-better-understood (but deliberately not fully fixed) cube-state incomplete-data rate all remain open.

- **Fixed a Step 4 mischaracterization, caught by the user independently checking Galaxy's live site rather than trusting the report's "unmeasurable" conclusion.** Step 4's design report had called a game's first-move `roll: null` an unfixable gap — `game_started` can carry the real opening roll, but it's never stored as its own `Decision` row, so a DB-rows-only backfill structurally can't see it. Asked for 10 real, spread-out examples (not all from one opponent/time period) to check by hand; confirmed independently via Galaxy's own site for 2 of them — match 46514290: `[4,1]`, match 5722740: `[3,6]` — both verified by hand-matching the pip math against the move actually played, falsifying "unmeasurable."
  - Investigating how to use `game_started`'s roll turned up a better fix than the one implied by the original framing: every affected row already has what's needed, already stored. `Decision.raw.moves` — a flat `[from1, to1, from2, to2, ...]` array Galaxy sends on every `move_commited` event (one `[from, to]` pair per die used), never previously read anywhere in this codebase — directly decodes to the roll via simple pip-distance math, no live Galaxy call needed at all.
  - Verified before implementing, not assumed: all 15,550 affected rows (every game's own first move, not just each match's game 1 — confirmed across 4,268 of 4,350 matches, ~98%) have a clean, fully-decodable `moves` array. Decoding decision id 1207111's `moves` (`[24,18,18,13]`) gives `[6,5]`, exactly matching what the user found on Galaxy's site for that same decision (`[5,6]`). Cross-checked this decode method against 5,000 already-known rolls elsewhere in the table and found ~6% mismatch, entirely concentrated in bear-off/bar-entry moves (where pip-distance ≠ die-face) — confirmed this doesn't matter for the actual target population, since a game's first move structurally can't be either.
  - Used `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` for this one (unlike Steps 3-5's "propose in a report, implement same pass" — the scope kept growing mid-investigation: first "maybe worth a live re-fetch," then "actually fully fixable with zero live calls," enough of a moving target to warrant a real plan+approval cycle). Asked two `AskUserQuestion`s mid-plan (how to represent the ~1-in-9 genuinely-partial case before the `event.moves` discovery made that moot; whether to also wire the decode into `lib/ingest.ts` as a defense-in-depth fallback — declined, since the existing `game_started`-aware logic is already believed-correct for fresh ingests, verified via direct code trace: `rollByEventId` runs on the full live event array before `NON_DECISION_EVENT_TYPES` filtering).
  - New `lib/mistakes.ts` function `decodeRollFromMoves`, documented with its real reliability scope (first-of-game only, not general-purpose) and the real example that proved it. New `lib/mistakes.test.ts` tests (5) covering the real example, a two-different-checkers case, a double, and the two null-return cases (single-die turn, malformed input).
  - `scripts/backfill-first-move-roll.ts`: single flat query (no per-game batching needed — unlike `roll`'s own backfill, this needs no cross-row context, each row's own `raw.moves` is self-sufficient, and the target population is small enough that there's no OOM risk the way pulling the whole table's `raw` at once would be). Dry-run then real run: 15,550/15,550 resolved, 0 unresolved, 28 distinct roll values. Spot-checked all 3 real examples with known expected values (the two the user verified on Galaxy's site, plus the `[6,5]` decode-vs-Galaxy cross-check) — all matched (caught and fixed a mix-up in my own first verification pass, where I'd paired the wrong id with the wrong expected value — not a backfill bug, a verification-script bug).
  - Checked `e2e/fixtures/seed-data.json` for the same gap (per the established "catch `bg_test` gaps proactively" discipline from Steps 4-5) — none of its 10 seed rows are affected (their first-of-game row is always the `CUBE`-kind `dice_rolled`-own-row, not a `CHECKER` row, so this specific fix's scope doesn't touch them). No re-seed needed.
  - Corrected `reports/2026-10-02-step4-dice-roll-column-design.md` (struck through the "unmeasurable... accepted limitation" paragraph, replaced with what actually happened) and `docs/field-mapping.md`'s `roll` row.
  - `tsc`/lint clean, `npm test` passing (new tests included).
  - Local only — matches every other step in this session; not applied to Oracle.
  - Changed files: `lib/mistakes.ts` (+`.test.ts`), `scripts/backfill-first-move-roll.ts` (new), `reports/2026-10-02-step4-dice-roll-column-design.md`, `docs/field-mapping.md`. Committed (`2a2c55f`).

- **Raw-field inventory re-verification** (`reports/2026-10-02-raw-field-reverification.md`), prompted by the first-move-roll fix revealing this was the third real case this session of a field's assumed meaning/location being wrong (after `final.gnubgid`-vs-`source_position.formatted_value` and `cube_analysis.cube_level`-vs-actual-cube-value) — treated as a systematic risk, not three isolated incidents. Re-verified the 2026-10-01 raw-field audit's full inventory against fresh data, not against anything already concluded.
  - Found `GALAXY_TOKEN` in `.env` was still live — tested before relying on it. Live-re-fetched 5 real games directly from Galaxy (399 events, all 4 `analysed_event` shapes), chosen for diversity: a known cube sequence, a known `game_started`-with-roll case, a resignation, a previously-assumed money game, and an `mwc`-populated game.
  - All priority-1 (correctness-sensitive: feeds `MistakeStat`/`RepeatedPosition`/cube rendering/PR) fields confirmed correct, several newly stress-tested against real data rather than just re-read: `is_blunder`-vs-`error_severity` consistency (0 mismatches/371), `move_played`/`rank` uniqueness (0 zero, 0 multi/172 real moves), source-vs-destination `classification` genuinely differing in real data (not hypothetical), the already-fixed cube-value/dice-roll findings re-confirmed from fresh independent games.
  - **Found one new, real, already-live discrepancy**: `metadata.scores`/`match_length`/`crawford_state` are **not** reliably null-together for money games as the existing docs claimed — found a real match (34206386) where `CHECKER`-kind events report them null while `CUBE`-kind events in the *same games* report real values. `lib/ingest.ts`'s `matchLength` variable is set unconditionally on every event (whichever is processed last wins), so this has already produced wrong data: confirmed match 34206386's stored `Match.matchLength` is `null` despite clearly being a real length-5 match, not money. Measured scope: **107 of 4,350 matches** misclassified this way. Assessed blast radius: display/categorization only (`matchLength`/scores/`crawfordState` don't feed grading logic) — real and already-live, but not the highest-stakes tier. **Not fixed in this pass** (verification only, per the ask) — flagged for a separate follow-up.
  - Also recorded (not bugs, just newly-confirmed): `mwc_context` has 3 real values (`cubeless`/`optimal`/`null`), not 2 as previously implied, though the code's binary-null-check logic is unaffected; several raw fields confirmed present-but-never-read (`review.second`/`.level`, `source_match`, `resigned_points`, per-candidate `equity_error`).
  - Not an implementation pass — report-only, no code changes.

- **Implemented items 1 & 2 from the re-verification's recommended actions**: dropped `isBlunder` and `cubeDetail`/`detail`; added `cubeActionPlayed`/`cubeActionBest`; renamed `notationPlayed`/`notationBest` → `movePlayed`/`moveBest`. Walked through the re-verification's findings interactively with the user field-by-field first (several follow-up discoveries came out of that conversation, not just the original report — see above), then planned and implemented this pair of items via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` given the real schema-rename risk involved.
  - **The one real risk in this change**: Prisma's default behavior for a field rename (no `@map`) is a DROP+ADD, which would have silently discarded all 590,933 real `notationPlayed`/`notationBest` values on the next `migrate dev`. Generated the migration via `prisma migrate diff` to see Prisma's actual proposed SQL, confirmed it was a DROP+ADD as expected, and hand-wrote the migration using `CHANGE COLUMN` (MySQL's rename-in-place syntax) instead — verified immediately after applying that all 590,933 rows still had their real data intact, not nulled out.
  - `lib/mistakes.ts`'s `actionLabels()` reused directly at ingest time for the new `cubeActionPlayed`/`cubeActionBest` columns (same "reuse the already-tested function" pattern as Steps 4-5's `findPrecedingRoll`/`computeCubeStates` reuse) — not reimplemented. `lib/decisionFromRow.ts` gained a `labelsFor` helper: `CHECKER`/`CUBE` kind read `myLabel`/`bestLabel` straight from the new columns, `RESIGNATION` kind still calls `actionLabels()` against raw (explicit scope decision, not every kind getting the same treatment).
  - Backfill for `cubeActionPlayed`/`cubeActionBest`: unlike `roll`/`cubeState`'s backfills, this needed no cross-row game context (same category as `sourcePositionId`'s single-row case), so a single SQL `UPDATE` with a `CASE` on the already-stored `analysedEvent` column handled all 664,579 `CUBE`-kind rows in one pass (~1m38s) — no batching, no OOM risk. Verified against the real double→take→redouble→pass sequence already used throughout this session (decisions 635034/635035/635052/635053): exact match to the literal values independently confirmed earlier via `actionLabels()` directly against live data.
  - `prisma/seed.ts` (fake local-dev data) and `e2e/fixtures/seed-data.json` (real `bg_test` snapshot) both updated — the latter's 5 `CUBE`-kind rows' `cubeActionPlayed`/`cubeActionBest` computed via the same `actionLabels()` the app itself uses, not hand-typed. `bg_test` migrated and re-seeded.
  - `docs/field-mapping.md` updated (required by its own sync note) — `isBlunder` row struck through with the drop rationale, `movePlayed`/`moveBest`/`cubeActionPlayed`/`cubeActionBest` rows added/updated, the general-fields summary paragraph corrected.
  - Full verification: `tsc`/lint clean, `npm test` 228/228 (3 new `decisionFromRow.test.ts` tests for the `labelsFor` kind-branching, 1 new `ingest.test.ts` test for the backfill-matching computation), `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:visual` 19/19 with **zero diffs** — confirms the new columns reproduce exactly what the old recomputed `myLabel`/`bestLabel` rendered.
  - Local only — not applied to Oracle. Item 3 (the `matchScoreBlack`/`matchScoreWhite`/`crawfordState` → `Game`-level, `userId`-keyed redesign, which also fixes the live `matchLength` bug) remains open, deliberately not bundled into this pass per the report's own suggested sequencing.

- **Implemented item 3**: moved `matchScoreBlack`/`matchScoreWhite`/`crawfordState` from `Decision` to `Game` (as `userScore`/`opponentScore`/`crawfordState`, actor-relative-resolved) and fixed the live `Match.matchLength` bug the re-verification found (the old "whichever event is processed last wins" assignment). Planned via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` (3rd plan-mode session this session).
  - **The real fix**: resolving `userScore`/`opponentScore`/`crawfordState`/`matchLength` all from **the same one event per game** — the first event (by `eventId`) whose `metadata.scores` is non-null, not literally the first event. Checked directly against the known-affected match (34206386) that naively using "the game's first event by `eventId`" alone (the existing `playedAt`/`plyNumber` pattern) does *not* fix this — its first-by-`eventId` decision is itself one of the unreliable null-scores events. `lib/ingest.ts`'s new `gameScoreByEventId` extends the exact same `decisionTimestampsByEventId` → `reduce`-to-min-`eventId` shape `Game.playedAt` already uses, just carrying `userId`/`scores`/`crawfordState`/`matchLength` instead of a timestamp. `Match.matchLength` mirrors `Match.playedAt`'s own "first game that resolves one wins" rule.
  - `metadata.scores.black`/`.white` are actor-relative (confirmed this session, not literal board colors) — resolved against `PlayerIdentity.isMe` so `userScore`/`opponentScore` are unambiguous regardless of which color "you" played as.
  - Schema: added `Game.userScore`/`opponentScore Int?`/`crawfordState String?`; dropped `Decision.matchScoreBlack`/`matchScoreWhite`/`crawfordState` (confirmed write-only, same as items 1/2's dropped columns) — a plain drop in one migration, no rename-data-loss risk since this isn't a rename, it's a replacement at a different granularity (one resolved value per game, backfilled separately, not migrated column-to-column).
  - Backfill (`scripts/backfill-game-score-crawford.ts`): deliberately reads `Decision.raw` JSON directly (same source `lib/ingest.ts` itself parses) rather than the old, about-to-be-dropped `Decision` columns — keeps the script correct regardless of migration ordering, matching the precedent of every other raw-parsing backfill (`backfill-played-at.ts`, `backfill-decision-roll.ts`) rather than the single-row-`UPDATE` precedent (`sourcePositionId`/`cubeActionPlayed`) that would have required the old columns to still exist. Ran against local `app_dev`: 4,350 matches / 15,640 games processed; **117 matches'** `matchLength` actually changed (close to, not identical to, the report's earlier 107-match estimate — this is the real, precise count) and **7,553 games'** score/crawford resolved (the rest — 2,383 matches / 8,087 games — correctly left null: genuine money games or index-only matches with no detail-ingested decisions).
  - End-to-end spot-check against the running example (match 34206386): `matchLength` resolved to `5` (as predicted throughout this session) with a full, sensible 7-game score/crawford progression (`0-0` → ... → crawford at `4-2` → post-crawford at `4-3`).

Not committed.

- **Fixed the Oracle TLS cert failure** (`CERT_SIGNATURE_FAILURE` on every real Oracle connection attempt). Root cause: HeatWave rotated its MySQL endpoint's self-signed CA — the pinned cert at `certs/oracle-mysql-ca.pem` was issued 2026-09-22; the live endpoint had switched to a new self-signed CA issued 2026-09-29 (one week later, different key/fingerprint entirely), and `lib/prisma.ts` deliberately pins chain-of-trust validation to that one specific CA (not disabled verification), so the old pin now fails outright rather than silently degrading.
  - Diagnosed without weakening TLS or touching production data: reproduced the failure directly against the real Oracle host using the app's exact `ssl`/`checkServerIdentity` config; fetched the live cert chain via `openssl s_client -connect <host>:3306 -starttls mysql -showcerts` (macOS's built-in LibreSSL `openssl` doesn't support `-starttls mysql` at all — had to use the Homebrew OpenSSL 3.6.2 build instead); confirmed the live leaf cert verifies cleanly against the live CA with `openssl verify -CAfile`, entirely offline, before touching the DB again.
  - Fix: replaced `certs/oracle-mysql-ca.pem` with the new live CA cert, updated the SHA256 fingerprint comment in `lib/prisma.ts`, and added a note there for next time this happens (HeatWave apparently rotates this periodically) — re-fetch via the same `openssl s_client -starttls mysql` command, take the self-signed cert from the chain (not the leaf), verify it offline first, never disable verification as a workaround.
  - Verified end-to-end through the real app code path: `buildConnectionConfig` against the live Oracle host with the real read-only credentials, `SELECT 1` succeeded.
  - Changed files: `certs/oracle-mysql-ca.pem`, `lib/prisma.ts` (comment only). Not committed.

- **Added warning/detection for the next Oracle CA rotation** — the user explicitly didn't want to find out about the next one only when something breaks. Planned via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` (4th plan-mode session this session) covering 4 things agreed on in conversation: a scheduled pre-emptive check, a clear `/status` message, a fail-fast sync path, and a generic "any DB failure gets checked for this" net.
  - **Empirically determined the actual error shape first**, not guessed: a TLS chain-of-trust failure through `PrismaClient` + `@prisma/adapter-mariadb` surfaces as a generic `PrismaClientKnownRequestError` (`code: "P2039"`, message "pool timeout") — Prisma's own `.code` is useless here. The real OpenSSL verification string is buried 4 levels deep at `error.meta.driverAdapterError.cause.cause`. Reproduced this directly (a real connection attempt against a deliberately-wrong, but validly self-signed, CA) rather than assuming Prisma would surface it cleanly.
  - New `lib/oracleCertCheck.ts`: `isCertVerificationError(error)` recursively collects text from an error's `.message`/`.cause`/own-enumerable-properties (depth-limited, `WeakSet`-guarded against cycles, never throws) and substring-matches a small fixed list of known OpenSSL chain-of-trust failure strings — a classifier, not a `.code` check, since Prisma's own code doesn't carry this signal. Caught and fixed a real bug while writing its first test: `Error.cause` is a non-enumerable own property (confirmed directly — `Object.keys`/`Object.values` both skip it), so it needs its own explicit check alongside `Object.values(error)`, which only covers *other* own-enumerable properties (like Prisma's `.meta`).
  - `lib/prisma.ts`: wrapped both shared clients (`prisma`/`prismaReadOnly`) with a Prisma Client Extension (`$extends({ query: { $allOperations } })`) that logs the clear hint and rethrows the original error unchanged on any cert-classified failure — applies to every call through either client (ingest, sync, every page/route), not just the two bespoke integration points below. Verified at runtime (not just type-checked) against a real failing query through the real extended client.
  - `app/status/page.tsx`: `getDbStatus` builds its own ad-hoc client (not the shared ones), so it needed its own check — prepends the clear hint to the displayed error instead of showing only Prisma's cryptic "pool timeout" text. Verified the happy path is unaffected (live `/status` still shows `Connection: OK` with real counts) and the error path separately (same classifier, direct test).
  - `lib/sync.ts`: wrapped the very first DB call (`prisma.syncRun.create`) — without this, a rotation would make `runSync` hang through *every* pending match's own ~10s pool-timeout one at a time (each looking like an unrelated per-match failure) before finishing. Now fails immediately with a clear message, same uncaught-fatal-error shape `runSync` already had for other top-level failures (so `scripts/runSyncCli.ts`/`/api/sync/incremental`'s existing catch blocks need no changes). Verified directly: the real `prisma.syncRun.create()` call against a wrong CA now fails fast (~one pool-timeout, not N) with the clear message.
  - New `scripts/check-oracle-cert.ts` + `.github/workflows/check-oracle-cert.yml` (daily cron + `workflow_dispatch`): a **credential-free** pre-emptive check — attempts a real TLS-verified connection to the Oracle host (via the already-used `mariadb` package + `lib/prisma.ts`'s own `buildConnectionConfig`, reused not reimplemented) with the real pinned CA but a made-up username/password. Confirmed empirically this session that TLS handshake/verification completes or fails entirely before MySQL authentication is attempted, so no real credentials are needed in CI at all: a cert-classified failure → exit 1 (rotation happened); the expected `ER_ACCESS_DENIED_ERROR` (confirmed its exact shape against the real endpoint) → exit 0 (TLS fine); anything else → exit 1 with a distinct message. A failing scheduled GitHub Actions run emails repo owners by default — no new notification infra.
  - Host handling: confirmed via `git log --all -p` that the real Oracle IP has never been committed anywhere in this repo's history (`.env` gitignored, `.env.example` only a placeholder) — rather than hardcode it into the new committed workflow/script (a new exposure), added `ORACLE_DB_HOST` as a separate env var (`.env` locally, a GitHub Actions repository secret in CI) that the script reads instead. **You need to add the `ORACLE_DB_HOST` secret yourself** (repo Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions) before the scheduled workflow will actually run — not something doable from here.
  - Verified end-to-end: `npm test` 236/236 (7 new `oracleCertCheck.test.ts` tests, including the real captured nested-error shape and a circular-reference safety check), `tsc`/lint/build clean, ran `scripts/check-oracle-cert.ts` locally against the real (now-fixed) endpoint — exit 0 as expected.
  - Changed files: `lib/oracleCertCheck.ts` (+`.test.ts`, new), `lib/prisma.ts`, `app/status/page.tsx`, `lib/sync.ts`, `scripts/check-oracle-cert.ts` (new), `.github/workflows/check-oracle-cert.yml` (new), `.env`/`.env.example` (`ORACLE_DB_HOST`). Not committed.
