- **Consolidated the three decision/move-list implementations** (`MistakesSection`'s `MistakeTable`, `DecisionListWithDetail`'s list, `GameReplay`'s list) into one shared `app/components/match-analysis/DecisionList.tsx` (+ `.styles.ts`) — a continuation of 2026-10-01's "refactor batch 0/4" work (`lib/listSelection.ts`, `useListSelection`, `MoveDelta.tsx`), one level further into the list markup itself. Investigated and proposed the API first (via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`), confirming real per-caller differences before designing around them — not three permanently-separate components, but not a silent behavior unification either.
  - Confirmed differences, not assumed: selection key type differs (id for two callers, array index for `GameReplay`, which has no "fall back to first" case); only `MistakesSection` has checkboxes/select-all/select-none; column sets differ (checkbox/index/roll/error all independently present/absent per caller); the Roll column's empty-state differs (a muted "—" for `MistakesSection` vs. nothing at all for `GameReplay`, matching `DiceRoll`'s own null-for-empty behavior); and — the one non-cosmetic finding — the Roll column's *data* differs by source, not just by display: `extractDecisions` (live-fetch, feeds `MistakesSection`) always reports `roll: []` for non-checker decisions, while `decisionFromRowForReplay` (DB-row, feeds `GameReplay`) reads the real `roll` column, populated for any kind — so a cube/resignation row in replay can show real dice that the equivalent row in `MistakesSection` never would. The shared component doesn't try to unify this — it only ever renders whatever `roll` each row already carries.
  - `DecisionList` is deliberately **not** responsible for selection/tick state — each caller keeps its own `useListSelection`/`useTickSet` (their key types are incompatible) and passes plain callbacks down; the shared component just takes `isSelected(row, index)`/`onSelectRow(row, index, tab?)` and a handful of column-toggle props (`showIndexColumn`, `showRollColumn`/`rollEmptyPlaceholder`/`rollDiceSize`, `showErrorColumn`, `isChecked`/`onToggleCheck`/`onSelectAll`/`onSelectNone`, `renderDetailCell` — the last one is `DecisionListWithDetail`'s only override, to prepend severity/classification badges before `MoveDelta`; the other two callers use the default plain-`MoveDelta` cell). `DecisionListWithDetail` builds a `classificationByDecisionId` map via `useMemo` rather than threading its own `DecisionListItem` wrapper type into the shared component at all.
  - The row/table/head-row/selected-row Tailwind classes were already byte-identical across all three callers' own `.styles.ts` files before this (confirmed by direct comparison) — lifted verbatim into `DecisionList.styles.ts`, not redesigned. Each caller's own `.styles.ts` lost the now-dead table/row entries (kept only what's still genuinely caller-specific: `MistakesSection`'s Roll-column "—" placeholder style, `DecisionListWithDetail`'s badge-group wrapper, both route folders' own outer `[380px]`/overflow-y-auto sizing wrapper, which stays caller-owned by design).
  - Verification found and fixed a **test-script bug, not a code regression**, before trusting any comparison: the first throwaway Playwright baseline script showed real-looking but non-reproducible differences (checker-mistake row count 45 → 35 → 10 across runs of the *same* unmodified code) — root-caused to the script counting rows before `/matches/[matchId]`'s sequential per-game fetches and player-identity resolution had settled (a real race in the test harness, confirmed by running the identical code twice in a row and getting different numbers both times). Fixed by waiting for the Game dropdown to show all 6 real games before counting anything, confirmed stable across 2 consecutive runs before reusing it for the real comparison.
  - Real comparison (`git stash` to get a clean before/after pair of the same running dev server, same DB, same script): 22 recorded steps across all three contexts — `MistakesSection`'s PR recalculation on tick/untick, select-all/select-none, cross-table selection (clicking a cube row correctly deselects the checker table), move-tab switching; `DecisionListWithDetail`'s row selection/card update; `GameReplay`'s Next button, direct row click, arrow-key navigation, move-tab switching — byte-identical across 3 independent runs each of before and after.

Restarted it cleanly against local once this was diagnosed.

The new `scripts/galaxy-get.ts` is a GET-only Galaxy API fetch through `lib/galaxy-client.ts`, used for "verified against an external source" checks.

## 2026-10-07

- **GNU Match ID follow-ups (local).** Spec approved by the user, following the investigator's check of ec5490a.
  - **Even match lengths are money.** Galaxy matches are always odd. Five old single-game "matches" decode to 8 (`72588`, `72587`, `72577`, `19719`) or 16 (`249289`), with final scores no such match can end on. New `effectiveMatchLength` in `lib/gnuMatchId.ts` is the one place the rule lives: an even decoded length counts as 0. Ingest, `gameScoreFromMatchId`, `crawfordStateFor` and the scores backfill all use it; `decodeGnuMatchId` still returns the literal bits. No display path reads the length. Tests added in `lib/gnuMatchId.test.ts` and `lib/ingest.test.ts`.
  - **No "Doesn't match Galaxy" badge on exact ties** (`lib/cubeAction.ts`): ND == DP (roll and double both match), DT == ND below DP (both match), DT == DP (take and pass both match, `cube_pass` too). The derived action is unchanged. That's 324 counted local rows: 307 + 8 with ND == DP and Galaxy "double", 8 with DT == ND, and 1 with DT == DP. Tests use 662455, 1228774 and 1020878, and C2 (629849) stays a mismatch.
  - **Doc fix:** the Crawford check is 7,471 of 7,471 games of length 2+ (the old "7,531" also counted the 60 money games, which agree).
  - **Backfills (local):** the scores backfill changed exactly the five even-length matches (length to 0) and their five games (Crawford to null, and 3 scores to null; the other two were already null). The re-run dry run shows 0 pending. The cube backfill's dry run had 27 `cubeConfident = 1` changes. A read-only SQL check (decoding the Match ID's first byte) confirmed that the confident rows disagreeing with the Match ID are exactly ids 1172684–1172710 (match 46000168 g4). It was then run with `--accept-confident-changes=27`, writing 275,363 rows. The re-run dry run shows 0, and all 1,268,047 Decision rows are now `cubeConfident = 1`.

List order changed in 4 of 3,344 per-player mistake lists (2 matches): equal-error ties, now in eventId order.

  - **Dice:** `lib/gnuMatchId.ts`'s `diceRollFor` returns the higher die first (6-3, as Galaxy's client). This is the one place every dice display passes through (DB and live paths). Display only. Tests in `gnuMatchId.test.ts` and `galaxyFields.test.ts`.
  - **Schema:** migration `20261007200000_add_review_cards_tags` adds `ReviewCard` (FSRS state, `suspended`, `(suspended, due)` index, RESTRICT to Decision), `ReviewLog` (CASCADE from its card), `Tag` (unique case-insensitive name) and `DecisionTag` (RESTRICT to Decision, CASCADE from Tag). It went through `migrate diff` → hand-check → `migrate deploy` on `app_dev` and `bg_test`; `migrate status` is clean on both. There's no `utf8mb4_bin`; the header says why. `prisma/seed.ts` deletes the new tables first.
  - **Logic** (`lib/review/`, pure, 68 tests):
    - Eligibility, options and grading follow the spec's tables, including ties, threshold edges, C2 629849 → Double/Take, and receivers.
    - FSRS is `ts-fsrs` 5.4.2 with default parameters, behind `fsrs.ts`.
    - The queue (`queue.ts`) works by the server's local day: 20 new and 200 reviews a day, learning cards not limited, an Again card requeued after 3 cards.
    - Bulk add (`bulk.ts`), tags, filters and request validation are here too.
    - Settings live in `lib/settings.ts`.
    - New dispatchers: `decisionMatchContext` and `hasAnalysisTranslator`.
  - **Writes:** these routes use the read-write client and are added to the `proxy.ts` matcher, so they 404 without write mode:
    - `/api/review/cards` (POST), `/api/review/cards/[id]` (PATCH suspend, DELETE) and `/api/review/cards/[id]/answer`, which grades on the server and writes the log and the schedule in one transaction;
    - `/api/review/bulk` (dry run, then a real run in one transaction);
    - `/api/review/queue` and `/api/review/summary`, which only read, with the read-only client;
    - `/api/tags`, `/api/tags/attach` and `/api/tags/detach`.
  - **UI:**
    - **Below the note on every DB-backed board** (`DecisionReviewTools`): "Add to review" or "In review · due …", plus the tag editor (`TagEditor`). This covers /mistakes, /repeated-positions, the replay, and /matches/[matchId] through `/api/decision-notes`, which now also returns tags and, with write mode on, card and eligibility. Never /galaxy.
    - **/mistakes:** "Add all to review" with a confirmation.
    - **/review:** the session. `BoardPanel` gets a new `quiz` mode, and the back of the card has the note, tags, links and rating.
    - **/review/cards:** the card list.
    - **Home:** a Review link with the due count, write mode only.
    - The replay accepts `?decision=<id>`.

After: 0 / 0 / 0 / 0. The two other notes (1258195, 1258038) were left alone. `bg_test` had no review, tag or note rows.

The card still shows the single best answer.
  - **Next:** the user's manual check of Phase B, then the Oracle migration (prepared, not run), then push.
- **Oracle: review tables migrated (2026-10-08).** The user applied `20261007200000_add_review_cards_tags` to Oracle with `bg_db_rw`. Checked read-only:
  - the migration is finished, with 1 step and no rollback;
  - `ReviewCard`, `ReviewLog`, `Tag` and `DecisionTag` exist and are empty, with the expected unique keys, the `(suspended, due)`, `(cardId, reviewedAt)` and `reviewedAt` indexes, and the RESTRICT/CASCADE foreign keys;
  - Match and Game counts are unchanged.

  The code push comes after this, by design. Oracle is now the home for notes, cards and tags. The double-answer guard (reject an answer when the card's `reps` has already moved on) is deferred.
- **App shell: navbar, mode badge, shared page layout, breadcrumbs, sync, `?` help, dashboard (code only; server checks pending).** Spec approved by the user (the app-shell plan). No DB change, and no change to grading, translators, derivations, sync/ingest, notes or review scheduling.
  - **Navbar** (`app/components/ui/AppNav.tsx` server + `NavLinks.tsx` client, in `app/layout.tsx` inside `GameStatsAuthProvider`): Matches · Analysis · Mistakes · Repeated · Review (Cards as a hover/focus dropdown; an indented sub-item in the narrow-screen menu) · Galaxy · Status on the right. Galaxy, the due badge and the sync line appear only with `isGalaxyEnabled()`, so the read-only site never queries the review tables or `SyncRun` from the shell. Active route = most specific prefix (`lib/navItems.ts`); a Menu button below `md`. `connection()` keeps the navbar request-time, so every route is now dynamic (build output: all `ƒ`).
  - **Mode badge** (`lib/runtimeMode.ts`): "Oracle · write" / "Local · write" (host localhost or 127.0.0.1) / "Read-only" (write mode off, so Vercel with no `DATABASE_URL`). An unparseable URL in write mode reads "Oracle". Only the label leaves the server; the built client bundles contain none of `DATABASE_URL`, `mysql://`, `bg_db_`, `ENABLE_WRITE_MODE`.
  - **Sync** (`SyncControl.tsx`): "Last synced … · N matches" from the latest finished `SyncRun`. The token is the existing in-memory `GameStatsAuthProvider` one, which wraps the whole app: with a token, "Sync" POSTs to the unchanged `/api/sync/incremental` and refreshes the server components; without one (e.g. after a reload) it links to `/galaxy/matches` ("Add token to sync"). Nothing new is stored.
  - **`PageShell`** (`app/components/ui/PageShell.tsx`): one container, with the three widths the pages already used (narrow 2xl for /status, medium 4xl for the lists, wide 7xl for the boards), and a title/subtitle/actions slot. Every page moved onto it; each page's own `pageContainer`/`main`/`title`/`subtitle` styles were removed. /matches/analysis's gap went from 8 to 6.
  - **Breadcrumbs:** the match page shows "Matches › {opponent} ({id})": the opponent is the non-isMe `PlayerIdentity` among the loaded games' players, from `/api/player-identities`, and it reads "Match {id}" until that resolves. The replay shows "Matches › {opponent} › Game N" (`opponentName` added to the replay's existing match `select`). Also "Review › Cards" and "Galaxy › Match {id}".

First written as one four-table join; the coordinator's read-only EXPLAIN ANALYZE on Oracle showed MySQL driving it from PlayerIdentity through `Decision_userId_idx` (633,728 rows read, 7.9 s). Now small steps driven from the recent matches: isMe ids and recent match ids, then their game ids, then one `groupBy` on Decision by those game ids (the `(gameId, eventId)` unique key). Same filters and per-source userId matching, so the counts are unchanged. No new index;
